# Model checking for Sysprose — the eight candidates, the rider, and the order they ship in

## Summary

You get the behaviour lane's missing half. Today `reach` answers Stanley & Laski's condition (1) — which states are unreachable — and nothing in the tool answers condition (2): once the system enters degraded, failsafe or maintenance, can it get back out? Today the only expressible spelling of "the design shall be *able* to enter `failsafe`" is `absence (state failsafe)`, which exits **1** with `verification/refuted` on the design that satisfies the intent and exits **0** on the design that violates it — both polarities inverted, both measured on `examples/uav-isr.sysml`. This plan closes both, and it does so on top of one ten-line engine change: `exploreMachine` already computes the successor relation and throws it away one line later.

It ships in nine lanes. **Lane A is the spine and is not optional**: one publishability predicate replacing the two duplicated conjunctions that exist today, **one exactness gate replacing the per-mechanism conditions on the whole increasing side**, two registers that make an unguarded absence claim or an ungated witness a red test rather than a review comment, the retained successor relation, one Tarjan pass, and two guards — a vocabulary ratchet and a widened claims guard — that land *before* the features they protect. **Lane B is a second spine** — lanes C, G and I depend on it and lane H depends on lane E; given A and B, lanes C, D, E, F and G may be re-ordered or dropped whole. The draft called B…I mutually independent, which its own dependency lines contradict (§4).

Nine of the eleven features can emit an **absence** claim — *not covered*, *no trap*, *not recoverable*, *guaranteed*, *holds on every maximal run*, *no fair cycle*, *no simulation relation*, *no cut set*, *current within this proof's scope*. That is the single way this lane starts lying, and it is one line away: recording the retained edge at the **admission** point in `exploreMachine` — where a newly discovered configuration is enqueued — instead of at the **revisit prune**, where the edge into an already-seen configuration is the one being discarded, yields the BFS spanning tree, acyclic by construction on every machine in this repository, and Tarjan over it names `{failsafe}` a bottom SCC on the flagship example — a shipped model reported as having an inescapable failsafe that does not exist. So the ordering lens is **risk retired**, not value delivered, and every absence claim in §3 carries the exact conjunction that licenses it, registered in one table.

**And the conjunction is not the same for all of them, which is the correction the adversarial review forced.** An absence claim's soundness under this engine depends on its **polarity in the edge relation**: the two claims that ship today are monotone-decreasing, which is exactly why the walk's deliberate over-approximation of time is sound for them, and most of the claims this plan adds are monotone-increasing, for which the same mechanism is a licence to lie. §2.3 states the rule and makes it a reflection test rather than a paragraph. Three contract sentences move with it (§2.1) and three of this plan's own draft claims were withdrawn or re-worded rather than shipped.

**A second review then broke the first fix in four places, and those corrections are in here too.** The publishability predicate is **two** conjunctions, not one — a single boolean would have turned the two shipped absence claims into refusals on any timed machine and emptied a true finding out of `reach`. There is a **second register**, for positive witnesses: a refutation, a named avoiding cycle and a published witness trace are increasing claims over the retained relation, and the draft gated none of them. And the hazard's own reachability moved: **`accept after(n)` is a parse error** (§0 row 22, measured), so a timed transition is API-only, every dwell fixture is factory-built, and the dwell census is re-priced as an SDK-population number rather than a claim about a user's corpus.

**Four reviews found four ways the walk's edge relation differs from the machine's, a fifth arrived while this section was being written, and the answer is structural rather than another patch — and the tree has since shipped the structure.** Round 1 found time (`after(n)` labels enter the alphabet and no clock advances). Round 3 found the **cooperative environment** — every trigger in the alphabet is offered at every configuration, so `locked → nominal` exists in the walk only because the walk hands the machine `unlatch` (measured, §2.3) — and the **seeded store**, which under-approximates in the other direction, so a guard over an attribute with no declared literal manufactured a trap and a `not recoverable` at exit 1 on a correct design. Round 5 found the fourth and it was of a different kind: an edge **metaclass** simply absent from the step relation, so a `succession` between two states parsed clean, landed in the model and was never walked. That one is fixed at `a66ba1f` — and the way it was fixed is what this plan now stands on. `edgeCensus` (`explore.ts`) counts every edge-bearing element under a machine **without asking the walk** and puts each in a bucket that says what became of it; an edge in the `unaccounted` bucket fails a test **and refuses the machine**. So the question is inverted: **this plan stops enumerating the ways the relation can be wrong and requires instead that it be right** — one predicate, `walkIsExact`, on the whole increasing side (§2.3), whose element-set half is already carried by the shipped census and whose remaining three clauses are about what the walk *offers* at a configuration rather than which edges exist. Every increasing absence claim reads it; every **existential** witness reads its relation half, because a bound can hide such a witness and can never invent one; the two witnesses that are really **maximality** claims — a cycle with no exit, a run with no successor — read the whole of it, and the register's `kind` column is what makes that a test rather than a paragraph. The measured survey of every state machine in the tree (§5(d), re-taken at `dc6662c`) says the gate is **free on today's corpus and only partly falsifiable by it**: nine of eleven machines are exact, the two that are not are already suppressed today, no model contains a dwell or a named trigger — so clauses (b) and (c) are exercised only by fixtures this plan invents — while clause (d) and the element-set half now have real corpus witnesses, in the fixture models `c929278` and `a66ba1f` left behind.

Five of the eleven are **held behind a census the tool itself installs**, because their kill-measurement already reads *no* on this tree: 0 of 3 shipped machines are acyclic; 0 of every `.sysml` file in the tree relate two state machines by `refine` or `:>`; 0 of them carry a requirement that inherits a clause; exactly 1 part declares boundary ports over internal structure and it is degenerate; and the connector graph contains no dependency template at all, so conditions 1 and 2 as specified would fire on **100 % of ports on every model in the tree**. Each of those five is specified here in full, so that the day its census reads non-zero the build is a transcription and not a fresh design.

Nothing here creates a new exit-code contract. Four **sentences** inside three existing contracts move, and the behaviour lane's 0 row moves three times — `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`' 0 and 2 rows (commits 6 and 16) and its 0 row again in commit 18, `FAULT_TREE_EXIT_CODES`' 2 row, `REFINE_EXIT_CODES`' 2 row — and §2.1 writes each one out, because a verdict routed to an exit code whose published sentence means something else is the laundering §2.2 exists to refuse. The claim vocabulary opens exactly once, from four words to six, and is then closed for the rest of the plan.

**Measured against `main @ dc6662c`, working tree clean.** Five commits landed under this plan while it was being written (§0.1) and every figure and every citation below is re-taken against that tree. Baseline: the counts this plan would otherwise quote — subcommands, validation rules, catalogue codes, property patterns, exit-code contracts, examples, test files, `.sysml` files in the tree — are **named by their guard rather than by their number** wherever a sentence can carry the guard instead. `test/unit/docs-counts.test.ts` is that guard for every prose figure (`COMMANDS.length`, `RULES.length`, `DIAGNOSTIC_CODES.length`, the example count, the two file counts); `test/unit/validation.rules.test.ts` pins `RULES.length`; `docs/CONFORMANCE.md`'s scorecard carries the suite figures and is itself checked. Where a number is unavoidable in prose it is stated once, at the point of use, with the guard that would redden if it moved. The two absolute figures this plan does still assert about the tree — that `COMMANDS.length` does not move and `RULES.length` does not move — are stated as *does not move*, which is what an implementer needs and what a test can hold.

---

## 0.0 The citation contract: line numbers are not normative anywhere in this document

Four revisions of this plan were spent re-pointing line numbers at a tree that kept moving underneath them, and roughly sixty percent of the last review's findings were exactly that. A 1,600-line plan pinned line-by-line to a repository under active change cannot converge. So the rule for this document, and it holds in every section:

- **An instruction names a SYMBOL and an ANCHOR.** *"In `reachOne`, after the unsupported refusal and before the product search"* — never *"insert at :1177"*. The symbol is what an implementer greps for; the anchor is what tells them which of its occurrences.
- **A line number, where one is kept, is a dated HINT.** The form is `` `explore.ts` (`reachOne`, ~:1199 as of `dc6662c`) `` — and every bare `` `file`:NNN `` still standing in this document is to be read that way, as a hint measured at `dc6662c` and not as an instruction. A hint that misses is stale, not wrong; the symbol beside it is the instruction and it is what the implementer follows.
- **An instruction that cannot be written without a line number is a sign the change is not yet understood.** Describe the edit by what it must achieve instead, and let the per-commit brief find the position.
- **The per-commit briefs carry the precision.** They are written fresh against the tree at implementation time, and that is the only place an exact anchor belongs. §4's *Files:* lists name files, symbols and the sentence that moves; they do not name positions.
- **A sentence being deleted or re-recorded is quoted, not located.** The standing rule in §4 already says this for deletions — grep for the SENTENCE across `test/` and `docs/`, never for the function that composed it — and it is now the rule for every re-record as well. A hand-pasted transcript is found by its text; it has never been findable by its line.

Two consequences worth stating before they surprise anybody. Nothing in this plan is blocked on a line number being right: every *Files:* entry resolves through a symbol name, a `it(...)` title, or a quoted string. And a reviewer who finds a stale number has found a stale hint, which is a one-word fix in a document, not a defect in a plan.

---

## 0. Corrections to the two source documents, measured before anything is built

Twenty-three statements in `docs/05-model-checking-literature.md`, the scope note, or `docs/04` have moved or are wrong. Thirteen of them would have produced a wrong line of code or a false finding. Rows 15–21 were added after the first adversarial review of this plan and rows 22–23 after the second; rows 15, 17, 19 and **22** are the four that gate the build. Everything below uses the measured form; **do not re-quote the originals into a commit message or a verdict line.**

| # | Stated | Measured | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | "memory is bounded by `maxConfigs × (1 + \|alphabet\|)` edges — four edges on the shipped example" (scope note) | **Wrong, in the unsafe direction.** `FlightModes` (`examples/uav-isr.sysml`:111-121) declares 4 states and **5** trigger-less transitions over 4 configurations, so the relation is 4 nodes / **5** edges against a stated bound of 4. Exceeded on two of three shipped machines, because a nondeterministic choice point yields several successors on one input — which *is* the model's own headline `verification/nondeterministic-choice` row | State the bound as **one edge per innermost-level `stepConfig` call**, which is exact. Never re-quote the product form |
| 2 | `1 \| cover \| P` (scope-note dependency table) | **Overstated.** `search` (`patterns.ts`:857) already does the breadth-first product walk, already carries `ProductNode.parent`, already reconstructs the shortest path (`traceOf`, :1009) and already computes the exhaustiveness conjunction (:1178). The successor relation is never read | `cover` ships off the spine, not off P |
| 3 | `traceOf` at `patterns.ts`:1008 | :1008 is the JSDoc; the declaration is **:1009** | cosmetic; every citation here uses :1009 |
| 4 | `PATTERNS` at :132-172 | The table runs **:132-175**; :172 is mid-entry inside `response` | cosmetic |
| 5 | `checkProperty` lives in `src/semantics/mc/check.ts` (`docs/04` §3.8) | **That file does not exist.** `checkProperty` is `patterns.ts`:1085-1290 | pre-existing plan-vs-shipped drift; commit 0 fixes the sentence |
| 6 | `exploreMachine` at :405 | The declaration is **:418**; the walk body runs to :558 | cosmetic |
| 7 | `fault-tree`'s refusal points at `check-behaviour … --from-keywords` (`fault-tree.ts`:337-351) | **The pointer is dead.** Measured: `check-behaviour`'s flags are `element · pattern · max-configs · strict-vacuity`. `--from-keywords` is declared only on `obligations`. A user who follows the shipped sentence gets `unknown option` | Commit 5 fixes the sentence and adds the assertion that kills the class |
| 8 | the fairness set can default to "the transitions already named in `verification/nondeterministic-choice` rows" | **The rows exist; the names do not.** Both transitions at `autonomous` are anonymous and render as the identical placeholder `UAVSurveillanceSystem::FlightModes::«TransitionUsage»`, and `fires T` resolves by `declaredName` only | A fairness set addresses the **choice point's state** and, where present, a **trigger** — never a transition name |
| 9 | "ship the acyclic half of liveness first" | **0 of 3** shipped machines are acyclic; each is a single non-trivial SCC. The only acyclic machines in every `.sysml` file in the tree are two `off -> on` fixtures, whose terminal configuration is already a `verification/deadlock` row | 5a is specified in full and **held** behind commit 3's census |
| 10 | candidate 6 "the shipped examples contain none" | **Zero** `state def X :> Y` anywhere in every `.sysml` file in the tree; exactly **one** `refine` in the whole tree, requirement→requirement | 6 is held behind a census that already reads 0 |
| 11 | "`connectorEndsOf`, `bindingEquivalenceClasses`, `itemFlowsOf` and `propagateValues` already compute the value-flow relation the dependency template needs" | Three of the four contribute **nothing** on the flagship example: 0 non-library binding classes, 0 user item flows, 0 values propagated past the literal seeds, 0 relations joining two ports of one owner, 0 directed features inside any `port def`, 0 of 14 parts owning a State or Action | The Dependency Template is **absent from the model**, not uncomputed. Only the census ships |
| 12 | candidate 7's kill measurement: "if no `in` port fails condition 2, the check reduces to the dangling list" | The opposite is true and worse: **every** `in` port fails it, because no relation exists that could make one pass. 16 findings on `uav-isr` over 15 ports, 4/4 on `vehicle`, 6/6 on `views-tour` | The check is not redundant; it is empty |
| 13 | the rider: the two modalities differ on `FlightModes` "because the declaration-order tie-break means a simulation never reaches `failsafe`" | The fact holds; **the reason does not.** The measured reason is the `manual ⇄ autonomous` cycle inside the non-violating region. The tie-break is a fact about one simulated run | A modality computed from the tie-break prints a claim about the **simulator** as a claim about the **model** |
| 14 | `DIAGNOSTIC_CODES.length` is 103, and the `**N catalogue codes**` figure is a hand edit | **Neither number survives, and the second half is the durable correction.** The count is **100** at `dc6662c`, and it moves whenever a lane adds a code — so this plan never states it. The figure in `docs/AGENT-AUTHORING-CAMPAIGN.md` is **generated** by `npm run codes` and pinned against `DIAGNOSTIC_CODES.length` by `test/unit/docs-counts.test.ts`'s `catalogue codes` CLAIM | adding a code costs a regeneration, never a doc edit — and every guard line in §4 states a **delta**, never a total (§4 Standing rules) |
| **15** | candidates 1, 2 and 8 may print `covered`, `recoverable`, *"nothing this walk found is inescapable"* and a cut set under the four publishability conditions (`docs/05` §2) | **Unsound in one direction, and no bound sees it.** `machineAlphabet` (`explore.ts`:277, docstring :266-276) puts every `after(n)` label in the alphabet by design, `exploreMachine` offers every alphabet member at every configuration (:457-460), and `matches` (`config.ts`:597-606) applies the dwell test **only** on the `timeout` branch — the walk issues no `timeout` and advances no clock. Executed on a two-dwell fixture — **`ModelFactory`-built, because `accept after(n)` does not parse; see row 22** — whose `after(5)` transition is declared before `after(10)`: `machineAlphabet → ["after(5)","after(10)"]`, the walk reports `configs 3, exhaustive TRUE, boundHit 'none'` and calls `B` reachable, while `runStateMachine` at every advance granularity (`[5]`,`[10]`,`[100]`,`3×8`,`1×12`) enters `B` on **no run** — `execute.ts`:1305-1308 fires `enabled[0]` | The edge relation is an over-approximation that is **not** a bound, so **every publishability condition is blind to it**. §2.3 states the **polarity rule**; after the third review found two more mechanisms (§2.3), the per-mechanism conditions are collapsed into ONE gate — `walkIsExact` — and every increasing absence claim reads it while every positive witness reads its relation half or is re-worded |
| **16** | candidate 5's refutation prints a lasso `standby → manual → (autonomous → manual)^ω` (`docs/05` §2) | A lasso is an ω-run. The finite-run semantics 5a must ship — which **this plan, not `docs/05`,** introduces — admits only finite maximal runs, and `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`' 1 row spends the 1 on *"a witness trace of a run this semantics admits"* | §3.5a's semantic-profile field defines **both** run classes, and every 5a/5b verdict says which one it is about. §3.5b gains the Exit-code contract it lacked |
| **17** | candidate 2's `p` is read over any of the five atoms | `resolveProperty` (`patterns.ts`:539-550) applies no per-pattern kind filter, and `configStates` holds state ids only. A `fires`/`trigger` atom is a property of a STEP (`atoms.ts`:93-99, :346-348), and an `expression` atom needs the store §3.P deliberately does not retain — so the target set is empty and lands on §3.2b's second `fail` row: `verification/refuted`, **error, exit 1**, on a well-formed model | §3.2b gates the arm to `state` and `node` and refuses the other three as `verification/malformed-property`; §3.R takes the same gate |
| **18** | candidate 6 may print *"no simulation relation exists over the common labels …"* (`docs/05` §2) | `refinementExitCode` (`api/verification.ts`:2273-2288) has no bucket for a **decided** negative of this shape: `refined + notRefined === 0` returns **2** (:2280), whose published sentence says the model states no chain the `--via` family can read and nothing was decided | §3.6 gains an Exit-code contract and a Tests subsection; `REFINE_EXIT_CODES` (`sysprose-spec.ts`:154) gains one clause on its 2 row in commit 20, the way §2.1 writes one for `cover` |
| **19** | the scope note's hard constraint 3 — that the reserved-word guard covers this plan's new vocabulary once the forms are added | **Two of the four forms cannot be written at all, and the negative half of a third.** `negated()` (`claims.test.ts`:100-102) returns true when `NEGATION` (:93) matches the **matched span**, so any form containing `no`/`not`/`never` is defused at :157 before the allowance window is consulted. Executed on planted sentences: `no cut set` → **0** hits, `not covered` → **0**, `no simulation relation` → **0**; `recoverable` → 1, `guaranteed` → 1 | Commit 4 changes `negated()` itself (§2.6). Left alone, the guard §5(c) counts as one of two mechanisms is dead for exactly the three absence sentences this plan is organised around |
| **20** | `docs/05` §4's rejection of BDDs and symbolic representation | `docs/05`:295 carries the label *"This rejection is my inference, not a book claim"*, and :359 requires that label to travel wherever the inference is reused; §5 item 3 (the BDD ordering, and the explicit-state/symbolic scale figures) is a page its author did not open | §6.1 carries the label **and a trigger**, instead of shipping an unread-page inference as a settled, triggerless non-goal in a Reference doc linked from README |
| **21** | six further citations, re-measured after the review | liveness refusal **:1122-1146** (:1117-1121 are comment) · `exploreMachine` is called at **:1151**, and the unsupported refusal it guards closes at **:1169** · `ContractClause` **:193-209** (:193-220 runs into `ContractSubject`'s docstring) · `monitorStep` **:688-794** · the config-bound **test** is :533 and its `boundHit` assignment :534 · `judgeDerivation` **:1180**, docstring **:1156-1179** | Corrected throughout. The two load-bearing dispatch instructions are now phrased by **anchor** — "below the `const walk = exploreMachine(…)` call", "after the unsupported refusal" — not by number, so they survive the next commit that shifts the file |
| **22** | `docs/04`'s semantic profile describes `after(n)` as a dwell the explorer offers as a named event, and the first draft of §2.3 measured the time hazard on a fixture written as `.sysml` surface syntax — `transition failsafe accept after(5) then failsafeHold;` | **`accept after(n)` DOES NOT PARSE, and no user can write a timed transition in the notation at all.** Measured this session: `npm run check -- probe-after.sysml` → `expected: then \| -> found: (`, `FAILED — 8 error(s)`, rc=1 — one `no-viable-alt` per `accept`, and every following `then` in the block fails with it. `AcceptClause` is `'accept' payload=QualifiedName` (`sysml.langium`:214-215) and a QualifiedName admits no parentheses; there is **no `after` token** in the grammar and **no `attrs.after` write** anywhere in `map-to-model.ts`. The escaped spelling `accept 'after(5)'` does parse, but keeps the quotes: `attrs.trigger` is `'after(5)'` with them, `boundsSentence` (`explore.ts`:748-752) does no quoting, and `afterDuration`'s `/^after\s*\(\s*…\s*\)$/` (`config.ts`:265-270) returns `undefined` — a different trigger string, and **not** a timed transition | A timed transition is **API-only**, the same class of construct as `attrs.parallel`: reachable through `ModelFactory` (`{ trigger: 'after(5)' }`, as `semantics.mc.differential.test.ts`:403 already builds one) or through a numeric `attrs.after` written with `model.create` — **not** a `ModelFactory.transition` option, which accepts only `{ownerId, trigger, guard, effect}` and silently drops anything else (`factory.ts`:98-113). **The time clause stays** — the SDK is a supported surface and the over-approximation is real on it — but three things follow and are carried through §2.3, §3 and §4: every dwell fixture is built through the API in a `.test.ts` and **never** as a corpus `.sysml` file; **no time-clause assertion is made at the CLI level** in commits 6, 8, 9, 16 or 23; and the dwell census is re-priced as a census over **SDK/API-built** machines, because on any model a CLI command can load it is 0 by construction |
| **23** | `cover`'s `not covered` is a decided absence under the publishability conditions, and the bounds line names every parameter that decided it | **The seeded store is a sixth parameter, and when this row was first written no bound and no register row named it.** `seedStore` (`config.ts`) sets a feature only where `literalValueOf(f) !== undefined`, so an attribute declared without a literal is simply **absent** from the store; `evalStr` then yields `undefined`, and before `c929278` `guardHoldsStore` collapsed that into `false`, so the guarded edge was never offered and nothing said so. Reproduced at `2aced3c` on `package GuardProbe { part def Ctrl { attribute mode : Integer; state def Modes { state idle; state hazard; transition idle if mode == 3 then hazard; } } }`: `reach` printed `1 configuration(s) explored … exhaustive under {maxConfigs …, alphabet no named trigger}` with **every publishability condition of the day holding**, and raised `verification/unreachable-state`, `verification/dead-transition` and `verification/deadlock`. **`c929278` has since closed the decreasing half of this in the tree** — `ExploreResult.undeterminedGuards`, the fifth publishability conjunct and the `verification/guard-undetermined` warning — and it closed it **wider** than this row was written: the shipped predicate is *"a guard was consulted and decided nothing"*, which also catches a non-boolean operand under `not`/`and`/`or`, a mixed-type comparison and non-finite arithmetic. Re-executed at `dc6662c` on that same GuardProbe file: `undetermined under {…} — 1 guard(s) the walk could not evaluate; the unreachable, dead and no-way-out lists are WITHHELD`, one `verification/guard-undetermined` row, and **none of the three absence findings**. What is left for this plan is the **increasing** half | **Lifted, after the third review, from a §3.1 clause to a plan-wide disclosure and a gate clause; and after the fifth, wired to the field that shipped rather than to a narrower copy of it.** `boundsSentence` names the store, so every bounds line in every lane carries it (§2.3, §3.P); `--cover-required` **refuses to spend the 1** when every unreached cover state sits behind a guard the walk could not decide; the shape is recorded as a census field so the exposure is a number (§3.1); and **`ExploreResult.undeterminedGuards` — the shipped field, not a re-derived one — is clause (d) of `walkIsExact`**, because the same mechanism manufactures a trap, a `not recoverable` at exit 1 and a `no cut set` (§2.3, mechanism 3). A clause written to this row's original *"no declared value"* wording would read **empty** on `not mode` over a fully valued `mode`, and every increasing claim would publish over a relation missing an edge nobody decided — which is the exact failure clause (d) exists to prevent, re-entering through the definition |

**And what `docs/05` §5 flags as unverified on the page.** Item 2 (Baier & Katoen p.453, comparing over "the set of common atomic propositions") and item 4 (p.524, the `O(M·|S|)` simulation order) were **not read on the page by their own author**; item 5's Dependency Template and the SPARK `DERIVES` contrast carry the same label; item 3 (the BDD ordering and the explicit-state/symbolic scale figures) does too, and §6.1 is where this plan reuses it. Candidate 6 rests on 2 and 4; candidate 7 rests on 5. **Neither is cited as a source for a verdict anywhere in this document.** Candidate 6's labelling rule is declared as this tool's own choice, its cost is stated as "a genuinely new algorithm" rather than as a complexity bound, and candidate 7's conclusion is drawn from measurement on this tree and not from the book's transposition. **Where item 5 nevertheless shows up in prose — §3.7b's derived-vs-declared axis — it is now labelled unverified at the point of use and the sentence commit 14 ships into `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` states only the measured half** (correction 21's companion, §3.7b). `docs/05` §5's two labelled inferences ("liveness is cheap at this scale", "explicit-state beats symbolic below N states") travel with their label wherever this plan reuses them, which is §6.1 and nowhere else.

### 0.1 Five commits landed under this plan while it was being written, and this is what they moved

None of them is a lane here; all five are ground the lanes stand on. **Several things earlier revisions of this plan proposed to BUILD already exist**, and the rule for reading §3 and §4 is therefore: where this document describes a mechanism, the mechanism is described because a claim reasons about it, not because a commit adds it. A commit adds only what its own *Files:* list says it adds. Each item below was re-executed against `dc6662c` for this revision rather than taken from the commit message.

- **`c929278` — an undetermined guard is no longer indistinguishable from a false one.** `reach` used to publish `exhaustive` plus three absence findings on a model whose only transition is guarded by an attribute with no declared value. What SHIPPED, and what this plan therefore does **not** build: `ExploreResult.undeterminedGuards`, a `readonly UndeterminedGuardRow[]` populated by `stepCandidates`, which "returns the enabled transitions and the guards that decided" (`config.ts`); the three-valued `guardVerdictStore` beside it; a fifth publishability conjunct — `const guardsDecided = walk.undeterminedGuards.length === 0`, conjoined into `const publishable` in `reachOne`; the `verification/guard-undetermined` **warning**; and the `undetermined under {…} — N guard(s) the walk could not evaluate; the unreachable, dead and no-way-out lists are WITHHELD` qualification. **`undetermined` is deliberately WIDER than "no declared value"**, and the docstring on `guardVerdictStore` says so in as many words: it also covers a non-boolean operand under `not`/`and`/`or`, a mixed-type comparison and non-finite arithmetic — so `not mode` over a **fully valued** `mode` is undetermined while `mode` is decided. Any clause in this plan written as *"no declared value"* would be strictly narrower than the shipped predicate and would read green on a walk that is missing an edge; §2.3 spells clause (d) against the shipped field and nothing else. Three consequences run through this plan. (i) **`reach`'s golden output already moved**, so §3.P's *"the golden must not move"* differential is baselined on **`dc6662c`**; correction 23 below and §2.3's third mechanism are labelled with the tree they were taken on. (ii) **A bound does NOT withhold a deadlock row.** A bound stops the walk enqueueing successors and takes nothing from a configuration already dequeued and offered every input, so `recordDeadlock`'s subject is unaffected by it — which is a different fact from §2.4(b)'s Tarjan hazard, where a bound-dropped successor **does** leave a configuration looking like a sink *in the retained relation*. (iii) **A nondeterminism row IS withheld** when an undetermined guard sits strictly inside the level where the choice is reported, because that guard may have been the transition the priority rule would have picked — so §2.4(d)'s trigger is *"`nondeterministic > 0` **or** a nondeterminism row was withheld under that rule"*, or the simulator disclosure disappears on exactly the walks that least resemble the simulator.
- **`c37eb00` — `connectivity`'s self-contradiction is FIXED, and so is the same defect one level up.** It used to print *"every declared port is wired"* above three dangling ports its own connection list showed wired, because the wired and occurrence halves normalised the part reference differently. Re-executed at `dc6662c`: `connectivity examples/vehicle.sysml` prints `4 port(s), 2 connection(s), 4 connected, 0 unconnected`, the two connections, `every declared port is wired`, and the lifted-endpoint accounting line — **no `unconnected port usages` block at all**, exit 0. `ConnectivityReport.unreconciledPorts` exists and is the report's own check that the two readings agree; it is `[]` on the examples and non-empty only where they genuinely disagree. **This plan ships no commit for it**, and §3.7a cites the post-fix behaviour as the **baseline its tests re-run** rather than as a defect its census surfaces. Lane F's dependency lines say *needs the fixed inventory*, not *needs an unassigned fix*.
- **`a66ba1f` — every edge the machine has is walked, or the walk says which it did not.** `Succession` is now in `STEP_EDGE_KINDS` beside `TransitionUsage`; `SuccessionFlow` is refused by payload rather than by metaclass; `danglingTransitions` reads `CONTROL_EDGE_KINDS` so `first busy then nowhere;` and `transition busy then nowhere;` get the same answer. **And `edgeCensus` exists** — every edge-bearing element under a machine, counted off the model and not off the walk, each in exactly one bucket: `walked`, `opening`, `refused`, `off-stack`, `not-a-step`, `unaccounted`. It is published as `ExploreResult.census` and `MachineReach.census` (`--json` only). The `unaccounted` bucket is the failure bucket: a row in it **fails a test and refuses the machine**, through `unsupportedConstructs`, so an edge kind nobody enumerated cannot silently shrink an absence list. Re-executed at `dc6662c` on the probe that found the defect — three states, three transitions and one `succession degradedA then nominal` — `reach` now prints `4 of 4 transition(s) fired, 0 dead` and raises the nondeterminism row the fourth edge creates. **This is the generalisation the exactness gate was reaching for, and §2.3 is rewritten around it.**
- **`189fc6d` and `dc6662c` — the gate is 549 s, not 790 s, and the CLI campaign is restructured.** `test/campaign/cli.sysprose.test.ts` was 787 s of a 790 s run because every case spawned `npx tsx scripts/sysprose.ts`. `scripts/sysprose.ts` now exports `main(argv)` and runs `runMain` only when it was run as a script, and the campaign file keeps **two invokers**: `spawnCli(…)` for everything only a process can be wrong about — exit status, `-` on stdin, a payload past the pipe buffer, an unknown flag, an unreadable file, `--help`, one end-to-end run per exit contract — **and every solver-bearing subcommand, because z3's WASM module carries process-global state and does not survive being driven many times in one worker** — and `run(…)`, in-process, for what a case asserts about the TEXT. Measured at the gate: `npx vitest run` went **760.83 s → 548.74 s**, 0 failed and 0 solver aborts, over the file and test counts `docs/CONFORMANCE.md`'s scorecard records and `test/unit/docs-counts.test.ts` holds — **read them there rather than from this plan**, because they move with every commit that adds a test file and this document is not their guard. Every commit in §4 that budgets time, or that adds a case to that file, assumes **this** file: a case asserting text uses `run`, a case asserting the program or invoking a solver uses `spawnCli`, and a position in the file is named by its `it(...)` title. The failure mode of getting it wrong is a **green** run — vitest's own warning for an unhandled WASM abort says it "might cause false positive tests" — which is why the choice of invoker is part of a *Tests:* line and not a style preference.

## 1. What the tool has and lacks today, per candidate

Every "has" below is code that runs now; every "lacks" was reproduced this session.

| # | Candidate | What the tool already has | What it lacks — measured |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P** | the successor relation | `exploreMachine` computes every edge through `stepConfig` and discards it one line later at the revisit prune (`if (seen.has(key)) continue;`). `seen` is a `Set<string>` of configuration hashes; `Frontier` carries no identity. **What the machine's edge SET is, is already answered** — `edgeCensus` (`a66ba1f`) accounts for every edge-bearing element under the machine off the model — but the census counts edges, it does not relate configurations | No relation survives the walk, so nothing downstream can ask about cycles, bottom components, reverse reachability or acyclicity. `ExploreResult` has thirteen fields at `dc6662c` — `census` and `undeterminedGuards` among them — and **none of them is an edge between two configurations** |
| **1** | `cover` | `search` (:857) is already a breadth-first product walk with `parent` links and `traceOf`; five atoms; six patterns; the exhaustiveness conjunction at :1178 | No **existential** pattern. `pattern=existence` is refused (`verification/behaviour-unsupported-construct`, *"there is no flag that lowers it"*). The only expressible spelling of a positive reachability intent is `absence`, whose polarity **and** exit code are both inverted — measured rc=1 on the design that satisfies the intent, rc=0 on the design that violates it |
| **2** | recovery | `reach` reports unreachable states, dead transitions, deadlocks and nondeterminism; `recordDeadlock` (:631-648) already exempts `isFinalState` and `isTerminalNode` | Nothing computes what a run cannot leave. Measured on a five-state machine whose `failsafe ⇄ failsafeHold` is absorbing: *"5 configuration(s) explored, depth 4 — exhaustive … 5 of 5 state(s) reachable; 6 of 6 transition(s) fired, 0 dead"*, one pre-existing nondeterminism row, **exit 0, no finding** |
| **R** | modality | `fail` (`patterns.ts`:1219-1237) carries a witness, a code and exit 1 | `fail` means *there exists a run*, and nothing anywhere computes whether **every** run violates. Measured, the distinction separates real machines: `uav-isr` and the trap probe are *potential*; a mission DAG and a sink machine are *guaranteed* |
| **3** | `verify --why` | `encode.ts`:898 sets `(set-option :produce-unsat-cores true)` on every verify script; :923 labels every assertion `\|kind:qualifiedName\|` over four kinds — `axiom`, `premise`, `goal`, **`side`**; `z3-bridge.ts`:416 fills `CheckOutcome.core` on `unsat`; `coreSentence` (`smt.ts`:1014) already prints it for `axioms-inconsistent` and `vacuous`, **sorted** | The `proved` path drops it. `grep -rn 'negation\.core' src/ test/` returns **nothing** — the field is read nowhere in the tree. And `validation/stale-evidence` (`rules.ts`:1248) admits its own limitation verbatim at :1290: *"the digest is over the whole model, so this tool cannot say which of them moved"* |
| **4** | inheritance | `contracts` already prints `subject v : Vehicle (inherited)` — the tool owns the disclosure idiom | It withholds it for **clauses**. `clausesInheritedFrom` is populated only on the branch `clauses.length === 0 ? … : []` (`contracts.ts`:1085), so a child that writes one clause and inherits another gets `[]` and the reader is shown one clause where two apply. Measured population across every `.sysml` file in the tree: **0** |
| **7** | signature | `connectivityReport` (`analytics.ts`:1433) reports 15 ports / 9 connections / 14 connected / 1 unconnected on `uav-isr`, 5.08 ms warm; `portFacets` (`rules-connection.ts`:60) already resolves declared-or-inherited direction with **net conjugation** | The **intra-part** dependency relation the conditions need does not exist in these models (correction 11). The connector graph is one hop deep and bipartite: 9 directed edges, longest path **1**, intra-part edges **0** |
| **5a** | acyclic liveness | the liveness refusal at `patterns.ts`:1122-1146 | It returns **before** `exploreMachine` is called at :1151, so today a liveness row carries `configs: 0, exhaustive: false, witness: []` — **no graph is ever built for a liveness property.** And the engine has no finite-run semantics at all: it reasons only about finite **bad prefixes** |
| **5b** | fairness | `SYSPROSE_VERIFICATION_LIBRARY` (`verification-vocabulary.ts`:160-166) carries four `metadata def`s and the body-carrier idiom (`FaultHypothesis`, `PropertyPattern`) | No carrier for an assumption, no fair-SCC, and — measured — `bounds.alphabet` is `[]` on **all three** shipped machines, so environment justice has an empty domain exactly where it would be needed |
| **6** | simulation | `refine Abstract by Detailed;` between two `StateDefinition`s parses clean and lands as `Refine src=Detailed tgt=Abstract`; `:>` lands as `Subclassification src=Detailed tgt=Abstract` | `derivationsOf` (`refinement.ts`:1109) iterates **contracts**, so a `Refine` between two machines is invisible to it: measured, `refine <probe> --via refine` answers *"this model states no `refine` chain this lane can read"*, exit 2. The carrier exists; the reader does not |
| **8** | behavioural FTA | `judgeTarget` (`fault-tree.ts`:624-827) already owns the enumeration discipline: order-0 baseline, lexicographic `combinations`, superset pruning (*"MINIMALITY IS PRUNING, NOT FILTERING"*), `undecidedSets` tracked separately, three-valued `singlePointOfFailure`. The injection substrate works: seeding `navFail = false` leaves the hazard unreachable, `true` makes it reachable | `fault-tree` **refuses** a machine, and the refusal points at a flag that does not exist (correction 7). No failure-mode vocabulary |
