## 2. The exit contract

### 2.1 Seven contract kinds exist, and this plan creates none

Measured: `ExitContract` (`scripts/lib/sysprose-spec.ts`:327) is `'report' | 'verify' | 'refine' | 'bounds' | 'write' | 'behaviour' | 'fault-tree'`, dispatched by `exitCodesFor` at :353-359. Six of the seven judge; `report` does not, and its 1 means *the model did not load cleanly* rather than *refuted*. Every feature here lands under one that already exists:

| Feature | Command | `exitContract` | Why this one and not a new one |
|---|---|---|---|
| P | — | — | no command, no output |
| 1 `cover` | `check-behaviour` | `behaviour` | it is a property stated on a machine and decided by the same walk |
| 2a traps | `reach` | `report` | `reach` refutes nothing; a trap is a finding **inside** a report, exit 0 |
| 2b `recovery` | `check-behaviour` | `behaviour` | the engineer named the state; its refutation is a refuted assertion |
| R modality | `check-behaviour` | `behaviour` | a field and a clause on an existing row |
| 3a `--why` | `verify` | `verify` | display only; it decides nothing |
| 3b staleness | `npm run check` | `check` (`CHECK_EXIT_CODES`) | it is a validation rule; **`RULES.length` stays 25** |
| 4a disclosure | `contracts` | `report` | reports only, never a truth verdict |
| 4b specialize | `refine` | `refine` | a new **value** of `--via`, not a subcommand |
| 7a census | `connectivity` | `report` | a flag on the existing command |
| 7b conditions | `connectivity` | `report` | a structural disagreement is not a refutation |
| 5a / 5b | `check-behaviour` | `behaviour` | as 1 |
| 6 | `refine` | `refine` | it shares 4b's `--via` value |
| 8 | `fault-tree` | `fault-tree` | it reuses `judgeTarget`'s enumeration and its exit rules |

**The two places an eighth kind could be argued, and why neither earns one.**

- **`cover`'s `not covered`.** It is a *decided absence*: neither a discharged obligation (0) nor a refuted one (1). `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`' **2** row already reads *"or ANY undecided property"*, and `not covered` is decided — so the row gains one clause naming it, rather than the contract gaining a kind. The clause is: *"…or a `cover` property whose behaviour this walk did not find on any run it saw whole, which is a missing behaviour and not a violated requirement — `--cover-required` spends the 1 for it instead."* One sentence in one contract beats an eighth contract that would then have to be quoted by `renderCommandUsage`, the reference generator and `test/unit/cli-reference.test.ts`'s per-row assertion.
- **`reach`'s trap row.** A trap is a finding about a machine, exactly like `verification/unreachable-state`, and `reach` is `report`: exit **0** with findings. Giving `reach` a judging contract would change the meaning of its existing 1 — which today says *the model did not load cleanly* — for every one of its shipped rows.

**Four contract SENTENCES move, across three of the seven constants, in five edits — and the behaviour lane's 0 row is one of them, three times.** The draft of this plan said only the 2 row moved, and closed the subject with *"Nothing else in any of the seven changes"*; that was wrong in the direction that matters, and the first review caught it. The revised version then said the constant did not move again after commit 16, and the **third** review caught that too — 5b's fair pass discharges under none of the three shapes commit 16 leaves behind (the row below, and §3.5b). Measured, `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` (`sysprose-spec.ts`:201) publishes its **0** as *"every property stated on this machine was shown to hold **on every reachable configuration, over a graph this walk saw whole**, and there was at least one of them to decide"*. Today that sentence is true by construction: exit 0 requires every row to be `pass`, and `pass` is unreachable past the publishability conjunction at `patterns.ts`:1178, so **exit 0 entails an exhaustive walk**. Two of this plan's verdicts break that entailment:

- **`covered` is existential and explicitly stands on a partial walk** (§3.1). `check-behaviour <reachable probe> --element M --pattern "pattern=cover, …" --max-configs 2` yields one `covered` row, `counts.failed = 0`, nothing vacuous or inconclusive ⇒ **exit 0**, while `boundHit !== 'none'`. And even on an exhaustive walk a cover is Manna–Pnueli **Guarantee** (`◇β`) witnessed on ONE run: on the `cover-reachable` probe `state failsafe` holds at exactly one of the reachable configurations, so *"held on every reachable configuration"* is false of it in a second, independent way.
- **5a's `pass` holds on every maximal RUN**, not on every reachable configuration, and commit 16 decides a liveness pattern — which falsifies the 2 row's clause *"a liveness pattern no bad-prefix search decides"* the moment it lands.

So the contract moves as follows, and the edits are commits, not review comments:

| Constant | Row | Moves in | The edit |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`:201 | **0** | commit 6 | *"every ASSERTION stated on this machine was shown to hold on every reachable configuration, over a graph this walk saw whole, **and every `cover` stated on it was witnessed on a run this walk saw**, and there was at least one of them to decide"* |
| `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`:201 | **2** | commit 6 | the `not covered` clause quoted above |
| `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`:201 | **0** and **2** | commit 16 | 0 gains *"…or, on an acyclic graph, on every maximal run"*; 2's *"a liveness pattern no bad-prefix search decides"* narrows to *"a liveness pattern on a graph that is cyclic or was not seen whole"* |
| `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`:201 | **0** | **commit 18** | 0 gains a **third** discharge shape: *"…or, under a declared `FairnessAssumption` named on the verdict line, on every fair run of a graph this walk saw whole"*. **The draft said this constant did not move again after commit 16, and that was wrong on measurement.** After commit 16 the 0 row publishes three shapes — an assertion holding on every reachable configuration over a graph seen whole, a `cover` witnessed on a run this walk saw, and, **on an acyclic graph**, every maximal run — and 5b's `holds under fairness` is none of them: it is run-wise rather than configuration-wise, it is not a cover, and 5b runs **only** on cyclic graphs (§3.5a row 1 refuses whenever `acyclic !== true`), over the ω-run class §3.5a's profile invents for this arm alone. Without this edit `check-behaviour <cyclic fair model>` exits 0 while its own `--help` and the byte-pinned `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md` publish a sentence asserting something the run did not establish — the third instance of the defect §2.1 exists to close. Commit 18's byte-identity assertion narrows accordingly (§3.5b, §4 commit 18) |
| `FAULT_TREE_EXIT_CODES`:230 | **2** | **commit 21** | a **replacement, not a removal**, and the assignment is single. *"a state machine passed as `--element` (contract-level fault trees do not cover behaviour)"* becomes *"a state machine whose failure modes this command found none of"* — the answer commit 21 actually returns. The draft assigned this edit twice, to commit 21's Files list and to commit 23 here, and the two orderings are not interchangeable because commit 23 is HELD behind commit 21's census, which §3.8 and §5 expect to read **0**: editing in 23 leaves `--help` and the byte-pinned `CLI-REFERENCE.md`:48/:400 publishing a refusal the command stopped performing, indefinitely; deleting rather than replacing in 21 leaves the most common cause of that exit unpublished. **Commit 23 changes nothing further in this constant** |
| `REFINE_EXIT_CODES`:154 | **2** | commit 20 | one clause naming a **decided** no-simulation result (§3.6), so it is not filed under *"states no chain the `--via` family can read"* |

Nothing else in any of the seven contracts changes, and **no eighth kind is created**. That sentence has now been wrong twice, so it is stated as a rule with a test rather than as a promise: **every commit that creates a new discharge shape re-reads the row it discharges under, and the byte-identity assertions between neighbouring commits are scoped to the rows those commits do not touch** (commit 18's, narrowed in §3.5b, is the one this rule was written from). Each edit is asserted in `test/unit/cli-reference.test.ts`'s per-contract block (the `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` block at :206-214) in the commit that makes it, because the same constant is rendered into `--help` and into the byte-pinned `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md` by `exitCodesFor`. **The alternative — routing `covered` on a non-exhaustive walk to exit 2 — was considered and rejected:** it would make `--max-configs` change the claim of a witness that a bound can hide and can never invent, which is the one thing §3.1's condition row is right about.

### 2.2 The claim vocabulary opens once, from four words to six

`PropertyClaim` (`patterns.ts`:270) is `'pass' | 'fail' | 'vacuous' | 'inconclusive'`. It gains exactly two words — `'covered' | 'not-covered'` — at the `cover` commit, **used only by `cover`**, and is then **closed for the rest of this plan**. Every later feature reuses `pass`/`fail`:

- `recovery` is a universal assertion (`AG EF p`) the author wrote. Its exhaustive refutation is a violated requirement: `fail`, `verification/refuted`, exit 1. **No new word, and no rendering of one word as another** — the JSON claim and the printed sentence must agree.
- `existence` / `response` under 5a are assertions: `pass` / `fail`.
- `cover` is the only feature here whose negative answer is *a behaviour the design does not admit* rather than *a requirement the design violates*, which is exactly why hard constraint 4 exists.

**Why not launder it into the existing four.** The cheap option — `covered ⇒ pass`, `not covered ⇒ inconclusive` — files a **decided** answer under the word this tool reserves for **undecided**. That distinction is the whole load-bearing content of the exit contract, and saving six lines by dissolving it is the one economy this lane cannot make.

**The four sites that move together, or the run silently exits 0.** A word added to `PropertyClaim` and not to `counts` leaves every bucket at zero with `rows.length > 0`, which falls through the ternary at `patterns.ts`:1375-1380 to exit **0** — a green run that decided nothing. The sites, measured: `counts` (:1369-1374, filters by literal string) · `exitCode` (:1375-1380) · `BehaviourVerdict`/`judgeBehaviour` (`scripts/sysprose.ts`:410-418) · the printed sentence at `scripts/sysprose.ts`:3235, *"pass, fail, vacuous, inconclusive are the four words this command reaches"*, which appears in **every transcript** — plus `docs/CONFORMANCE.md`:882.

**The guard that makes the failure impossible:** one assertion that `counts.passed + counts.failed + counts.vacuous + counts.inconclusive + counts.covered + counts.notCovered === rows.length`, on every behaviour fixture.

**The new arithmetic:**

```
exitCode =
  counts.failed > 0                                             ? 1
: coverRequired && counts.notCovered > 0                        ? 1
: counts.vacuous || counts.inconclusive || counts.notCovered
    || rows.length === 0                                        ? 2
: 0                                     // `covered` and `pass` are both discharged
```

`--cover-required` **raises the exit code and never the claim**: promoting `not-covered` to `fail` would print `verification/refuted` about a design that violates nothing — the exact defect candidate 1 exists to fix, reproduced by the fix. A test asserts both spellings produce byte-identical claim words and different exit codes.

**Severity is a property of the code — but the shipped code does not yet read it that way, and commit 6 is what makes the sentence true.** The intent stands: a catalogue entry carries one severity, `test/unit/diagnostic-codes.test.ts`:67-89 asserts `VERIFICATION_ERROR_CODES` and `VERIFICATION_WARNING_CODES` (`src/api/verification.ts`:1078 and :1061) equal the catalogue's marked `verification/*` entries exactly, and **no flag may re-grade a code**. What the review measured is that the behaviour lane does not honour it: `behaviourReport`'s findings loop hardcodes `severity: row.claim === 'fail' ? 'error' : 'info'` (`patterns.ts`:1401), so severity is a function of the **claim** with per-branch overrides above it (`--strict-vacuity`'s own branch at :1384-1397 `continue`s before :1401). A `not-covered` row is not `fail`, so `verification/cover-required` would be catalogued `error` and **emitted `info`** — and the guard cited compares two hand-maintained constants against the catalogue, never an emitted diagnostic, so it stays green through exactly that drift. Commit 6 therefore ships three things together: a dedicated findings-loop branch for `not-covered` under `--cover-required` (severity `'error'`, mirroring :1384-1397), the registration of the new code in `VERIFICATION_ERROR_CODES`, and **the assertion this plan assumed already existed** — for every diagnostic any behaviour fixture emits, its severity equals the catalogue's severity for its code.

**And the precedent cited for the two-code design does not exist.** Measured: `verification/vacuous` and `verification/vacuous-property` never coexist. The loop at `patterns.ts`:1384-1397 handles a `vacuous` row by `continue`-ing with **no** finding when the flag is absent and by pushing exactly one `verification/vacuous-property` when it is present — one code or none, never two — and the catalogue says so verbatim at `diagnostic-codes.ts`:586: *"`check-behaviour` raises it from a third row, **which carries no code of its own**."* The SMT lane is a ternary substitution too (`api/verification.ts`:1626). So the two-code design is **this plan's own choice, not an inherited pattern**, and the reason is stated rather than borrowed: the info row must survive so that a `--cover-required` run and a plain run print the same claim word and the same `verification/not-covered` finding, differing only by the added error and the exit code — which is what §3.1's byte-identity test asserts. An implementer copying the substitution shape literally would drop `verification/not-covered` on the flagged run, and that is the failure this paragraph exists to stop.

**`--allow-inconclusive` is untouched.** It is scoped over SMT-lane codes (`docs/04` §2) and the behaviour lane's `verification/behaviour-unsupported-construct` is published as un-lowerable. Nothing in this plan widens it, and no new code joins its scope.

### 2.3 The publishability spine, and the absence-claim register

The rule that governs every absence claim exists **twice** today, as a local `const publishable` in `reachOne` (`explore.ts`:786-787) and a local `const exhaustive` in `checkProperty` (`patterns.ts`:1177-1178), each with a comment saying the other must not be deleted alone. This plan adds five more readers of it. **Commit 1 lifts it into one exported predicate and one register**, before anything reads it.

```ts
// src/semantics/mc/publishable.ts   (new, commit 1)
export interface Publishability {
  readonly exhaustive: boolean;      // walk.exhaustive — boundHit === 'none'
  readonly alphabetOffered: boolean; // every named trigger was actually offered
  readonly supported: boolean;       // walk.unsupported.length === 0
  /** The fifth clause, SHIPPED at `c929278` and not built here (§0.1):
   *  `walk.undeterminedGuards.length === 0` — no transition guard was
   *  consulted and left undecided. Transcribe it as that expression and
   *  nothing else. "No declared value for a feature it reads" is STRICTLY
   *  NARROWER than the shipped predicate — `guardVerdictStore`'s own docstring
   *  says `'undetermined'` also covers a non-boolean operand under
   *  `not`/`and`/`or`, a mixed-type comparison and non-finite arithmetic, so
   *  `not mode` over a fully valued `mode` is undetermined while `mode` is
   *  decided — and a narrower copy would let commit 1 regress a shipped
   *  withholding on a walk its own byte-equality gate cannot see, because none
   *  of the six examples carries a guard at all (§5(d)).
   *  `verification/guard-undetermined` is the warning that prints where it
   *  fails, and the decreasing lists are emptied rather than shortened. */
  readonly guardsDetermined: boolean;
  /** found.boundHit === 'none'. PRODUCT SEARCH ONLY. On a walk-only claim
   *  no search ran, there is no `found`, and this field is absent — never
   *  `false`, which would print "bound exhausted" about a bound nobody hit. */
  readonly searchComplete?: boolean;
  /** The conjunction over the fields present. What a monotone-DECREASING
   *  claim asks for. `reachOne` and today's `checkProperty` read THIS, which is
   *  why they stay byte-identical on every model, timed or not, and it is the
   *  ONLY thing this plan asks of the claims that ship today. */
  readonly decreasingOk: boolean;
  readonly sentence: string;         // the qualification string the row prints
}

/** THE GATE FOR THE WHOLE INCREASING SIDE. One predicate, two named halves.
 *  Read `walkIsExact` from an increasing ABSENCE claim; read
 *  `relationIsTheMachines` from an EXISTENTIAL witness (a bound can hide such a
 *  witness and can never invent one); read `walkIsExact` again from a
 *  MAXIMALITY witness — W3's cycle with no exit, W4's run with no successor —
 *  which a bound and an undecided guard both manufacture. Nothing on the
 *  increasing side reads anything else from this family, and the register's
 *  `kind` column is what selects between the two readings. */
export interface Exactness {
  /** The walk saw the graph whole: `walk.exhaustive` ∧ `walk.boundHit === 'none'`
   *  ∧ `walk.unsupported.length === 0`. Both conjuncts of the first are stated
   *  because the unsupported early return sets `exhaustive: false` with
   *  `boundHit: 'none'` and a bound sets `boundHit` before it `continue`s —
   *  neither implies the other. The `unsupported` conjunct is also where the
   *  ELEMENT-SET half of exactness is enforced; see the note below. */
  readonly seenWhole: boolean;
  /** THE PROPERTY, and the three clauses are how it is checked — not what it
   *  means. What it means: every edge the machine has was retained, and every
   *  edge retained is one the machine has.
   *
   *  The first direction is answered OFF THE MODEL by `edgeCensus`
   *  (`explore.ts`, shipped at `a66ba1f`) and needs no clause here: an edge
   *  under the machine that the relation does not follow and nothing refused
   *  lands in the census's `unaccounted` bucket, which REFUSES the machine
   *  through `unsupportedConstructs` — so `seenWhole` is already false and no
   *  absence, increasing or decreasing, is published over it. That is why this
   *  field has no clause (e) for the succession hole that found it (§0.1).
   *
   *  The three clauses below are the OTHER direction, and they are all of one
   *  kind: an edge that IS in the relation but whose availability at a
   *  configuration the walk gets wrong. The census cannot see any of them,
   *  because to the census the edge is present and walked.
   *   • (b) `walk.timedTransitions` is empty — no transition of this machine is
   *     a dwell, whether or not it contributed a label to the alphabet. The
   *     walk offers the edge at every configuration and advances no clock;  AND
   *   • (c) `walk.alphabet` is empty — this engine cannot establish that an
   *     offered trigger is one the environment could actually supply, so the
   *     only alphabet it can be exact over is the empty one;                 AND
   *   • (d) `walk.undeterminedGuards.length === 0` — no transition guard was
   *     consulted and left undecided. THE SHIPPED FIELD (`c929278`), read as
   *     the shipped predicate: not "no declared value", which is narrower and
   *     which reads empty on `not mode` over a fully valued `mode`. */
  readonly relationIsTheMachines: boolean;
  /** Both. NAMED `walkIsExact`, which is what every register cell, verdict row
   *  and MUST-NEVER in this plan calls it; the function that computes an
   *  `Exactness` is `walkIsExact(walk, bounds)` and returns this field under
   *  the same name, so a row wired to `walkIsExact` names a field that exists.
   *  (An earlier draft declared it `exact` here and used `walkIsExact`
   *  everywhere else, which would have landed commit 1's reflection test red
   *  against a producer with no such field on nine rows.) */
  readonly walkIsExact: boolean;
  /** Which clause failed, for the `inconclusive` sentence. Exactly one is
   *  reported, in THIS order, so two machines that fail for two reasons never
   *  print the same sentence:
   *    'bound'       — !walk.exhaustive with a boundHit
   *    'unsupported' — the early return for a refused construct, which includes
   *                    an `unaccounted` census row
   *    'environment' — `alphabet ∖ timedLabels` non-empty: a named trigger that
   *                    is not a dwell label. FIRST of the three relation
   *                    clauses, because an author whose machine names `abort`
   *                    has something to do about it and the dwell sentence
   *                    would send them to the wrong carrier (§3.R rows 5-6)
   *    'time'        — not the above, and `timedTransitions` non-empty
   *    'store'       — neither, and `undeterminedGuards` non-empty
   *  The draft ordered 'time' before 'environment', which contradicted §3.R's
   *  own row-precedence test (row 5 beats row 6 on a machine carrying both). */
  readonly failedClause:
    | 'bound' | 'unsupported' | 'environment' | 'time' | 'store' | null;
  /** The one-line reason, printed beside every claim this gate withholds. */
  readonly sentence: string;
}
```

**One name per predicate, and the numbered-condition vocabulary survives only on the decreasing side.** `walkIsExact(walk, bounds): Exactness` is the **function**; where a register cell, a verdict row or a MUST-NEVER says **`walkIsExact`** it means that function's `walkIsExact` field, and where a row needs one half it names the half — `seenWhole` or `relationIsTheMachines`. The numbered conditions belong to `Publishability` and to nothing else, and the mapping is recorded once, here, so an older reading of a §3 row can be checked against it: PC1 → `Publishability.exhaustive`, PC2 → `.alphabetOffered`, PC3 → `.supported`, PC4 → `.searchComplete`. **There is no PC5 and there never was one in this document's own vocabulary** — the draft's fifth, time-only condition has no numbered successor; it is a clause of `relationIsTheMachines`, and a clause of the gate is named by its letter and its producer, never by a number. `Publishability.guardsDetermined` shipped at `c929278` and has no number either. **No `PC` name appears in `publishable.ts`, in a register cell, in a verdict row, in a MUST-NEVER list or in a test name**, and a cell naming one is a red test in exactly the way a per-mechanism conjunction is — the numbers are what let the last two rounds of this defect be re-argued one row at a time, and they survive here only as a decoder ring for the decreasing conjunction.

`exhaustive`, `alphabetOffered` and `supported` are `reachOne`'s conjunction verbatim; `searchComplete` is `checkProperty`'s extra conjunct; `guardsDetermined` is the fifth conjunct that shipped at `c929278` (§0.1). Together they are `decreasingOk`, which is what the claims that ship today already read and what they keep reading, unchanged, byte for byte — against the **`dc6662c`** golden, which is the baseline commit 1's byte-equality gate is measured on.

**Why the gate is a predicate and not a list, stated once here and not re-litigated per feature.** Five readers have now found five distinct ways the retained relation differs from the machine an author wrote, one per review round, each invisible to the condition set built for the last one. That is not five bugs; it is one observation about the method. Enumerating mechanisms cannot converge, because the enumeration is written by reading the code and the next mechanism is precisely the one the code did not make visible. **So the question is inverted, and the tree has already shipped the inversion:** `edgeCensus` counts the edge-bearing elements under a machine **without asking the walk** and requires each one to land in a bucket that says what became of it. An edge nobody thought about does not shrink an absence list quietly; it lands in `unaccounted`, fails a test and refuses the machine. The five mechanisms below are the **evidence for that rule, not the definition of it**, and they divide cleanly along the line the census draws:

1. **Time, over-approximating — the walk's offering, not the edge set.** `machineAlphabet` (`explore.ts`) puts every `after(n)` label in the alphabet by design; `exploreMachine` offers every alphabet member at every configuration; `matches` (`config.ts`) applies the dwell test **only** on the `timeout` branch, and the walk issues no `timeout` and advances no clock. The edge is in the relation; what is wrong is when it is available. **Clause (b).**
2. **The cooperative environment, over-approximating — also the offering.** Every trigger in the alphabet is offered at every configuration, unconditionally. Measured on a clean-loading file — `state nominal; state locked; transition nominal then locked; transition locked accept unlatch then nominal;` — `reach` prints `2 configuration(s) explored, depth 1 — exhaustive under {maxConfigs 10000, maxDepth 200, maxCompletion 64, alphabet unlatch}`, `2 of 2 state(s) reachable; 2 of 2 transition(s) fired, 0 dead`, rc=0, no findings. Every publishability condition holds — the guard clause included, since this machine has no guard at all — and no transition is a dwell, so the retained relation is one SCC of two containing the opening: exemption 3 fires, §3.2a would publish *no component of this walk is inescapable beyond its endings*, and §3.2b would answer `recovery p = state nominal` with `recoverable` at exit **0** — about a design in which `locked → nominal` exists only because the walk hands the machine `unlatch` at every configuration. In the system where `unlatch` never arrives, `locked` is an inescapable mode: the defect §3.2a exists to find, published as its negation. **Clause (c).**
3. **The undecided guard, under-approximating — the offering again, in the other direction.** `seedStore` (`config.ts`) sets a feature only where `literalValueOf(f) !== undefined`, so a guard over an attribute declared without a literal evaluates `undefined`; before `c929278` `guardHoldsStore` collapsed that into `false` and the machine's edge was simply missing from the walk with nothing said. Measured at `2aced3c` on `attribute resetOk : Boolean;` with `transition nominal then degradedA; transition degradedA then degradedB; transition degradedB then degradedA; transition degradedA if resetOk then nominal;`: `3 configuration(s) explored, depth 2 — exhaustive under {…}`, `3 of 4 transition(s) fired, 1 dead`, rc=0, every publishability condition of the day holding. **`c929278` closed the decreasing half** — re-executed at `dc6662c` the same file reports `undetermined under {…} — 1 guard(s) the walk could not evaluate`, `3 of 4 transition(s) fired, dead withheld`, rc=0 — and left the increasing half exactly where it was: the retained relation still holds a non-trivial bottom SCC that does not contain the opening and whose states are neither final nor terminal, so all three of §3.2a's exemptions still pass, and without a gate §3.2a would publish `trap — 2 configuration(s) form a set nothing leaves: {degradedA, degradedB}` and §3.2b would answer `not recoverable: 2 configuration(s) cannot reach \`state nominal\``, `verification/refuted`, **error, exit 1**, on a design whose escape edge the model states. **Clause (d)**, and it reads the field `c929278` shipped rather than a narrower re-derivation of it.
4. **The unlabelled dwell — the same mechanism as 1, reached through the producer nobody instrumented.** A transition carrying a numeric `attrs.after` and **no** `attrs.trigger` is timed by the tool's own predicate (`afterDuration` reads the number before it tries the string), is a **completion** transition to `isCompletion` so `matches` never reaches the dwell test, and contributes **nothing** to the alphabet because `triggerLabelOf` is `''`. Measured on a `Model`+`ModelFactory` machine — `failsafe --after(5)--> failsafeHold`, `failsafe --after(60)--> standby`, `failsafeHold --after(5)--> failsafe`, all three written `m.create('TransitionUsage', {attrs:{after: n}})` — `afterDuration(t1) = 5`, `isCompletion(t1) = true`, `alphabet = []`, `configs 3 exhaustive true boundHit none unsupported 0 offered [] fired 3 reachable 3`. A clause (b) scoped to *alphabet contributors* reads **empty** here, clause (c) reads empty and clause (d) reads empty — the gate would return true on a machine every one of whose edges is a dwell the walk erased. **So clause (b)'s producer is the TRANSITION SET and not an alphabet subset**, the `timedLabels ⊆ alphabet` containment and the *"clause (c) entails clause (b)"* inference that rested on it are **deleted**, and the per-step disjunction tests `afterDuration` on the **step's transition id** and never on the recorded label, which for such a step is `''`. This one is not a fifth mechanism; it is the first mechanism read through the wrong field, and it is the sharpest argument in this section against clause-by-clause reasoning.
5. **The missing edge KIND — the element set, and the one the census now owns.** A `succession degradedA then nominal;` written between two states of a `state def` parsed clean, landed in the model as a real edge, and was dropped by the step relation because that relation enumerated `TransitionUsage` alone. Every clause above read green on it: no dwell, no alphabet, no guard, exhaustive, nothing unsupported. It manufactured a trap and a `not recoverable` at exit 1 in the increasing direction **and** invented a `verification/unreachable-state` and a `verification/deadlock` in the decreasing one — the only mechanism so far that falsifies both sides. **It is FIXED at `a66ba1f`** (`STEP_EDGE_KINDS` is `{TransitionUsage, Succession}`; `SuccessionFlow` is refused by payload) and, more to the point, the *class* is closed: `edgeCensus` reads its domain off `model.descendants(machineId)` — every element carrying an endpoint — so a sixth edge kind lands in `unaccounted`, refuses the machine, and is caught by the census's own test before any absence is published. Re-executed at `dc6662c`, the probe walks `4 of 4 transition(s) fired, 0 dead` and raises the nondeterminism row the fourth edge creates.

**What that leaves the gate to do, exactly.** `relationIsTheMachines` asks one question — *is the relation this walk retained the machine's relation?* — and it is answered in two places, deliberately: the **element set** by the shipped census, which needs no clause here because an `unaccounted` row already refuses the machine and therefore already falsifies `seenWhole`; and the **availability of an edge at a configuration** by the three clauses, which the census cannot see because to the census those edges are present and walked. A sixth mechanism of the first kind costs this plan nothing — the census catches it and no register row, verdict table or commit moves. A sixth mechanism of the second kind costs one clause inside `Exactness` and, still, no register row, no verdict table and no commit. That is the whole property the collapse was built for, and it is why the answer to *"a fifth mechanism will be found"* is not a fifth patch.

**What the gate costs, and where it is unfalsifiable.** Clause (c) is the expensive one: *the alphabet must be empty*. This engine has no carrier for "which triggers can the environment actually supply", so the only alphabet over which it can honestly claim exactness is the empty one — and that is a real narrowing, not a formality. It means A6 (traps), A7 (`recoverable`) and A11 (`no fair cycle`) publish **only** on completion-driven machines. §5(d)'s measured survey says what that costs today, and it is the kill-measurement every feature that reads this gate is retired on: all eleven analysable machines in this tree have an empty alphabet and **no dwell transition**, so the gate suppresses nothing that ships — and **no model in the corpus can falsify clause (b) or clause (c): there is no `after(n)` and no `accept` anywhere in it.** Clause (d) is the exception, and it is a recent one: `test/fixtures/verification/models/guard-undetermined.sysml` (`c929278`) carries an undecidable guard and two decided controls, so that clause has a corpus witness and this plan's trapguard fixtures extend a family rather than inventing one. Every `inconclusive` path this predicate creates is exercised only by the fixtures this plan invents (the latch machine, the trapguard machine, the preempted-dwell machine, and the numeric-`attrs.after` machine of reading 4 above), which makes those fixtures the only coverage those branches will ever get and not optional colour. **The element-set half is the exception and it is worth saying why:** `census.unaccounted` is checked on every machine every walk, including all six that ship, so the one clause of exactness with real corpus coverage is the one this plan does not implement. §5 states this plainly rather than implying the corpus exercises them, and each affected feature's census row carries the same number as its own kill-measurement.

**And one thing the gate does NOT cover, which must print anyway.** `walkIsExact` says the walk explored the machine's relation. It does not say the walk explored what the **shipped simulator** does: measured, `verification/nondeterministic-choice` reads **1** on `UAVSurveillanceSystem::FlightModes` (at `autonomous`) and **1** on `VehicleModel::VehicleStates` (at `idling`) — on two of the three shipped machines the walk explores both branches of a declaration-order tie-break that `runStateMachine` resolves by firing `enabled[0]`. That is exact for the machine and inexact for the simulator, so §2.4 gains a fourth standing sentence saying so, printed on every increasing claim.

**There are two conjunctions and not one, and that is a correction the second review forced.** The draft exported a single `ok`, "the conjunction over the fields present", and it would have shipped a regression on **already-shipped behaviour** the moment commit 3 landed. `reachOne` has one call site for its conjunction — `const publishable = walk.exhaustive && alphabetOffered && walk.unsupported.length === 0 && guardsDecided`, **four conjuncts at `dc6662c`, not the three it had before `c929278`** — feeding **five** published fields: `unreachable: []`, `dead: []`, `exhaustive`, `suppressed`, and the `qualification` string with its four branches. A1/A2 are monotone-**decreasing**: the polarity argument below is that the time clause is *unnecessary* for them. Under a single `ok`, any machine carrying an `after(n)` transition would flip `publishable` false, empty a sound `verification/unreachable-state` finding out of a shipped command, and print the `partial under {…} — a trigger the machine names was never offered` branch when every trigger **was** offered. The register's `walkRequires` column is per claim; a boolean cannot express it. So each claim's producer reads the conjunction its **own** row names — `decreasingOk` on the decreasing side, `Exactness.walkIsExact` or `Exactness.relationIsTheMachines` on the increasing one — and the reflection test of §5(a) asserts the selection rather than trusting it. The regression is also invisible to commit 1's gate — byte-equality on the six examples, none of which contains an `after(n)` transition, a named trigger or a guard (measured, §5's census; and correction 22 says why no `.sysml` file will ever carry the first) — so commits 1 and 3 each carry a **factory-built timed machine** in their differential, which is the only input that can tell `decreasingOk` and the exactness gate apart.

**The polarity rule, which the draft of this plan did not state and which is the reason the increasing side is gated at all.** Publishability is not one property. An absence claim is a statement of the form *"no object of this kind exists in the graph"*, and the graph this lane reasons over is not the machine's: it **over-approximates** what the machine offers in two ways (a dwell taken with no clock, a trigger nobody would send), **under-approximates** it in a third (an edge behind a guard nothing decided), and — until `a66ba1f` — could **miss an edge kind outright**, which is the one direction that falsifies both sides at once and the one the census now closes off the model rather than off the walk. Two things follow, and they point in opposite directions:

- **A1 and A2 — the two absence LISTS `reach` publishes today — are monotone-DECREASING in the edge relation.** (A0, the third shipped claim, is the exception that proves the rule and is why it has its own row: it is decreasing in the two over-approximating mechanisms and **increasing in the undecided-guard one**, because a missing edge invents a deadlock rather than hiding one — which is exactly why `c929278` gates it, and why the row survives as a register entry over shipped machinery rather than as a code change.) Add edges and the unreachable list can only shrink and the dead-transition list can only shrink. That is precisely why the over-approximation is sound for them, and the engine says so in its own words, in `explore.ts`'s header: *"an over-approximation can only shrink the absence claims, never invent one, which is the direction a finding may be wrong in"*, and again in `profile.ts`'s `time` facet, *"the unreachable and dead lists stay lower bounds."* **Both sentences are about an over-approximation and neither covers a missing edge**, which is why reading 5 above falsified them and why the census, not a clause, is what makes them true again. A5 (`not covered`), A3 (`pass`) and A4 (`vacuous`) are decreasing for the same reason: every one of them gets **harder** to state as edges are added, and they keep `decreasingOk` and nothing more.
- **A6, A7, A11, A12 and A13's positive half are monotone-INCREASING.** *"Nothing this walk found is inescapable"*, *"`state standby` is reachable from every reachable configuration"*, *"no fair cycle"*, *"no simulation relation"*, and every published witness — a cover, a trap entry, a cut set — get **easier** to state as edges are added. The exact mechanism that protects A1/A2 falsifies them, and they read `walkIsExact`.
- **A8 (`guaranteed`) and A10 (`holds on every maximal run`) are decreasing in edges and still read the exactness gate**, which is the case that shows why the mechanisms are not all edge-polarity phenomena. Both quantify over the **run set**, not over the edge set, and the cooperative environment changes the run set in a direction the edge polarity does not see: on the measured `latch` machine, `nominal` forever is a maximal run of the system and is not a run of the walk at all, so `guaranteed` is published about runs that were never candidates. Their own §3 sections already required an empty alphabet for exactly this reason; reading `walkIsExact` is that clause plus three more, and it removes the third independent spelling of one idea.

**The polarity column stays honest, and the executable column is SPLIT IN TWO — which is the decision the fourth review forced, because the draft's "one of exactly two values" was false of nine of its own fifteen absence rows and of all six of its witness rows.** The draft asked one column to hold both the walk predicate a producer reads and every non-walk conjunct a claim happens to need (acyclicity, a fair continuation, four solver digests, a non-empty dependency template), then asserted by reflection that the column held one of two values. That test could not have been written against the plan's own data, and the repair under a red gate is to weaken the test. So the register carries two columns and the assertion moves to the first:

- **`walkRequires`** — what the producer reads out of this family, and it takes one of exactly **four** values: **`'decreasingOk'`**, **`'walkIsExact'`**, **`'relationIsTheMachines'`**, or **`null`** for a claim that is not about a walk at all (A9, A14, A15). `relationIsTheMachines` is admissible because it is what a positive existential witness reads — *a bound can hide a witness and can never invent one* — and the draft's two-value rule made every witness row illegal by construction, which is how the register and §5(a) came to assert something no row could satisfy.
- **`alsoRequires`** — free-form conjuncts that are **not** members of this family: `acyclic === true`, the fair-continuation condition, both run classes checked, the four digests of A9, a non-empty dependency template. A per-mechanism conjunction is still a red test **in `walkRequires`**; what is now legal is a shape conjunct that was always legal and had nowhere honest to sit.

- **`polarity`** — and this column takes the same treatment `walkRequires` just got, because the two-value version of it was false of six of the plan's own fifteen absence rows in exactly the way the `requires` column was false of nine. It is a closed enumeration **wide enough for its own data**: `'decreasing' | 'increasing' | 'mixed' | 'not-a-walk'`. `mixed` is A0 (decreasing in the over-approximating mechanisms, increasing in the undecided-guard one), A8 and A10 (decreasing in edges, but quantifying over the **run set**) and A13 (`no cut set` decreasing, each published cut set increasing); `not-a-walk` is A9, A14 and A15. Beside it sits **`polarityNote`**, free-form, which names the mechanism the polarity flips in — *"increasing in the undecided-guard mechanism: a missing edge invents this row"*, *"over the run set, which the cooperative environment changes"* — because that detail is what the reflection test needs and what a two-valued cell was silently dropping.

The reflection test asserts, mechanically: `walkRequires` is one of the four values; A1–A5 read `decreasingOk` byte-identically to their shipped conjunctions; **no `alsoRequires` entry on any row names a member of the bound family** (`seenWhole`, `decreasingOk`, `Publishability.searchComplete`) — that is where a bound condition would otherwise creep back onto a witness one conjunct at a time; and `walkRequires: null` appears only on a row whose `polarity` is `'not-a-walk'`. **And the polarity rule, restated so that it is satisfiable and still bites:** *no row whose polarity is `'increasing'` or `'mixed'` may have `walkRequires === 'decreasingOk'` **unless** its `alsoRequires` names a conjunct that covers the mechanism its `polarityNote` identifies.* Exactly one row in the register uses that escape — A0, whose leaf-wise guard conjunct covers the one mechanism its note names — and it is legal by **stating why**, which is what the two-valued column could only have achieved by mis-typing the row. A row whose polarity is `'decreasing'` and whose `walkRequires` is the gate is legal in the other direction with no escape needed, and states the strengthening in its own `Otherwise` cell; the reverse, unexcused, is a red test.

The over-approximation is not hypothetical and it is not a bound. `machineAlphabet` (`explore.ts`, and its own docstring says so) puts every `after(n)` spelling in the alphabet on purpose; `exploreMachine` offers every alphabet member at every configuration; and `matches` (`config.ts`) tests the dwell **only** on the `timeout` branch — `cfg.clock - entryTime[sid] >= n` — while the walk issues no `timeout` input and advances no clock. Measured, on a machine **built through `ModelFactory`** — `f.transition(failsafe, failsafeHold, { trigger: 'after(5)' })` declared before `f.transition(failsafe, standby, { trigger: 'after(60)' })` — the interpreter fires `enabled[0]` on any clock advance (`execute.ts`), so no run ever takes the dwell-60 edge and `{failsafe, failsafeHold}` **is** an inescapable trap — the exact defect §3.2a exists to find. The walk offers `"after(60)"` as a named event with no dwell test, the escape edge exists in `successors`, the component is not bottom, and the walk is fully exhaustive: `boundHit: 'none'`, alphabet offered, nothing unsupported, **the census clean** — every edge of this machine is walked, which is the point: the census is blind here by construction, because nothing is missing from the relation. Under the draft's four publishability conditions, A6 and A7 both held; `recoverable` printed at exit 0 and `reach --json` said nothing was inescapable. Under `walkIsExact` the machine fails clause (b) *and* clause (c) — it has dwell labels, and it has a non-empty alphabet — and both claims are withheld with the failed clause named. **No compensating diagnostic fires** — `recordNondeterminism` is per (configuration, input) and the two transitions match different trigger strings, so there is not even a `verification/nondeterministic-choice` row. And §2.4(a)'s standing sentence does not cover it: a dwell is not a trigger an environment can withhold, so that disclosure names the wrong hazard.

**And the dwell fixture is written through the factory because it cannot be written any other way — correction 22, and it re-frames what clause (b) costs and what it buys.** `accept after(n)` is a **parse error**: measured, `npm run check` on a file carrying `transition failsafe accept after(5) then failsafeHold;` reports `expected: then | -> found: (` and `FAILED — 8 error(s)`, rc=1, because `AcceptClause` is `'accept' payload=QualifiedName` (`sysml.langium`:214-215). The escaped spelling `accept 'after(5)'` parses and is a *different trigger string* — quotes included — which `afterDuration` (`config.ts`:265-270) does not match, so it is not a timed transition either. **A timed transition is therefore API-only**, exactly like `attrs.parallel`: it arrives through `ModelFactory`'s `{ trigger: 'after(5)' }` or a numeric `attrs.after`, and never from a `.sysml` file. Three consequences run through the rest of this plan, and none of them is that clause (b) is dropped — the SDK is a supported surface, `reach`, `check-behaviour` and `fault-tree` all run over API-built models in-process, and the over-approximation is real there:

1. **Every dwell fixture in this plan is factory-built, in a `.test.ts`.** The names stay — *the two-dwell fixture*, *the preempted-dwell fixture* — but they are `ModelFactory` machines, never corpus files. A campaign fixture written in text would fail the corpus gate on landing with 6–8 parse errors and then assert a failed time clause over a walk that never ran.
2. **No time-clause assertion is made at the CLI level.** Commits 6, 8, 9, 16 and 23 assert the dwell rows through the in-process API (`checkProperty`, `reachReport`), not through `check-behaviour`/`reach` argv. **The environment and store clauses are the opposite** — the latch machine and the trapguard machine are ordinary `.sysml` files that load clean (both measured this session), so those two clauses are asserted through argv as well as in-process, and they are the only clauses of the gate a corpus fixture can reach.
3. **The dwell census is a census over SDK-built machines, not over a user's `.sysml` corpus.** On anything a CLI command can load `timedTransitions.size` is 0 by construction, so §5's commit-3 row is re-priced accordingly: it can retire the time clause's *apparatus* only against API traffic, and against text models the honest reading is that the clause costs nothing and guards a surface only the SDK reaches.

**The gate's producers, and TWO OF THE FOUR ALREADY SHIP.** Read this list as *what the gate reads*, not as *what commit 2 builds*; the *Files:* line in §4 is what says which of them is new.

- **`timedTransitions: ReadonlySet<ElementId>`** — **clause (b)'s gate producer. NEW, commit 2.** Recorded by `exploreMachine` over `walkableTransitions(model, machineId).filter(tr => afterDuration(tr) !== undefined)` — **the transition set, not the subset of the alphabet those transitions contributed**, which is the correction reading 4 forced above: a numeric `attrs.after` with no trigger is a dwell that contributes no label, and a clause scoped to alphabet contributors reads empty on a machine made entirely of them. `afterDuration` has **two** producers, a numeric `attrs.after` and a `trigger` string matching `/^after\s*\(\s*n\s*\)$/`; correction 22 measured that neither is writable from the notation — the first arrives through `model.create` (**not** a `ModelFactory.transition` option, which accepts only `{ownerId, trigger, guard, effect}` and silently drops the rest) and the second through `ModelFactory`'s `{ trigger: 'after(5)' }`.
- **`timedLabels: ReadonlySet<string>`** — **not a gate producer. NEW, commit 2.** The alphabet members contributed by those transitions, retained for one job only: deciding whether a non-empty alphabet is *real triggers* or *all dwells*, which is what §3.R's rows 5-6 and §3.5a's rows 2-3 split on and what `failedClause`'s `'environment'`-before-`'time'` order is computed from. **The containment claim — `timedLabels ⊆ alphabet`, therefore "clause (c) entails clause (b)" — is deleted.** It is true of the field and false of the clause: the two clauses are **independent**, and the machine that shows it has `alphabet === []` and three dwell transitions.
- **`bounds.alphabet`** — clause (c). **Already computed** and already published on every bounds line; the gate reads `alphabet.length === 0` and nothing new is produced.
- **`walk.undeterminedGuards`** — clause (d). **ALREADY SHIPPED at `c929278`**, as `readonly UndeterminedGuardRow[]` on `ExploreResult`, populated by `stepCandidates` — *"returns the enabled transitions and the guards that decided"* — and already read by `reachOne` as the fifth publishability conjunct. **The gate reads `walk.undeterminedGuards.length === 0` and nothing else.** No second field, no narrower re-derivation, and no edit to `guardHoldsStore` or its callers: a second copy under a second name is precisely the drift `config.ts`'s own header exists to prevent, and the narrower reading would go green on `not mode` over a fully valued `mode`. **On the decreasing side this clause is not this plan's** — the earlier warrant here, *"the walk and the interpreter agree there, so no publishability condition on that side can see it"*, was true of `unreachable`, `dead` and `not covered`, whose polarity a missing edge does not flip, and **false of `verification/deadlock`**, whose polarity it inverts outright. `c929278` closed that too, gating the `deadlocks` array on the guards alone. What survives here is register row **A0**, which is now a row *about shipped machinery* plus one strengthening (below).
- **`walk.census`** — no clause, and that is the point. `census.unaccounted` non-empty already refuses the machine, so `seenWhole` carries the element-set half and the gate needs no producer for it. It is read by §5's exactness census as a recorded number.

Equivalently and more cheaply stated: *every retained edge is an edge the machine has, and every edge the machine has was retained — the second half checked off the model by the census, the first half by the three clauses.* Where the gate fails, the honest answer is stated per feature — an `inconclusive` naming the failed clause for A6, A7, A8, A10, A11, A12 and A13, and a **re-worded or withheld witness** for every positive claim (§2.4(c), and `WITNESS_CLAIMS` per row) — never a silent publish.

**A sixth mechanism arrived while this section was being written, and it is left
without a clause on purpose.** Run-to-completion preemption is an *availability*
difference: a transition the model states can be unavailable at a configuration
because the completion chase took the machine elsewhere first. It is the second
kind, so by the rule stated just above it would cost one clause — and it is
recorded here instead, because six mechanisms found by six readers is no longer a
list that can be completed by looking harder. What closes this class is the
element-set half, which `edgeCensus` answers off the MODEL without asking the
walk: an edge nobody accounted for refuses the machine whether or not anyone
predicted the way it would go missing. A seventh will arrive. The register's job
is to make its arrival a red test rather than a published sentence, and the
census is the half that does that without being told what to look for.

**And the bounds line names the store, in every lane, because that is what `exhaustive under {…}` has always meant.** Correction 23's clause is lifted out of §3.1 and into `boundsSentence` itself, which today names `maxConfigs`, `maxDepth`, `maxCompletion` and `alphabet` and omits the one parameter that decided the trapguard measurement above. After commit 3 it reads `…, store seeded from declared literal values — a guard over an attribute with no declared value is read as false`. One edit covers A5, A6, A7, A8, A10 and A13 at once, and it is the honest reading of the qualification string every one of them prints. **It is a disclosure and not a clause**, and it says less than clause (d) does: `c929278`'s predicate covers guards that decided nothing for reasons other than an undecided feature, and the bounds line names only the common one. Where the two differ the `verification/guard-undetermined` row is what a reader acts on.

**What the exactness gate is NOT for.** It is not a truncation flag. A truncation flag was considered and rejected as a *soundness* device: measured, every bound gate in `exploreMachine` — completion, depth, configs — sets `boundHit` before it `continue`s, so an edge into a dropped successor always implies `!exhaustive`, which already implies `!decreasingOk`. What ships beside the gate is one walk-level boolean, **`openFrontier`**, defined as **`!walk.exhaustive`** — *not* as `boundHit !== 'none'`, and the difference is a real bug the review caught. `exploreMachine` has an early return for a refused construct that sets `configs: 0`, `exhaustive: false` and `boundHit: 'none'`: no bound was hit because no walk happened. Reproduced from ordinary surface syntax — a transition with an unresolved endpoint gives `reach dangle.sysml → 0 configuration(s) explored … not explored — transition-without-endpoints`, so this is reachable from the corpus and not only through `attrs.parallel` via the API. Under the draft's definition that walk would publish `openFrontier: false` and, over an empty successor relation with no cycle, **`acyclic: true`** — a census field asserting a property of a graph that was never built, on the one machine class the tool refuses to walk, flipping lane G's release gate on a false reading. So: `openFrontier === !walk.exhaustive` is what the test asserts (the draft's `openFrontier === (boundHit !== 'none')` would have **pinned the bug**), and the census field `acyclic` is **three-valued** — `true | false | null`.

**And `acyclic` reads `null` whenever `walkIsExact` is false, not merely whenever `openFrontier` is true — which is the fourth review's correction and is the difference between a census and an artefact.** Acyclicity of the *retained* relation is precisely the property clause (d) manufactures: an edge the model states and `seedStore` never offered is a cycle-closing edge that is simply absent. Measured on the trapguard shape's two-state sibling — `attribute resetOk : Boolean;` with `transition nominal then degraded; transition degraded if resetOk then nominal;` — the retained relation is two nodes and the single edge 0→1, so **`acyclic` reads `true` on a machine whose stated relation has the cycle `nominal → degraded → nominal`**. Re-measured at `dc6662c` the printed report is `undetermined under {…} — 1 guard(s) the walk could not evaluate`, `1 of 2 transition(s) fired, dead withheld`, `boundHit none` — the *report* is withheld and the *relation* is unchanged, which is the whole point: `c929278` gates what is published and does not add the edge back. A10 is safe on its own (it conjoins the gate), but the census field is not A10 — it is published in `--json` as a fact about the machine and it is the number §5's table and **lane G's release gate** read, so a corpus machine that is acyclic only because a guard was unbound could flip lane G green and release §3.5a on a measurement of an artefact. §5's note that not one transition in this repository carries a guard is the same sentence read the other way: the first user model that does is also the first model that can flip this gate. **Lane G's release condition therefore counts only `acyclic === true` on a walk where `walkIsExact` holds**, and §3.P's test list asserts `acyclic: null` on the trapguard file and `acyclic: false` on its `= true` variant, with `acyclic: true` named as the wrong answer.

**The register.** Every claim in this plan whose truth is "nothing of this kind exists" is a row of data in `publishable.ts`, and `test/unit/semantics.mc.publishable.test.ts` asserts by reflection that each registered claim's producer is unreachable unless its condition holds. **Each row also carries its polarity** — `'decreasing' | 'increasing' | 'mixed' | 'not-a-walk'`, plus a free-form `polarityNote` — and the reflection test asserts the rule mechanically in the form stated above: *no `increasing` or `mixed` row may read `decreasingOk` unless `alsoRequires` names a conjunct covering the mechanism its note identifies.* That is how the polarity argument stops being prose. **`walkRequires` takes one of exactly four values and `alsoRequires` holds everything that is not one of them** (above), so the register is the wiring between the predicate and the claims and the reflection test checks the wiring rather than trusting the implementer to have read the right field. Any per-mechanism conjunction in `walkRequires` is a red test: the whole point of the collapse is that the increasing side has one gate and no menu.

The `Polarity` column below carries the enumerated value in bold and the note after it.

| # | Absence claim | Feature | Polarity | `walkRequires` | `alsoRequires` | Otherwise |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A0** | **`state X has no enabled outgoing transition and is not marked final`** (`verification/deadlock`) | `reach` (ships today, **gated today**) | **`mixed`** — *decreasing in the over-approximating mechanisms, increasing in the undecided-guard one: a missing edge invents this row* | `decreasingOk` | **no outgoing transition of the deadlocked leaf is in `walk.undeterminedGuards`** — the conjunct that excuses this row's `mixed` polarity, and the only such escape in the register | the row is **withheld** and the store sentence prints in its place. See the note below the table |

The store clause is not written as a condition here: this claim's polarity is not flipped by a missing edge. It is a disclosure plus §3.1's `--cover-required` refusal, and the exposure is a census field |
