| A15 | **`no in port fails condition 2`** | 7b | **`not-a-walk`** — *structural* | `null` | the census non-degenerate ∧ a non-empty DT | the feature is not built |

**A0 was the fourth review's finding, and the fifth review found that the tree had closed it first — so the row survives as a REGISTER ENTRY over shipped machinery, plus one strengthening.** `verification/deadlock` — *"no transition is enabled at this configuration"* — is a fifth absence claim `reach` publishes, and it was in neither register. It **used** to be emitted unconditionally: `reachOne` returned `deadlocks: walk.deadlocks` with no guard, in contrast to the neighbouring `unreachable` and `dead`. **`c929278` gated it**, and gated it on exactly the argument A0 was written from — the comment above the line says so: *"Gated on the guards ALONE, not on `publishable`. A deadlock row says one configuration the walk REACHED had no enabled edge out, and a bound cannot make that wrong … An undetermined guard CAN make it wrong: it is an edge out that may have been enabled, and nothing here decided whether it was."* The shipped expression is `deadlocks: guardsDecided ? walk.deadlocks : []`.

**So the pre-`c929278` measurement this row was built on no longer reproduces, and quoting it would send an implementer to write a fixture that cannot fail.** Re-executed at `dc6662c` on the file A0 named — `package P { state def Modes { attribute resetOk : Boolean; state nominal; state degraded; transition nominal then degraded; transition degraded if resetOk then nominal; } }` — `reach --json` gives `"exhaustive": false`, `"boundHit": "none"`, `"deadlocks": []`, `"suppressed": true`, and **one** finding, `verification/guard-undetermined`. No deadlock row, and no dead-transition row either. The pre-`c929278` transcript (`deadlocks ['P::Modes::degraded']`, codes `['verification/dead-transition','verification/deadlock']`, rc 0, with the shipped hint *"Give it a way out"* on a model that states one) is retained above only as the record of what the defect was.

**What is left for this plan is one strengthening, and it is stated unambiguously because the earlier wording contradicted itself.** The shipped gate is **walk-wise**: one undetermined guard anywhere in the machine empties the whole `deadlocks` array. A0 proposes to make it **also leaf-wise** — `decreasingOk ∧ (no outgoing transition of the deadlocked leaf is in `walk.undeterminedGuards`)` — so that an undecided guard in one region does not silence a genuine, fully-decided sink in another. That is a **strengthening of the row's availability, not a relaxation of its gate**: the walk-wise conjunct stays and the leaf-wise conjunct is added on top. The earlier phrasing said *"suppressed leaf-wise, not walk-wise"* in one clause and *"the walk-wise clause is the first conjunct"* in the next; read the first way it would have **regressed `c929278`**, and read the second way it is a no-op on every input this plan names. It is written as a conjunction here and nowhere else.

**And the fixture the row needs is one no earlier revision named**, because on the trapguard family the two gates agree: a machine with an undetermined guard in one region **and a real, fully-decided sink in another**. That is the only input on which the leaf-wise conjunct changes an output, and without it commit 3's A0 assertion passes vacuously. §5(d)'s two fixture machines that do carry a deadlock (`on`, `running`) carry no guard at all, so both gates are vacuously true on them and their goldens do not move; no shipped example carries a deadlock row, so the campaign's exact diagnostics array does not move either. **The motivating contradiction also has a sharper form now, and it survives `c929278`:** the succession probe of reading 5 invented a `verification/deadlock` with every guard clause vacuously true — which is what the census, not this row, is what closes.

**A9 is the odd one out, deliberately:** it is the only row whose condition is not a walk fact at all but a set of *global solver facts* (§3.3b). **A13 is the second odd one:** its two halves have opposite polarity, so it is the one row where the gate holds a report rather than a sentence — the `no cut set` half would survive without it, but the two print together and a reader cannot be asked to know which half is which.

**Why A7 names no product-search condition, stated so the register is not decorative.** §3.2b makes `recovery` a dispatch arm that never reaches `search`, and the commit's headline test asserts exactly that. With no search there is no `found`, so `Publishability.searchComplete` has **no value** on this path. The draft required it anyway, which is executable — the register is asserted by reflection — and so had two failure modes and no third: read as `false`, `recovery` on `examples/uav-isr.sysml` with `p = state standby` (an exhaustive four-configuration walk, `boundHit: 'none'`) publishes *"inconclusive: bound exhausted"* about a bound nobody hit and §3.2b's own `recoverable` test goes red; read as vacuously `true`, the register row is decoration and the reflection test passes on nothing. The collapse fixes this by construction: `Exactness` has no `searchComplete` field to get wrong. `searchComplete` stays **optional and absent** on `decreasingOk` for walk-only claims, asserted once in `semantics.mc.publishable.test.ts`.

**The second register: positive witnesses, because a refutation is a positive claim too — and an EXISTENTIAL one reads the RELATION half of the gate, not the whole of it, while the two that are maximality claims read all of it.** The draft registered absences and left every witness to prose, and the second review found the hole in three places at once: §3.1's two `covered` rows leave a walk with dwell labels whose witness fired **no** after-label matched by neither row, with no test that could go red either way; §3.R's `potential` names an avoiding **cycle** computed over the retained relation and is gated on nothing; and §3.5a's `fail` and §3.5b's `refuted under fairness` spend **exit 1** on a witness run — the existence of a `p`-free maximal run is monotone-**increasing** in the edge relation, which is the polarity §2.3 says the approximation falsifies. So `publishable.ts` carries a **second table**, `WITNESS_CLAIMS`, with the same shape and the same reflection assertion, and §5(a)'s "the polarity rule is machine-checked" becomes true of witnesses as well as absences.

**And the split of the gate is forced by measurement, not by taste.** A witness row must read `relationIsTheMachines` and must **not** read `seenWhole`, because **a bound can hide a witness and can never invent one** — while a wrong relation can invent one. Measured at this plan's own named boundary: `check-behaviour examples/uav-isr.sysml --element FlightModes --pattern "pattern=absence, scope=globally, p=state failsafe" --max-configs 3` (witness depth 3) gives rc=1 `FAIL` with the qualification `3 product state(s) explored — **partial under** {maxConfigs 3, …} — the configuration bound was reached`; at `--max-configs 4` the same row reads `exhaustive under`. `cover` reuses that identical breach, so a witness row that required `seenWhole` would make commit 6's own headline test (*"`--max-configs <depth>` keeps `covered`"*, §3.1) and §5(a)'s reflection test mutually unsatisfiable — one of them lands red, and the cheap repair under time pressure is to weaken the reflection test, which hollows out one of the two mechanisms §5 counts. §2.1 already rejected routing a bounded `covered` anywhere else, and §3.1 already says the `covered` row *"stands on a partial walk, for the same reason `fail` does"*. So the rule is stated once, positively, and asserted — **but it is scoped by an explicit column, because two of the six witness rows are not existential at all and the draft's unscoped assertion could not have been satisfied by its own register.** `WITNESS_CLAIMS` carries a **`kind`** column, `'existential' | 'maximality'`. An **existential** witness says *this run exists*; a bound can hide it and can never invent it, so such a row reads `relationIsTheMachines` and is **required NOT to read the bound family** — not `seenWhole`, not `decreasingOk`, not `Publishability.searchComplete` — and the reflection test asserts exactly that, scoped to `kind === 'existential'`. A **maximality** witness says *this cycle has no exit* or *this run has no successor*: an absence wearing a witness's clothes, falsified by a **missing** edge, which is what a bound and clause (d) both produce. Such a row reads the **whole gate**, `walkIsExact`, and its `kind` cell is what exempts it — the draft instead wrote a bare bound condition into two rows and then asserted, four times over, that no row had one. The `--max-configs <depth>` case lives in `semantics.mc.publishable.test.ts` as well as in commit 6's fixture list, so the next author cannot re-add a bound condition to an **existential** row without a red test.

**`ridesOn` is the second column, and it is what stops a row naming another row's gate.** Three witnesses are riders on an absence row rather than independent claims — W2 on A6, W5 on A11, W6 on A13 — and the draft spelled that by writing *"A6's gate"* into the `Required` cell, which is neither of the admissible values and is unassertable. `ridesOn` names the row; the reflection test resolves it and requires the rider's own `walkRequires` to be no weaker than what its host publishes under.

| # | Positive witness | Feature | `kind` | `walkRequires` | `ridesOn` | `alsoRequires` | Otherwise |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | the `covered` witness trace | 1 | **existential** | **`relationIsTheMachines`**, or the **per-step disjunction** below in its place | — | `found.violation !== null` | the **walk-admits re-wording** (§3.1), never suppression — a bound can hide a witness and can never invent one |
| W2 | a trap's `entry` path | 2a | **existential** | `relationIsTheMachines`, or the per-step disjunction | **A6** | — | the row is already suppressed with A6; this clause is what stops a per-claim relaxation of the gate being made silently (§3.2a) |
| W3 | **`potential` — the named avoiding cycle or full-relation sink** | R | **maximality** | **`walkIsExact`** — both halves, and the per-step disjunction does **not** substitute: the claim is that the cycle or sink has no exit, and a missing edge is exactly what invents one (§3.R's sink-scoping rule) | — | memoryless monitor ∧ configuration-valued atom | `not decided`, with the reason split by §3.R's `not decided` rows |
| W4 | **5a's `fail` — the refuting finite maximal run** | 5a | **maximality** | **`walkIsExact`** — a refutation of `F p` asserts the witness run *has no successors*, and a bound-dropped configuration is indistinguishable from a terminal one. The per-step disjunction may substitute for the **over-approximating** clauses only, never for `seenWhole` and never for clause (d): a dwell or a trigger the walk supplies can only **add** edges, which makes terminality harder to claim, while a bound and an undecided guard remove them, which manufactures it | — | `acyclic === true` | today's refusal, verbatim |
| W5 | **5b's `refuted under fairness` — the lasso** | 5b | **existential** | `relationIsTheMachines`, or the per-step disjunction over the prefix **and** the cycle | **A11** | — | `inconclusive` |
| W6 | each **published** cut set | 8 | **existential** | `relationIsTheMachines`, or the per-step disjunction | **A13** | — | `inconclusive`; A13's gate holds the whole report first, so in practice no cut set is published and no `no cut set` is printed (§3.8). As with W2, this row is what stops that gate being relaxed per claim without a sentence |

**The per-step disjunction, written once here and referenced by row — and the environment is deliberately NOT in it.** A witness may still publish on a walk whose relation is not the machine's, **provided no step of the witness was taken across a dwell transition and no step crossed a transition in `undeterminedGuards`**. **The first half is tested as `afterDuration(step.transition) !== undefined` on the step's transition id, never as a match against the recorded label** — `labelFor` (`config.ts`:617-621) records a completion step as `''`, so a numeric-`attrs.after` dwell is a step whose label is empty and a label-based test waves it through, which is the same defect the gate's clause (b) had and must not be re-introduced one row down. That is a per-trace check over the retained edges the trace used, not a walk-level one, and it is what makes W1's two `covered` rows partition rather than overlap. Its store half is satisfied by construction and stated anyway: an edge in `undeterminedGuards` was never offered, so no witness can have crossed one — the store cannot invent an existential witness, which is why the store gates the maximality rows and only discloses on the existential ones. When it fails, the witness is **re-worded** (§3.1, §3.2a, §3.8) or **withheld** (§3.R, §3.5a, §3.5b) — `WITNESS_CLAIMS` says which per row, and §2.4(c) or the store sentence prints beside whatever survives.

**A step that consumed a trigger is a different case and keeps the bare claim, with §2.4(a) beside it — stated here so the asymmetry is a decision and not an oversight.** A trace that fires `abort` **is** a run of the system, under an environment that supplies `abort`; §2.4(a)'s sentence names that environment, and the reader is told what the witness costs. A step under a dwell is not that: the interpreter fires `enabled[0]` at every advance granularity, so the trace is a run of **no** environment. A step across an undecided guard is not that either: the model states the edge and the walk never had it, so a witness that crossed one crossed an edge the walk manufactured from a `false` it should have read as unknown. That is why the environment mechanism gates the increasing **absence** side (clause (c), where "no escape exists" is falsified by a trigger nobody will send) and discloses on the **witness** side, while time and the store do both. The register carries the asymmetry as data, so it is checkable rather than argued: `WITNESS_CLAIMS`' per-step clause names two mechanisms and `Exactness.relationIsTheMachines` names three.

**Why the refutation direction is not free, which the draft asserted and the review broke.** §3.5a's warrant for leaving refutations ungated was: *any prefix of a `p`-free run is `p`-free, so a restricted environment either admits the same refuting run or stalls earlier.* That argument is about a **withheld trigger** — an environment that offers less. A dwell is not that. Under a dwell the interpreter does not stall; it takes a **different** edge, and that edge may satisfy the property. Measured shape: a machine built with `{ trigger: 'after(5)' } idle → done` declared before `{ trigger: 'after(60)' } idle → stuck`, `stuck` terminal. The alphabet is `["after(5)","after(60)"]`, the graph is acyclic, the walk is exhaustive and `boundHit` is `'none'` — `seenWhole` holds and only `relationIsTheMachines` fails — and `existence (state done) globally` finds the walk-only maximal run `idle → stuck` and prints `fail — refuted — finite maximal run of 1 step(s) on which \`state done\` never holds`, `verification/refuted`, **error, exit 1**, on a design where `runStateMachine` fires `enabled[0]` at every advance (`execute.ts`:1305-1308) and `matches` tests the dwell only on the `timeout` branch (`config.ts`:601-604), so `stuck` is entered on **no** run. 5b's lasso has the identical shape: the fair cycle may be composed entirely of dwell edges. Hence W4 and W5, and hence the re-worded transfer argument in §3.5a: it covers withheld triggers and **not** preempted dwells, and it says nothing at all about the store.

### 2.4 The four standing sentences

**(a) The environment sentence.** `exploreMachine` offers `{completion} ∪ alphabet` at *every* configuration (`explore.ts`:457-460, iterated at :483). Any witness that consumed a trigger, and any escape from a trap, is a claim under a maximally cooperative environment. Measured: `grep -rniE 'cooperative|offers every trigger|every trigger in the alphabet'` over `src/ scripts/ docs/` returns **zero** hits outside `docs/05`. **This sentence does not exist in the tool today** — it is new prose, written once as a shared constant and printed by 1, 2, 5 and 8 on every verdict line whose alphabet is non-empty:

> `the environment offered every trigger this machine names at every configuration, so a witness that consumes \`abort\` is a claim about that environment and not about one that withholds it`

**(b) The absence rule.** Every A-row above is suppressed, **never qualified**, when its condition fails. Two measured hazards attach:

- `search` **returns at the first violation** (`patterns.ts`:944-953), so `found.configs` is "product states seen up to the witness" and the code says so in its own words at :1223-1228. **No absence sentence may quote `found.configs` as a total.** Where a row's prose wants the configuration-graph count instead, it quotes a **named field** — `PropertyVerdict` gains `machineConfigs` (§3.1) — because `base` sets `configs: found.configs` unconditionally at :1189 and every verdict branch spreads it, so a row that says one number in JSON and another in prose publishes two unlabelled counts for one row.
- A configuration whose successors were all dropped at a bound looks, to Tarjan, exactly like a sink. Under `seenWhole` — the gate's first half, which every SCC-derived claim reads through `walkIsExact` — no such claim publishes from such a walk at all; `openFrontier` keeps the census honest as well. **This is not the same fact as `c929278`'s** (§0.1): a bound does **not** withhold a `verification/deadlock` row, because `recordDeadlock`'s subject is a configuration that was dequeued and offered every input and found nothing enabled, which a bound does not touch. The bound damages the **retained relation** (an edge into a dropped successor is not recorded), and that is what Tarjan reads. Two objects, two rules, and a §3 row that reasons about one may not quote the other.

**(c) The dwell sentence.** Printed on every verdict line whose walk has a non-empty `timedTransitions` — i.e. wherever the gate's clause (b) fails — beside whatever claim survives:

> `this walk advances no clock: a transition carrying an \`after(n)\` dwell is offered at every configuration — as a named event where the dwell is written as a trigger, as a completion where it is written as \`attrs.after\` — so a step taken across one is a step the interpreter may never take`

The second half of that sentence is the fourth reading of §2.3 made visible to the reader: the draft's wording said *"every `after(n)` label"*, which is a true sentence about the machines the draft measured and a false one about a machine whose dwells carry no label at all.

It is not a substitute for the gate. Where the claim is increasing the claim is **withheld** and this sentence says why; where the claim is a positive witness, `WITNESS_CLAIMS` says per row which of the two happens — the witness is **re-worded** at §3.1 (`covered`), §3.2a (a trap's `entry` path) and §3.8 (a published cut set), and it is **withheld** at §3.R (the named avoiding cycle or sink — the row the draft left ungated entirely), §3.5a (the refuting finite maximal run) and §3.5b (the lasso). In every case this sentence stands beside whatever survives. The two hazards are different and the disclosure must not be asked to do the suppression's job — which is exactly the error §2.4(a) would have made if it had been left to cover this case, since a dwell is not a trigger an environment can withhold or offer out of order. **The store has the third sentence of this family**, printed by `boundsSentence` on every bounds line in every lane (§2.3): `store seeded from declared literal values — a guard over an attribute with no declared value is read as false`.

**(d) The simulator sentence — what the exactness gate does NOT cover, and the reason it must print on every increasing claim.** **Every increasing claim in this plan is a claim about the MACHINE's semantics and never a claim about what the shipped simulator does.** That sentence is the standing one; the rest of this paragraph is why it has to be printed rather than assumed. `walkIsExact` establishes that the retained relation is the **machine's**. It establishes nothing at all about what the shipped interpreter does with that machine, and the two differ on every nondeterministic choice point: the walk explores both branches of a declaration-order tie-break and `runStateMachine` fires `enabled[0]` (`execute.ts`:1300-1308). Measured, this is not a corner case — `verification/nondeterministic-choice` reads **1** on `UAVSurveillanceSystem::FlightModes` (at `…::autonomous`, `event=""`, enabled `autonomous→manual` and `autonomous→failsafe`, taken `→manual`, notTaken `→failsafe`) and **1** on `VehicleModel::VehicleStates` (at `…::idling`, `event=""`, enabled `idling→moving` and `idling→off`, taken `→moving`, notTaken `→off`). On **two of the three shipped machines**, every increasing claim this plan publishes is therefore a claim about the machine's semantics and not about the simulator's. So, printed once as a shared constant beside every increasing claim and every published witness, whenever `nondeterministic > 0` **or a nondeterminism row was withheld under `c929278`'s rule** (§0.1 — a row is withheld when an undetermined guard sits strictly inside the level where the choice is reported, and a trigger written as *"the count is non-zero"* would drop the disclosure on exactly the walks that least resemble the simulator):

> `every claim above is about this MACHINE's semantics — the walk explores both branches of a nondeterministic choice; the simulator takes the first declared one, so a run this verdict quantifies over may be a run \`simulate\` never produces`

It is deliberately **not** gated away. Correction 13 already forbids deriving a *verdict* from the tie-break — that would print a claim about the simulator as a claim about the model — and this sentence is the mirror discipline: the verdict is about the model, and the reader is told so rather than left to assume the two agree. Where §3.R's `potential` names a cycle and §3.5a's `fail` names a run, the same sentence prints, because those are the two rows a reader is most likely to try to reproduce with `simulate`.

### 2.5 The census rule

`mc-scope`'s standing rule — *a feature that cannot report on its own usefulness is not finished* — ships as a **field in a report, not a note**. Every census is `--json`-only, costs nothing on the text path, and lands **inside** the existing payload object, never beside it: `test/campaign/cli.sysprose.test.ts` pins the top-level key lists (`['file','ok','reach']`, `['connectivity','file','ok']`) and a census at the top level breaks them on contact. §5 is the single table of what each census retires.

**And a HELD feature owes a census in the same way, but it does not write one: it is retired or released by the census of the commit that gates it.** §3.4b reads commit 12's, §3.7b reads commit 14's, §3.5a reads commit 3's `acyclic`, §3.6 reads commit 19's pair count — which is why those four subsections carry a **Release condition** where a shipped feature carries a **Census**, and why each of those release conditions names the field and the number it is read against rather than a judgement. §3.8 was the exception and it was an oversight: it is HELD behind commit 21's census *and* ships a permanent vocabulary commitment, so it now carries both, and §5's table has the row for commit 23 that the draft never wrote.

### 2.6 Reserved words, and what the guard does **not** catch

Measured in `test/unit/claims.test.ts`: `RESERVED_FORMS[1]` bans `deadlock-free` and `realizable` as **bare words with no allowance at all**. Consequence for 5b: Baier & Katoen's fair-continuation condition may never be called by its book name; it is written here as *the fair-continuation condition*, and its verdict says *"the declared assumption has a fair continuation from every reachable configuration"*.

**And the guard is narrower than three of the input analyses assumed.** `SUBJECT` (:303) is `sysprose|(the|this|that|these|those|our) (tools|models|properties|requirements|obligations|proofs|clauses|verdicts|results|architectures|systems)`. It does **not** contain *machine*, *design*, *state*, *run* or *cut set*, and no form mentions `guaranteed`, `covered`, `recoverable`, `no cut set` or `no simulation relation`. So:

- *"the machine is safe to enter failsafe"* would **pass** the guard today.
- `guaranteed` and `every run is guaranteed to …` **pass** it today.

**And the guard, as it is written, CANNOT RESERVE A NEGATIVE PHRASE AT ALL.** This is the third of the three findings that gate the build, and it was confirmed by running the guard's own scanner. `scanDocs` calls `negated(before, m[0])` at `claims.test.ts`:157, and `negated` (:100-102) returns true when `NEGATION.test(matched)` — `NEGATION` (:93) being `/\b(not|never|no|non|without|isn't|aren't|nor|neither)\b/i`. Any form whose **matched span** contains `no`, `not` or `never` is defused unconditionally, before the allowance window is even consulted. Executed on planted sentences:

```
"This design has no cut set."                    -> 0 hits   (defused)
"The design is not covered."                     -> 0 hits   (defused)
"There is no simulation relation between them."  -> 0 hits   (defused)
"The machine is recoverable."                    -> 1 hit
"The machine is guaranteed."                     -> 1 hit
```

So two of the four forms the draft promised, and the negative half of a third, are **dead on arrival** — and the claims they would have covered are exactly the ABSENCE claims (`no cut set`, `no simulation relation`, `not covered`) that hard constraint 1 and §1 name as the single way this lane starts lying. Worse than a gap: the draft's own `it('… asserted to fire')` test would either go red on landing or be quietly rewritten to pass on a positive stand-in, and then §5(c) counts a guard that covers nothing as one of two mechanisms.

**Commit 4 therefore changes `negated()` itself, and that is the point of the commit.** `RESERVED_FORMS`' entry shape (`claims.test.ts`:328, `{what, pattern, allow}`) gains a fourth optional field, `negativeIsTheClaim?: true`; the call site at :157 passes the form and skips the **in-span** half of the defusal for those entries, keeping the look-back half (`NEGATION` in `before`) for every form, so *"we do not claim there is no cut set"* is still defused by the words in front of it.

**And then the tree was measured with that change in place, before any form was written — which is what decided the forms' shape, and which contradicts the draft's own promise.** The draft said each new form is "measured at **0** pre-existing hits over the tree first". Executed against the real tree — 698 scanned files, the shipped `SKIP_FILES` applied, the look-back defusal kept and only the in-span half skipped — the **bare phrases** are not at 0:

```
bare  no cut set              -> 23 hits
bare  not covered             ->  3 hits
bare  recoverable             ->  2 hits
bare  no simulation relation  ->  0 hits
```

Nine of the 23 are in `src/semantics/fault-tree.ts`, and **two of those are the command's own shipped verdict strings** — :859 `` `${input.events} basic event(s); no cut set was found ${input.bound} and ` `` and :861 `` `… no cut set ${input.bound} — ${ORDERS_NOT_EXPLORED_NOTE}` `` — i.e. the honest, bounded absence A14 that this plan's register keeps. The rest are prose about the sentence (`sysprose-spec.ts`:45/:215, `sysprose.ts`:2444/:2446, `refinement.ts`:2619/:2660, `USER-GUIDE.md`:1411, `cli-reference.test.ts`:419, `verification.test.ts`:3641/:3931/:3933/:3960) and innocent uses elsewhere (`CONFORMANCE.md`:11 *"states, candidly, what is and is not covered"*, `metaclasses.ts`:258, `AGENT-AUTHORING-CAMPAIGN.md`:2511, `analytics.ts`:896 and `metamodel.ts`:15's *"recoverable from"*). A bare-phrase form lands **red on arrival** with 28 violations, and the only allowance that clears :859/:861 is one matching `no cut set (was found|up to order)` — which defuses precisely the sentence the form was added to reserve. Reserving the bare phrase fails in one direction or the other, and there is no third setting.

**So commit 4 scopes the new forms rather than landing a triage list, and the scoped forms are measured at 0.** That is the choice, and it is the one the guard's own design already makes — `RESERVED_FORMS` reserves *sentence forms, not words*, and every form that ships today is subject-scoped for exactly this reason. Re-measured with `negativeIsTheClaim` in place, over the same 698 files:

| Reserved form (commit 4) | Allowance | Pre-existing hits | Planted sentence it fires on | Planted sentence it leaves alone |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SUBJECT COPULA GAP guaranteed` \| `guaranteed to (reach\|enter\|hold\|terminate)` | — | **0** | *"The machine is guaranteed."*, *"This design is guaranteed to reach failsafe."* | — |
| `SUBJECT COPULA GAP (covered\|not covered\|uncovered)` | — | **0** | *"The design is not covered."* | `CONFORMANCE.md`:11's *"what is and is not covered"* |
| `SUBJECT COPULA GAP recoverable` \| `(can\|will) always recover` | — | **0** | *"The machine is recoverable."* | `analytics.ts`:896's *"recoverable from"* |
| `(SUBJECT (has\|have\|had\|contains\|holds) \| there (is\|are)) no cut sets?` \| `SUBJECT COPULA GAP (free of cut sets\|cut-set-free)` | `/up to order/i` | **0** | *"This design has no cut set."*, *"There is no cut set."* | *"The design has no cut set up to order 2."*, and `fault-tree.ts`:859/:861 |
| `no simulation relation` | `/over the common labels/i` | **0** | *"There is no simulation relation between them."* | §3.6's printed *"no simulation relation exists over the common labels {idle, active}: the pair … fails"* |

Each row was executed both ways in this session: **0** hits over the tree, and the planted sentence asserted to fire. **Widening `SUBJECT` with `machines?|designs?|state machines?|runs?` costs 0 additional hits** on the three subject-scoped forms that ship today — verified/correct/safe 0 → 0, proved 0 → 0, consistent 0 → 0 — so the widening is free and is measured to be, exactly as the `GAP` widening was.

**What this buys, stated exactly, so §5(c) is not overclaimed.** The guard now reserves the **sentence a reader would quote** for all five pieces of new vocabulary — that is the prose half, and it is real: *"the machine is recoverable"*, *"this design has no cut set"* and *"the design is not covered"* each go red on landing after commit 4. It does **not** reserve the verdict strings themselves, which carry no subject (`not covered under {…}`, `no cut set up to order 2`) and are pinned byte-identically by mechanism (b) instead. That division is the honest one and it is written into §5(c) rather than left to be discovered: no claim in this plan is covered by fewer than two mechanisms, and for these five the two are the register (a) and the fixture (b), with the sentence guard (c) covering the prose.

Commit 4 adds these five forms **before** any feature that could print them. A form that is planted and does not fire is a red test in commit 4, not a review comment in commit 23. This plan does not lean on a guard that would not fire, and it does not claim a measurement it did not take.

| Lane | Ships | Name | Question | CLI | Engine | Verdict it can give |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 1–5 | spine | — | — | TS | *no verdict; no printed change* |
| B | 6 | `cover` | Can this design ever get into the state I care about? | `check-behaviour … --pattern "pattern=cover, …" [--cover-required]` | TS | covered · not covered · vacuous · inconclusive |

| C | 9 | `recovery` | Can it always get back to the state I name? | `check-behaviour … --pattern "pattern=recovery, …"` | TS | recoverable · not recoverable · vacuous · inconclusive |

### 3.P — Retain the successor relation `exploreMachine` already computes

**What it decides.** Nothing. It is an engine change with no report, no diagnostic and no verdict, and that is why it is its own commit: its gate is *"`reach`'s output did not move"*, and that gate proves something only when nothing else moved with it.

**Command and flags.** None.

**Engine.** In-process TypeScript. **Zero new compute** — every edge is already produced by `stepConfig` at `explore.ts`:509 and discarded at :512.

**The change, ~12 lines inside `exploreMachine`.** Every row below names the construct it edits; the positions are found by reading `exploreMachine` top to bottom, and the order of the rows is the order they appear in it.

| Site | Today | After |
|---|---|---|
| the `seen` declaration | `const seen = new Set<string>([hashConfig(opening.config)])` | `new Map<string, number>([[hash, 0]])` — indexed by BFS discovery order |
| the opening `queue.push` | `queue.push({config, depth, completionRun})` | `+ index: 0`; `Frontier` gains `readonly index: number` |
| — | — | `const succ: number[][] = [[]];` beside `seen` |
| **the revisit prune** | `if (seen.has(key)) continue;` | `const known = seen.get(key); if (known !== undefined) { succ[here.index].push(known); continue; }` — **the edge is recorded before the prune, then the prune happens exactly as before.** This is the one line the feature's honesty turns on |
| the three bound gates (completion, depth, configs) | `boundHit = …; continue;` | unchanged — **no edge is recorded into a dropped target**; `openFrontier` is derived from `walk.exhaustive`, **not** from `boundHit` (§2.3) |
| the admission point — `seen.add(key); queue.push(…)` | — | `const to = seen.size; seen.set(key, to); succ.push([]); succ[here.index].push(to); queue.push({…, index: to});` |
| the config-bound **test** | `if (seen.size >= bounds.maxConfigs)` | unchanged — `Map.size` is `Set.size`; the `boundHit` assignment beside it is unchanged too |
| the walk's result assembly | `machineAlphabet` returns labels only | unchanged; `exploreMachine` additionally records **`timedTransitions`**, the walkable transitions with `afterDuration(tr) !== undefined` — **clause (b)'s producer, the TRANSITION set and not an alphabet subset** (§2.3: a numeric `attrs.after` with no trigger is a dwell that contributes no label, and an alphabet-scoped version reads empty on a machine made entirely of them) — and `timedLabels`, the labels those transitions did contribute, which gates **no** clause and exists only to split the environment sentence from the time one |
| ~~`guardHoldsStore` and its caller~~ | **NOT EDITED. Clause (d)'s producer already ships.** `stepCandidates` records every guard it consulted and could not evaluate, and `ExploreResult.undeterminedGuards` is that list (`c929278`, §0.1) | nothing. A second, narrower field under a second name is the drift `config.ts`'s header exists to prevent, and it would read empty on `not mode` over a fully valued `mode` |
| `boundsSentence` | names `maxConfigs`, `maxDepth`, `maxCompletion`, `alphabet` | += `store seeded from declared literal values — a guard over an attribute with no declared value is read as false`, **appended AFTER the `alphabet …` clause**, which is not a style choice: the campaign asserts a **prefix** that stops before `alphabet` and asserts the substring `alphabet no named trigger` separately, so appending keeps both green while inserting earlier reddens the first. **In commit 3, not commit 2** — every qualification string in every lane moves with it, so it cannot land in the one commit whose entire gate is *"`reach`'s output did not move"*. It is a deliberate re-record in commit 3, and it takes the **hand-pasted `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` transcripts that print this string in full** with it, which are found by grepping the guide for `exhaustive under {maxConfigs` and not by line number — measured at `dc6662c` there are two, one under the `reach` walkthrough (`4 configuration(s) explored, depth 3 — …`) and one under the `check-behaviour` walkthrough (`3 product state(s) explored — …`) — neither machine-checked by any `docs-counts` claim, which is exactly why a Files list that says *"every qualification string in every lane"* and then names only `CONFORMANCE.md` leaves them publishing output the tool no longer produces |

`ExploreResult` — thirteen fields at `dc6662c` — gains **six** `readonly` fields. **Clause (d)'s producer is not among them:** `undeterminedGuards` is already there, and a seventh field duplicating it is what the fifth review found and what this list no longer asks for.

```ts
  /** Successor indices, in BFS discovery order. No edge points at a
   *  configuration a bound refused — that configuration is not a node. */
  successors: readonly (readonly number[])[];
  /** The ACTIVE STACK per index — not the leaf. One state spans many
   *  configurations and a composite sits on the stack of every configuration
   *  inside it, so `stack.includes(p)` is the correct target predicate for a
   *  `state` atom, for recovery and for simulation labelling. */
  configStates: readonly (readonly ElementId[])[];
  /** The ACTIVE LEAF per index — `leafOf(config)`, `null` where there is none.
   *  One integer array beside `configStates`, and it is NOT redundant with it:
   *  `atomHolds` reads a `state` atom as `obs.config.stack.includes(...)` and a
   *  `node` atom as `leafOf(obs.config) === ...` (`atoms.ts`:341-345), and
   *  `nodesOf` (:137-151) admits every StateUsage in the machine, composites
   *  included, so the two readings DIVERGE on any composite state. Retaining
   *  both is what lets §3.2b and §3.R read `p` through `atomHolds`'s own
   *  selection instead of a hand-written predicate that silently picks one. */
  configLeaves: readonly (ElementId | null)[];
  /** This walk did not see the graph whole. Equals `!exhaustive`, asserted —
   *  NOT `boundHit !== 'none'`, which reads `false` on the unsupported-construct
   *  early return at :440-455 (`configs: 0, exhaustive: false, boundHit: 'none'`)
   *  and would let the acyclicity CENSUS publish `acyclic: true` over an empty
   *  relation on the one machine class this engine refuses to walk. */
  openFrontier: boolean;
  /** Every walkable transition of this machine for which `afterDuration` is
   *  defined — a numeric `attrs.after` OR an `after(n)` trigger string, and
   *  NOT only the ones that reached the alphabet. Empty is the exactness
   *  gate's clause (b). Non-empty means every escape, every recovery and
   *  every witness in this walk may be an artefact of a dwell the interpreter
   *  never grants (§2.3, mechanism 1). Measured: a transition carrying only
   *  `attrs.after` is a COMPLETION transition to `isCompletion` and
   *  contributes nothing to the alphabet, so the draft's alphabet-scoped
   *  version of this field read empty on a machine all of whose edges are
   *  dwells. */
  timedTransitions: ReadonlySet<ElementId>;
  /** The alphabet members those transitions contributed. Gates NOTHING. Its
   *  only reader is the `alphabet ∖ timedLabels` subtraction that decides
   *  whether a non-empty alphabet is real triggers (`failedClause` reads
   *  'environment') or all dwells ('time') — §3.R rows 5-6, §3.5a rows 2-3. */
  timedLabels: ReadonlySet<string>;

  //  NOT ADDED — clause (d)'s producer already exists on this interface:
  //    undeterminedGuards: readonly UndeterminedGuardRow[];
  //  shipped at c929278, populated by `stepCandidates`, and already read by
  //  `reachOne` as the fifth publishability conjunct. The gate reads
  //  `walk.undeterminedGuards.length === 0`. Do not add a narrower ElementId
  //  set beside it: the shipped predicate is "consulted and decided nothing",
  //  which is WIDER than "no declared value" and catches `not mode` over a
  //  fully valued `mode` — the machine on which the narrow reading publishes
  //  a trap over a missing edge with the gate green.
  //
  //  NOT ADDED, for the same reason:
  //    census: EdgeCensus;
  //  shipped at a66ba1f. §2.3 reads it; nothing here produces it.
```

**Clause (c) needs no new field**: it reads `bounds.alphabet`, which is already computed, already published on every bounds line, and already `[]` on every machine in this tree (§5(d)).

The census field `acyclic` (commit 3) is **three-valued** — `true | false | null` — and reads `null` whenever **`walkIsExact` is false**, which subsumes `openFrontier` and adds the three clauses that matter here: an undecided guard is a cycle-closing edge the walk never had, so a walk that is exhaustive and inexact can report `acyclic: true` about a machine whose stated relation has a cycle (§2.3, measured). Lane G's release condition counts only `acyclic === true` **on a walk where `walkIsExact` holds**.

**Never** export a `Map` keyed on the configuration hash. `hashConfig` (`config.ts`:677-696) builds `regionId|stack|dwell|store|history` from element ids, ids are fresh per load (`docs/04` D3), and the `FlightModes` hash is 114 characters of them. Intern to integers in BFS discovery order, which depends only on model structure because `hashConfig` sorts store and history by name. **No hash string may reach a report, a digest or a JSON payload.**

**The one line that decides whether this feature lies.** Recording at the admission point (:537) instead of at the prune (:512) yields the **BFS spanning tree** — `nodes − 1` edges, acyclic by construction on every machine in this repository — and Tarjan over it names `{failsafe}` a bottom SCC on `examples/uav-isr.sysml`. The two pruned edges on `FlightModes` are precisely the cycle-closing ones (`autonomous → manual`, `failsafe → standby`). This is the exact failure direction `docs/04` §6 forbids, and it is one line away.

**Verdict vocabulary.** None.

**MUST NEVER.** Emit anything. The moment this commit adds one diagnostic, `test/campaign/cli.sysprose.test.ts`:3175-3177 — `expect(body.reach.diagnostics.map(d => d.code)).toEqual(['verification/nondeterministic-choice'])`, an **exact array** — moves, and the differential that is the whole point of the commit stops proving anything. That assertion is the intended trip-wire, not a nuisance.

**Exit-code contract.** Unchanged everywhere.

**Tests.**
- NEW `test/unit/semantics.mc.successors.test.ts`: on `FlightModes`, `successors` is the **5-edge** relation over 4 nodes — `standby→manual`, `manual→autonomous`, `autonomous→manual`, `autonomous→failsafe`, `failsafe→standby`; on `examples/vehicle.sysml` 3 nodes / 4 edges; on `views-tour` 2 / 2.
- **The named negative test:** a fixture asserting that recording at the admission point yields 4 nodes / 3 edges, so the regression has a name in the suite rather than a comment.
- `--max-configs 2` on `uav-isr`: `successors.flat().every(i => i < configs)`, and `openFrontier === true`.
- **The unsupported-construct fixture, which is the second named negative test.** A `.sysml` file with a transition whose endpoint does not resolve — reproduced: `reach dangle.sysml` prints `0 configuration(s) explored … not explored — transition-without-endpoints` — asserts `configs: 0`, `exhaustive: false`, `boundHit: 'none'` **and `openFrontier === true`**. The assertion is `openFrontier === !walk.exhaustive`; writing it as `openFrontier === (boundHit !== 'none')` would pin the bug, so the wrong spelling is named here for the reviewer.
- `timedTransitions` holds both dwell transitions on the two-dwell fixture and `timedLabels` is `{"after(5)","after(10)"}` — **built through `ModelFactory` in this test file, because `accept after(n)` does not parse (correction 22)** — and both are **empty** on every machine in the tree (§5(d)), so clause (b) holds everywhere the notation reaches today and costs nothing on any model a CLI command can load.
- **The numeric-`attrs.after` fixture, which is the test the draft's clause (b) could not have passed.** A machine whose only timed transitions carry `attrs.after` and no trigger — written `m.create('TransitionUsage', {attrs:{after: n}})`, since `ModelFactory.transition` drops the field (`factory.ts`:98-113) — asserts `afterDuration` defined and `isCompletion` **true** on each, `bounds.alphabet === []`, `timedLabels` **empty**, **`timedTransitions` non-empty**, and **`walkIsExact === false` with `failedClause === 'time'`**. The wrong answers are named: `walkIsExact === true`, and downstream of it §3.2b's `recoverable` at exit 0 and §3.2a's `no component of this walk is inescapable beyond its endings` on a machine whose `{failsafe, failsafeHold}` is absorbing under `enabled[0]`.
- **`undeterminedGuards`, from both sides, and unlike the dwell fixture this one is a `.sysml` file.** The trapguard model — `attribute resetOk : Boolean;` with `transition nominal then degradedA; transition degradedA then degradedB; transition degradedB then degradedA; transition degradedA if resetOk then nominal;` — loads clean and, re-measured at `dc6662c`, walks `3 configuration(s) explored, depth 2 — undetermined under {…} — 1 guard(s) the walk could not evaluate`, `3 of 4 transition(s) fired, dead withheld`, rc=0. **`walk.undeterminedGuards` already holds exactly the `if resetOk` transition on it — that is `c929278`'s field and this plan asserts it rather than building it.** Both literal variants assert it **empty**: `= true` with the escape edge present, `= false` with the escape edge absent-but-decided (`3 of 4 transition(s) fired, 1 dead`, `dead degradedA -> nominal`, `exhaustive under {…}`). The trio is what shows the field tracks *decidedness* and not the presence of a guard, and it is the only fixture set in this plan that separates clause (d) from a narrower "no declared value" reading — for which the fourth member is `attribute mode : Integer = 3;` with `transition degradedA if not mode then nominal`, measured `"undeterminedGuards": [{ … "guard": "not mode", "unresolved": [] }]`: a **fully valued** feature, an empty `unresolved`, and the guard undetermined all the same. Measured baseline: `undeterminedGuards` is empty on every machine in the tree, because **not one transition in this repository carries a guard at all** (`grep -c ' if '` is 0 in every one of the six source files) — which is exactly why this fixture is the only coverage clause (d) will ever have. **And the `acyclic` census is asserted on the same file's two-state sibling**, because that is where the artefact shows: `transition nominal then degraded; transition degraded if resetOk then nominal;` walks `2 configuration(s) explored, depth 1 — exhaustive`, `1 of 2 transition(s) fired, 1 dead`, and asserts **`acyclic: null`** with **`acyclic: true` named as the wrong answer**; the `= true` variant asserts **`acyclic: false`**, because the escape edge closes the cycle the walk could not see. That pair is what stops lane G's release gate being flipped green by a machine that is acyclic only because a guard was unbound.
- **The alphabet baseline, asserted rather than assumed:** `bounds.alphabet` is `[]` on all eleven machines in the tree (`grep -c accept` is 0 in every source file), so clause (c) is satisfied everywhere today; the latch model — `state nominal; state locked; transition nominal then locked; transition locked accept unlatch then nominal;`, also a clean-loading `.sysml` file, measured `alphabet unlatch` — asserts a non-empty alphabet and is the fixture every clause-(c) refusal in §3 is written against.
- **The parse fact itself is a test, so it cannot rot back in as an assumption:** a `.sysml` source carrying `transition idle accept after(5) then done;` is asserted to fail the parse with the `no-viable-alt` at the `(`, and the escaped spelling `accept 'after(5)'` is asserted to yield `attrs.trigger === "'after(5)'"` with `afterDuration` returning `undefined`. If a future grammar change makes `after(n)` writable, this is the assertion that goes red and tells the next author to re-price the time clause's census.
- `configStates[i]` is the full stack **and `configLeaves[i]` is the leaf**, asserted on the composite-state fixture where the two differ: `state nominal; state degraded { state sub1; state sub2; transition sub1 -> sub2; } transition nominal -> degraded;`. The same fixture pins the two readings apart at the atom level, because that is where a hand-written predicate goes wrong — measured on the same walk, one atom spelled two ways: `--pattern "pattern=absence, scope=globally, p=node degraded"` → **PASS**, `` `node degraded` never holds, over the whole run ``; `p=state degraded` → **FAIL** with a 1-step witness. The test asserts `configStates` explains the second and `configLeaves` explains the first, and that both agree with `atomHolds` (`atoms.ts`:341-345) on every configuration of the walk.
- **The differential:** `reachReport(model)` is deep-equal before and after on all six examples, by structural comparison in the same file. `test/unit/semantics.mc.reach.test.ts` (26 tests) and the campaign reach block are **re-run untouched**; any movement is a defect, not a re-record.

**Cost.** Compute zero; memory one integer pair per innermost-level `stepConfig` call — measured 5 on `uav-isr`, 4 on `vehicle`, 2 on `views-tour`. Code ~12 lines plus **six** `ExploreResult` fields, of which one (`configLeaves`) is the third review's addition and a plain integer array, and one (`timedTransitions`) is the fourth's. The seventh field earlier revisions listed here — a re-derived undetermined-guard set — is **not built**: `c929278` shipped it. `reachReport` on `uav-isr` is 3.0 ms warm today and gains one array push per existing call.

**Census.** `successors.length`, `openFrontier`, **`timedTransitions.size`**, `timedLabels.size`, **`alphabet.length`**, **`walk.undeterminedGuards.length`** and **`walk.census.counts`** — the last two already produced, recorded here rather than computed — plus the existing `boundHit`, per machine, which makes commit 3's acyclicity census, lane C's trap census and — new here — **the exactness census** free. The last is the number §5's table reads: how many machines in a corpus `walkIsExact` would publish about at all. Measured on this tree it is **5 of 6** (the sixth is the unsupported-construct fixture, already suppressed), and the three clauses that could ever refuse are at 0 across the board: 0 dwell transitions, 0 named triggers, 0 guards. **`timedTransitions.size` is priced honestly, not as the draft priced it.** The draft offered it as "how many machines in a user's corpus this plan can make an increasing-polarity claim about", which correction 22 makes impossible: on every model any CLI command can load it is **0 by construction**, because no `.sysml` file can carry a timed transition. What it actually measures is **API traffic** — machines built through `ModelFactory` or carrying `attrs.after`, which is where the SDK's own callers live. So the census is recorded per machine and read as an SDK-population number, and the retirement question it answers is *"does anything but our own test suite build a timed machine?"* — not *"is the time clause a wall for users?"*, whose answer is already known and is **no**.

---

### 3.1 `cover` — an existential reachability property with a witness

**What it decides.** Can this design *ever* get into the situation I care about — and did my safety property pass because the machine cannot get anywhere interesting? Manna–Pnueli's **Guarantee** class (`◇β`): not finitely refutable, which is why the bad-prefix engine correctly refuses `existence`; finitely **witnessable**, which is why the same engine can confirm it.

**Why it is a gap and not a rename, measured on both polarities.** A probe machine that *can* reach `failsafe` and one that cannot, against the only expressible spelling of the intent:

- `--pattern "pattern=absence, scope=globally, p=state failsafe"` on the reachable machine → **rc=1**, `1 fail`, `verification/refuted`, **error** severity. The design that satisfies the intent is red.
- The same pattern on the sealed machine → **rc=0**, `1 pass`. The design that violates the intent is green.
- `pattern=existence` on the reachable machine → **rc=2**, `verification/behaviour-unsupported-construct`, *"there is no flag that lowers it"*.

**Command and flags.**
```
npm run sysprose -- check-behaviour <file> --element REF \
    [--pattern "pattern=cover, scope=globally, p=state failsafe"] [--cover-required]
```
`--cover-required` is a **flag**, never a subcommand: `COMMANDS.length` stays 22, so `test/unit/readme.test.ts`:307 (`APP_DOORS.length === COMMANDS.length + 1`), the README capability table and the two `docs-counts` subcommand claims all stay still. The carrier form needs no new *metaclass* — `resolveProperty` reads `pattern.fields ++ scope.fields` generically at `patterns.ts`:539-551 and the five atoms need no work:
`@SysproseVerification::PropertyPattern { attribute pattern = "cover"; attribute scope = "globally"; attribute p = "state failsafe"; }`

**But the VALUE is a forward-compatibility commitment, and it is priced here rather than left unsaid.** The carrier shape is unchanged; the string `"cover"` is persisted in a user's `.sysml` file. Any build before commit 6 reads it through the same generic cell reader (`patterns.ts`:460-475) and then refuses it in `resolveProperty` as `verification/malformed-property` (:499-502), which the docstring at :505-509 says makes the property **inconclusive** — reproduced today: **rc=2** on a model that is green on the new build. That is the same direction and the same permanence as an added evidence-record field, so hard constraint 6's reasoning applies to a persisted enum value as much as to a `metadata def`. It is recorded in `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` beside the two `metadata def` commitments, in the commit that spends it. The alternative — making `cover` a `--pattern`-only feature with no carrier value — is **rejected explicitly**: it would make the one pattern class an author cannot write into the model the one class the model cannot be re-checked on, and `PropertyPattern` exists so a property travels with the file. §3.2b's `"recovery"` carries the identical row.

**Engine.** In-process TypeScript, no solver, and **no dependency on P** (correction 2). `search` is unchanged. The monitor breach for `cover` is byte-for-byte `absence`'s (`breach = v.p === true`), so `monitorStep` (:688-794) gains one `case` falling to the same body; all the work is in `checkProperty`'s verdict tail (:1200-1299), re-reading the same result.

**Catalogue placement, and the one ordering rule.** `PatternClass` gains `'guarantee'`; the `PATTERNS` entry inserts **before `existence`**, because `test/unit/cli-reference.test.ts` asserts `PATTERNS.filter(p => p.kind === 'liveness').map(p => p.name)` equals `PATTERN_NAMES.slice(-2)` with the message *"the liveness pair moved"* — appending breaks it by construction, and inserting before `existence` keeps it green with no edit, which is the point. `PATTERN_NAMES` (`sysprose-spec.ts`) is a hand-copy kept free of model imports and is compared for **order** in the same file; that comparison does move, in this commit.

**Verdict vocabulary it MAY print, with the precise condition for each.**

| Claim | Sentence | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| `covered` | `covered — witness trace of N step(s), a run this semantics admits` + the environment sentence of §2.4(a) when the witness consumed a trigger + §2.4(d) when the machine has a choice point | **W1**, an **existential** row. `found.violation !== null` ∧ (**`relationIsTheMachines` ∨ the per-step disjunction of §2.3 — no step of the trace was taken across a transition for which `afterDuration` is defined, tested on the step's transition id and not on its recorded label, and no step crossed a transition in `undeterminedGuards`; a step that consumed a trigger keeps this row and prints §2.4(a) beside it**). **It reads no member of the bound family — not `seenWhole`, not `decreasingOk`, not `Publishability.searchComplete` — and that is asserted rather than left implicit** (§2.3): it **stands on a partial walk**, for the same reason `fail` does (`patterns.ts`:1214-1218) — a bound can hide a run and can never invent one — and measured, `--max-configs 3` on the 3-step `uav-isr` witness prints `partial under {maxConfigs 3, …}` and still publishes the row |
| `covered` | `covered — witness trace of N step(s), a run this WALK admits: step 2 fires \`after(10)\`, a dwell this engine offers as a named event without advancing a clock, so the interpreter may never take this trace` + §2.4(c) | `found.violation !== null` ∧ **some step of the trace was taken across a dwell transition (`afterDuration(step.transition) !== undefined`) or crossed a transition in `undeterminedGuards`**. The claim survives; the warrant is re-worded to the one the walk actually has, naming the mechanism the step used — a dwell (§2.4(c)) or a guard read against an undecided store (the store sentence) |
| `not-covered` | `not covered under {maxConfigs 10000, maxDepth 200, maxCompletion 64, alphabet …, store seeded from declared literal values — a guard over an attribute with no declared value is read as false} — over W configuration(s)` | **A5**, and it reads `decreasingOk` and nothing from the exactness gate. `W` is the new named field `machineConfigs` (= `walk.configs`), **never** `found.configs`. The store clause is no longer §3.1's own prose: `boundsSentence` prints it in every lane (§2.3, §3.P), which is what lifted correction 23 from a one-feature clause to a plan-wide disclosure |
| `vacuous` | `vacuous: no explored run opens the \`before\` scope` | `!found.activated`, non-`globally` scope, walk publishable |
| `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: bound exhausted — the not-covered claim is not made` | A5's condition fails and there is no breach |

**Sentences it MUST NEVER print, and why.**

- **"reachable"**, unqualified, when the witness consumed a trigger. The walk's environment offers every letter unconditionally; the word without §2.4(a) beside it claims something about the real environment. Measured on the probe: the witness consumes `abort`, and the bounds line says `alphabet abort` while nothing says every letter is offered at every configuration.
- **"not covered" on a non-exhaustive walk** — A5, suppressed exactly as `reach`'s unreachable list is at `explore.ts`:792.
- **"not covered over N product states"** where N is `found.configs` — the search short-circuits and :1228 says in the tool's own words that the number was never a total. **And the JSON must not disagree with the prose**: `base` (:1189) sets `configs: found.configs` unconditionally and every branch spreads it, so this commit adds `machineConfigs` to `PropertyVerdict`, the sentence names that field, and a test asserts the two numbers are both present, both labelled, and equal only where they should be.
- **`covered — … a run this semantics admits`** on a walk whose `timedTransitions` is non-empty and whose witness crossed one of them. This is the first affirmative claim this lane has ever published, and the engine's soundness note (`explore.ts`:29-31, *"an over-approximation can only shrink the absence claims, never invent one"*) was written when the lane had no positive claims at all. Measured, on a `ModelFactory` machine with `{trigger:'after(5)'} idle → A` declared before `{trigger:'after(10)'} idle → B` (there is no `.sysml` spelling — correction 22): the interpreter fires `enabled[0]` at every advance granularity, so `B` is entered by **no run**; the walk offers `"after(10)"` as a named event with no dwell test and finds a one-step witness. `reach` reporting `B` reachable is the safe direction — it only shrinks the unreachable list — while `cover` republishes the identical over-approximation as an affirmative finding **with a witness**, flipping the direction. The re-worded sentence above is what prints instead; the bare one is banned. **The two rows are exhaustive and mutually exclusive, and the draft's were not:** the draft required the time condition on row 1 and its negation *plus an after-label step* on row 2, leaving a real shape — a walk with `timedLabels` non-empty whose witness fired **no** after-label — matched by neither row, with the implementer free to pick either sentence and no test able to go red. Row 1's condition is therefore the disjunction, over all three mechanisms rather than only time, and `WITNESS_CLAIMS` row W1 is what makes the choice machine-checked instead of a coin toss. Measured shape: `{trigger: 'abort'} idle → target` and `{trigger: 'after(5)'} target → sink`; `cover (state target)` has `timedLabels = {"after(5)"}` and a one-step witness that fired `abort` — row 1, and the bare wording, correctly.

- **Exit 1 under `--cover-required` when every unreached state of the cover sits behind a guard that evaluated `undefined`.** Correction 23: `seedStore` (`config.ts`:231-243) fills the store from **declared literal values only**, so `attribute mode : Integer;` with no value is absent, `evalStr` yields `undefined`, `guardHoldsStore` (:70-77) returns false and the edge is never offered. **Before `c929278` that happened on a walk where every publishability condition held; after `c929278` (§0.1) it is `Publishability.guardsDetermined` that fails and `verification/guard-undetermined` that prints**, and this flag's refusal is the *cover*-shaped answer to the same shape rather than a substitute for it. The draft's warrant here — *"the walk and the interpreter agree on the decreasing side, which is why no publishability condition can see it"* — is **withdrawn**: it is true of `not covered`, whose polarity a missing edge does not flip, and false of `verification/deadlock`, which a missing edge invents (register A0, §2.3). (`undeterminedGuards` is non-empty on this walk, so the *increasing* side is already refused by the gate's clause (d); the flag's refusal below is the decreasing side's answer to the same shape, and the two are not interchangeable.) Reproduced this session: `part def Ctrl { attribute mode : Integer; state def Modes { state idle; state hazard; transition idle if mode == 3 then hazard; } }` walks `1 configuration(s) … exhaustive under {…}` and raises `verification/unreachable-state`, `verification/dead-transition` and `verification/deadlock`. `not covered` is still the honest answer there — the design as written admits no run into `hazard` — but it is an answer about an **unbound model parameter**, not about a violated requirement, so the store clause prints on the line and the flag does not spend the 1. The refusal sentence: `--cover-required not applied: every unreached state of this cover sits behind a guard over an attribute with no declared value, so the walk read it as false — exit 2, not 1`.
- **`claim: 'fail'` + `verification/refuted`** on a `not-covered` row. `cover` reuses `absence`'s identical monitor breach, so falling into that branch (:1229-1234) exits 1 for a correct design — the exact defect this feature exists to fix, reproduced by the fix.
- **"verified" / "safe"**, and **"the design can enter failsafe"** as a bare sentence — it drops the bound and the environment, and (measured, §2.6) today's guard would not catch it, which is why commit 4 widens the guard first.
- **Exit 1 by default.** A missing behaviour is not a violated requirement.

**Exit-code contract.** `behaviour`. `covered` counts toward the **0** row — **and the 0 row's own sentence moves in this commit to say so** (§2.1): it names the two discharge shapes separately, an assertion shown to hold on every reachable configuration over a graph seen whole, and a cover witnessed on a run this walk saw. Without that edit the command publishes, in its own `--help` and in the generated `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md`, a 0 that means something the run did not establish — on every `covered` row, exhaustive or not. `not-covered` ⇒ **2**, or **1** under `--cover-required` — **except on correction 23's shape, where the flag refuses to spend the 1 and says so** (the MUST-NEVER above): a cover whose unreached states all sit behind a guard over an attribute with no declared value is an answer about an unbound model parameter, not about a design that violates something. **The flag is recorded on the summary line and in the `--json` payload, and nowhere else.** The draft said "recorded in the evidence record's `flags`"; measured, `check-behaviour` writes no evidence record and has no way to — its flags are exactly `element · pattern · max-configs · strict-vacuity` (`sysprose-spec.ts`:878-907), `--record` is declared on `verify` alone (:616), and `EvidenceRecord.flags` (`api/evidence.ts`:613) is only ever filled on that path. Adding one would mean a new record kind through `evidence-record.schema.json`'s `additionalProperties: false`, i.e. exactly the one-directional schema commitment §3.3a says this plan does not spend outside commit 11. If a durable record of a flag-raised exit is wanted, it is §7 decision 11, priced the way commit 11's is — not a clause here. `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` (:201) gains the **0**-row rewrite and the **2**-row clause, both quoted in §2.1, and both asserted in `cli-reference.test.ts`'s per-contract block at :206-214.

**Tests.**
- A fixture pair (`cover-reachable`, `cover-sealed`): `cover` is `covered` with a 2-step witness on the first and `not-covered` / exit 2 on the second, **with the inverse `absence` verdicts asserted in the same test** — that pair is the polarity regression.
- `--cover-required` turns the second into exit **1** with the second code, turns nothing else red, and leaves both claim words byte-identical.
- **The bound frontier, asserted from BOTH sides, because the draft's version of this test would have failed on landing.** A witness at depth *d* needs `--max-configs ≥ d`: the violation check runs on a freshly-stepped successor (`patterns.ts`:944-953) but that successor is only enqueued **past** the config bound at :969, and `seen` starts holding the root. Measured on the shipped example — `check-behaviour examples/uav-isr.sysml --element FlightModes --pattern "pattern=absence, scope=globally, p=state failsafe"`, a 3-step witness — gives rc=2 `inconclusive` / `verification/bound-exhausted` at `--max-configs 1` **and 2**, and rc=1 `fail` only from `--max-configs 3`. `cover` reuses `absence`'s identical breach, so the draft's "`--max-configs 1`: `covered` still stands" was false for the 2-step probe. The test asserts the frontier instead of assuming it: `--max-configs <depth>` keeps `covered`, `--max-configs <depth − 1>` gives `inconclusive`. The sealed machine is `inconclusive` at any bound below exhaustion, **not** `not-covered`.
- **The two-dwell fixture** (§2.3), **built through `ModelFactory` inside the test file and exercised through `checkProperty` in-process, never through argv** — `accept after(n)` is a parse error (correction 22), so there is no `.sysml` spelling of it and a CLI-level assertion here is unwritable. `cover (state B)` where the shorter dwell preempts prints the **walk-admits** wording and never a bare `covered`, and `timedTransitions` is asserted non-empty in the same test.
- **The row-1 disjunction, which the draft could not have tested:** the `{trigger:'abort'} idle → target`, `{trigger:'after(5)'} target → sink` machine — `timedLabels` non-empty, witness fired no after-label — asserts the **bare** `covered` wording **with §2.4(a) beside it**, because the witness consumed `abort`; the walk-admits wording is named as the wrong answer. Together with the previous bullet the two rows are shown to partition, which is what W1 asserts by reflection.
- **The witness row does not read the bound, asserted from the register rather than only from the fixture.** In `semantics.mc.publishable.test.ts`, every row of `WITNESS_CLAIMS` whose `kind` is `'existential'` is asserted to read `relationIsTheMachines` and **not** to name any member of the bound family (`seenWhole`, `decreasingOk`, `Publishability.searchComplete`) in either column, and the `--max-configs <depth>` case is repeated there: the measured `partial under {maxConfigs 3, …}` walk still publishes `covered`. Without this assertion the reflection test and commit 6's bound-frontier test are mutually unsatisfiable and the cheap repair is to weaken the reflection test (§2.3).
- **The undecided-guard witness**, which is the store's half of the same partition and is an ordinary `.sysml` file rather than a factory machine: a cover whose witness crosses `transition idle if mode == 3 then hazard;` with `attribute mode : Integer;` undeclared cannot arise (the edge is never offered), so the fixture is its mirror — `attribute mode : Integer = 3;` gives a bare `covered`, and dropping the `= 3` gives `not covered` with the store named on the bounds line and `undeterminedGuards` non-empty. The pair pins that clause (d) tracks the declared literal and not the guard.
- **The unset-guard fixture** (correction 23): `attribute mode : Integer;` with `transition idle if mode == 3 then hazard;` ⇒ `not covered` whose bounds line **names the seeded store**, exit 2, and — under `--cover-required` — still exit **2** with the refusal sentence, never exit 1. The census field `coverUnreachedBehindUndefinedGuard` is asserted non-zero on it and zero on the sealed probe, so the two shapes are told apart by a number and not by a reviewer.
- Cover over a **`fires T`** atom, over an **expression** atom, and under a **`between`** scope — the three shapes `reach` structurally cannot answer, which are this feature's whole justification. (`cover` accepts all five atom kinds; `recovery` does not, and §3.2b says why.)
- **The counts-sum guard** (§2.2) across every behaviour fixture.
- `cli-reference.test.ts`'s `PATTERN_NAMES`-order assertion re-recorded, **and its liveness-pair guard asserted UNCHANGED** — green with no edit, because `cover` inserts before `existence` and `[existence, response]` stays the last two.
- The carrier round-trips **idempotently from the second save**.

**One semantic decision, written down before the first fixture is recorded.** `absence`'s `before`/`between` scopes only establish a breach once the segment closes (`needsClosing`, :782-793), so a cover witness under those scopes extends to the closing R. Defensible, but a decision and not a re-label: it goes in `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` §8.5 with the pattern table.

**Cost.** One `PatternClass` member, one `PATTERNS` entry, one `monitorStep` case, ~60 lines of verdict tail, two claim words, two codes, one flag. The real cost is documentation: `CONFORMANCE.md`:865-883 (*"Four patterns … are safety"*, *"Two … are liveness"*, *"the verdict vocabulary is four words"* — all three move), `USER-GUIDE.md`:1744-1751 (the pattern table with its `Decided here?` column), `DIAGNOSTIC-CODES.md`, `AGENT-AUTHORING-CAMPAIGN.md`:3556/:3608, `docs/04`:337/:590, and the sentence at `scripts/sysprose.ts`:3235 that appears in **every** transcript.

**Census.** `check-behaviour --json` records `{atomKind, scope, coverUnreachedBehindUndefinedGuard}` per cover row — the third being correction 23's exposure, counted rather than argued about: the number of states this cover did not reach whose only inbound guard read `undefined` against the seeded store. The kill measurement: if ≥80 % are `state X × globally`, `reach` already answers them and `cover` should be retired in favour of printing the witness path in `reach`. **Sharpened by measurement:** that shape *is* answered today — in the negative direction only, as a warning that changes no exit code, with no witness for the positive direction — and nothing in `reach`'s JSON reports an expression atom, a `fires T` or a scoped question. The escape hatch is real and the census is what decides it.

---

### 3.R — The guaranteed / potential modality on an existing `fail`

**What it decides.** Whether the violation the tool just printed happens on **some** run or on **every** run. It annotates; it replaces nothing.

**Command and flags.** None. `PropertyVerdict` (:286-314) gains one field and the text row gains one clause.

**Engine.** In-process TypeScript over P's relation. `walk` is already in lexical scope at `checkProperty`'s `fail` block — it is the same `const walk = exploreMachine(…)` the function opens with — so after P the machine graph with edges is in hand at the fail site at **zero extra cost**.

**Scope, restricted three times, all three restrictions measured.**

1. **Memoryless monitors only** — `absence` / `universality` × `globally` — where the product collapses onto the configuration graph. `search` short-circuits at :944-953, so the *product* graph does not exist at the fail site; a stateful monitor (`precedence`, `bounded-existence`, every non-`globally` scope) needs a second, non-short-circuiting pass and is refused with that reason.
2. **BOTH modality values require an empty alphabet, and the draft restricted only one of them.** Measured on `nominal --latch--> locked`, `locked --latch--> locked`: the naive computation calls `absence(locked)` **guaranteed**, because under the maximally cooperative environment every configuration has a successor. In the real system where `latch` never arrives, the machine sits in `nominal` forever. Restricting to completion-only machines is the only honest option before 5b lands a fairness carrier; the alternative — admitting "the environment stops offering" as a maximal run — degenerates the modality to `potential` on every triggered machine. **`potential` needs the same gate, for the mirror-image reason, and the draft left it ungated.** Its sentence names a specific avoiding **cycle** computed over the retained relation, and that relation over-approximates time: on a machine built with `{trigger:'after(5)'} nominal → hazard` declared before `{trigger:'after(60)'} nominal → hold` plus `{trigger:'after(1)'} hold → nominal`, `absence (state hazard) globally` correctly `fail`s, the violating set is `{hazard}`, *G₀* holds the cycle `nominal → hold → nominal` built **entirely** from dwell edges the walk offers as named events — and the tool would print `potential — some run avoids it: the cycle nominal → hold → nominal never enters \`hazard\`` about a machine on which `enabled[0]` sends **every** run into `hazard` and the named cycle exists on no run at all. That is this section's own MUST-NEVER — a claim about the simulator's tie-break, published as a claim about the model — reached from the other side. **The draft closed both hazards with one clause, on the containment `timedLabels ⊆ alphabet`; that containment is deleted (§2.3) and the two clauses are independent**, so the modality field is `not decided` whenever the alphabet is non-empty **or a transition of this machine is a dwell** — for either value — and §2.4(c)'s dwell sentence prints beside the refusal rather than beside a claim. On the measured machine above both clauses fail; on a machine whose dwells carry a numeric `attrs.after` and no trigger only the time clause does, and that is the machine the alphabet-only version of this rule published `potential` about. It is registered as `WITNESS_CLAIMS` **W3** so §5(a)'s reflection test sees it. **And the third review added a third hazard to the same gate**: on the trapguard machine (§2.3, mechanism 3 — a clean-loading `.sysml` file, unlike the dwell fixtures) the escape edge `degradedA → nominal` is guarded by an attribute with no declared value, so the walk never offered it, *G₀* holds the cycle `degradedA → degradedB → degradedA`, and the tool would print `potential — some run avoids it` about a machine whose model states the escape. That is neither a time nor an environment hazard and neither of the draft's two rows caught it, which is precisely why this section now reads one gate — `walkIsExact` — rather than its own conjunction. Row 7 is its sentence.

3. **Configuration-valued atoms only, and that means `state` and `node` — not `expression`.** The restriction the draft stated over patterns and scopes is not enough, because the violation set is a predicate over CONFIGURATIONS and **three** of the five atoms are not configuration predicates readable from what §3.P retains. `fires T` and `trigger t` are properties of a **step** (`atoms.ts`:93-99, and `patterns.ts`'s monitor reads the fired label). And `expression` needs the **store**: `atomHolds` evaluates `evalStr(atom.argument, obs.config.store, scope)` (`atoms.ts`:350-353), while the only per-configuration data §3.P retains is `configStates`, the stack of state ElementIds. **The draft accepted `expression` here and refused it in §3.2b, and the two sections stated opposite gates** — §6.1 and §7 decision 12 both already say `expression` "needs the store", so §3.R was the outlier and it is the one that moves. Measured consequence if left open, and it is the identical failure to the `fires` one: `--pattern "pattern=absence, scope=globally, p=(mode == 3)"` partitions the configurations with no store, so **no** configuration is marked violating, *G* is the whole graph, which on any cyclic machine holds a cycle, and the row prints `potential — some run avoids it: the cycle …` beside the `fail` on a machine where every run sets `mode = 3` and every run violates. So **`state` and `node` are accepted; `fires`, `trigger` and `expression` are refused**, each with the reason its own kind earns. That is the same shape as the stateful-monitor refusal, and §3.2b takes the identical gate — one gate, one wording, two sections.

**The decision, written over the retained relation explicitly.** Let *G* be the sub-graph **induced** on the non-violating configurations, and let *G₀* be the configurations of *G* reachable from the opening **within *G***. A run avoiding the violation exists iff *G₀* holds a cycle, **or** *G₀* holds a configuration `i` with `successors[i].length === 0` **in the full retained relation**. `guaranteed` iff neither.

**Both scopings in that sentence are load-bearing, and the draft got both wrong.**

- *"reachable from the opening"* alone admits a cycle that is only entered by passing **through** a violating configuration. On such a machine every run violates, the truth is `guaranteed`, and the tool would print `potential` naming a cycle no violation-avoiding run reaches — a sentence about a specific run that does not exist.
- *"a configuration with no successors"* alone reads naturally as *no successors in G*, and that reading is unsound. Take `nominal --completion--> hazard`, `hazard --completion--> hazard`, property `absence (state hazard) × globally`. Violating = `{hazard}`; *G* = `{nominal}`; in *G*, `nominal` has no successor, because its only successor is outside *G*. The rule would report `potential — some run avoids it`, while every maximal run is `nominal → hazard → hazard …` and every one violates. A sink is a sink in the **full** graph or it is not a sink.

The failure direction of both mistakes is toward `potential`, which is the safe direction for the *claim word* and the wrong direction for the *sentence* — and the sentence is the entire deliverable of this feature.

**Verdict vocabulary — and the rows are a DISPATCH, evaluated top to bottom, because the precedence is part of the specification.** The draft listed them unordered, the `potential` row and the `alphabet` row overlapped, and the prose beside the table resolved the overlap toward the unsound answer. The order below is the fix; a test asserts it by running a machine that satisfies rows 5, 6 and 7 at once and requiring row 5's sentence.

| # | Value | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `not decided: the walk was not exhaustive` | **`!seenWhole`** — `!walk.exhaustive`, `boundHit !== 'none'`, or an unsupported construct |
