| 2 | `not decided: this monitor carries state (\`precedence\`), so the product does not collapse onto the configuration graph` | stateful monitor (restriction 1) |
| 3 | `not decided: this atom is a property of a step, not of a configuration, so the product does not collapse onto the configuration graph` | `fires` or `trigger` atom (restriction 3) |
| 4 | `not decided: this atom reads the store, which this walk does not retain` | **`expression` atom** (restriction 3 — the row the draft's version of this table did not have, because §3.R accepted the kind that §3.2b, §6.1 and decision 12 all refuse) |
| 5 | `not decided: this machine names triggers, and "every run" would be a claim about an environment this walk has no carrier for` | **`alphabet ∖ timedLabels` non-empty** — a real trigger, not a dwell. `Exactness.failedClause === 'environment'` |
| 6 | `not decided: this machine carries \`after(n)\` dwell transitions this walk takes without advancing a clock, so a run that avoids the violation may be one the interpreter never takes` + §2.4(c) | **`timedTransitions` non-empty** and `alphabet ∖ timedLabels` empty. `failedClause === 'time'`. **No pointer to `FairnessAssumption`**: a dwell is not a trigger an environment can withhold, and §2.4(c) says so |
| 7 | `not decided: a transition of this machine is guarded by an attribute with no declared value, so this walk never offered an edge the model states and a run that avoids the violation may be one it could not see` + the store sentence | **`undeterminedGuards` non-empty.** `failedClause === 'store'`. This is the row the trapguard measurement (§2.3, mechanism 3) produced a false `potential`/`guaranteed` from, and it is a **`.sysml`-reachable** shape, unlike row 6 |
| 8 | `potential — some run avoids it: the cycle manual → autonomous → manual never enters \`failsafe\`` + §2.4(d) | **W3.** rows 1–7 all passed — i.e. `walkIsExact` holds and the atom and monitor are admissible — and *G₀* holds a cycle or a full-relation sink |
| 9 | `guaranteed — every maximal run of this machine reaches it` + §2.4(d) | **A8.** rows 1–7 all passed, and neither |

**Rows 1 and 5–7 are the four clauses of `walkIsExact`, spelled as four sentences rather than one.** The gate is one predicate and the dispatch is one `failedClause` switch: the implementer computes `Exactness` once and the table selects the sentence. That is what the collapse buys here — the draft had this section carrying its own bespoke conjunction (`walk.exhaustive ∧ alphabet.length === 0` on one value and nothing on the other), and §3.2a, §3.2b, §3.5a and §3.8 each carried a different one. **Why the clauses stay four sentences and not one:** an author whose machine names `abort` and an author whose machine guards on an unset attribute have two different things to do about it, and a single *"this walk is not exact"* would tell neither of them which.

**Why rows 5 and 6 are two rows and not the draft's one.** The draft's condition was `alphabet.length > 0` and its sentence was *"this machine names triggers"*. But `bounds.alphabet` **is** `machineAlphabet`, which by design contains every `after(n)` label (`explore.ts`:277, docstring :266-276), so on a machine whose only labelled transitions are dwells the tool would print *"this machine names triggers"* about a machine that names none — and send the author to a carrier for a hazard §2.4(c) explicitly says is not one. The split is by subtraction, and §3.5a takes the identical split for the identical reason.

**MUST NEVER print, and why.**

- **`guaranteed` on a triggered machine** — the `latch` measurement above.
- **`guaranteed` from a non-exhaustive walk** — A8.
- **`potential` on a triggered machine, on one whose `timedTransitions` is non-empty, or on one whose `undeterminedGuards` is non-empty** — rows 5, 6 and 7, and W3, which reads the whole gate because a claim that a cycle has no exit is a maximality claim (§2.3). The avoiding cycle is a positive existential claim over a relation that over-approximates the environment and time and under-approximates the store; the draft gated the universal value and left the existential one open, which is the polarity error §2.3 exists to stop, committed inside the section that names it.
- **`guaranteed` on a machine with an undecided guard** — row 7, and the mirror of the same measurement: an edge the model states and the walk never offered can only make *G₀* smaller, so it manufactures `guaranteed` as easily as `potential`.
- **`potential` or `guaranteed` from an `expression` atom** — row 4. The store is not retained, so no configuration is ever marked violating and *G* is the whole graph: the answer would be `potential` on every cyclic machine, whatever the model says.
- **The sentence *"this machine names triggers"* about a machine whose alphabet is all dwells** — row 5 vs row 6.
- **A modality derived from the declaration-order tie-break** (correction 13): it would print a claim about the simulator as a claim about the model.
- **A modality derived from `found`** — the prefix walked before the first witness is an accident of where the search stopped.
- **Any downgrade of the existing `fail`.** `claim`, `code`, `severity`, the witness and exit 1 are untouched.

**Exit-code contract.** Unchanged. `counts` and `exitCode` never see the field.

**Tests.** Four measured machines, four answers: `uav-isr absence(failsafe)` ⇒ `potential`, **with the reason string asserted to name the cycle and not the tie-break**; a mission DAG ⇒ `guaranteed`; a sink ⇒ `guaranteed`; the `latch` machine ⇒ `not decided` — the regression that stops the naive `guaranteed`. A `precedence` row ⇒ `not decided` with the stateful reason. `--max-configs 1` on the DAG ⇒ `not decided`. **Two fixtures that pin the corrected decision, and each names a wrong answer:** (a) a machine whose avoiding cycle sits **behind** a violating configuration — the reachability-within-*G* test, asserted `guaranteed`, with `potential` named as the wrong answer; (b) `nominal → hazard`, `hazard → hazard`, `absence (state hazard)` — the sink-scoping test, asserted `guaranteed`, with `potential` named as the wrong answer. Plus a `fires` atom ⇒ `not decided` with the step-property reason, **and an `expression` atom (`p=(mode == 3)`) ⇒ `not decided` with the store reason** — the refusal the draft's §3.R did not have, with `potential — some run avoids it: the cycle …` named as the wrong answer on a machine where every run sets `mode = 3`. **The dwell-cycle fixture, factory-built** (correction 22): `{trigger:'after(5)'} nominal → hazard` before `{trigger:'after(60)'} nominal → hold`, `{trigger:'after(1)'} hold → nominal` ⇒ row 6's `not decided` with §2.4(c) beside it, with `potential … the cycle nominal → hold → nominal` named as the wrong answer and **`FairnessAssumption` asserted absent from the string**. **The same fixture re-spelled with numeric `attrs.after` and no trigger** — `alphabet []`, so the draft's alphabet-only rule decided the modality — asserts row 6 as well, with `guaranteed` and `potential` both named as wrong answers; it is the fixture that shows rows 5 and 6 are two independent clauses and not one. **The trapguard fixture — an ordinary `.sysml` file, driven through argv as well as in-process** (`attribute resetOk : Boolean;` with `nominal → degradedA → degradedB → degradedA` and `degradedA if resetOk then nominal`) ⇒ row 7's `not decided` with the store sentence beside it, with **`potential — some run avoids it: the cycle degradedA → degradedB → degradedA`** named as the wrong answer; **both literal variants of the same file assert the modality is DECIDED** — `= true` and `= false` alike, because clause (d) is about whether the guard was decided and not about which way it went — so the row is shown to track decidedness and not the presence of a guard; the value each one decides to is asserted from the run rather than quoted here. **The latch fixture** (`transition locked accept unlatch then nominal;`, also a `.sysml` file) ⇒ row 5, which is the same machine §3.2a and §3.2b use for their clause-(c) refusals — one model, three sections, one gate. **The row-precedence test:** a machine satisfying rows 5, 6, 7 and 8 at once — a real trigger, a dwell, an undecided guard, and an avoiding cycle — asserts row 5's sentence, so the dispatch order is pinned rather than inferred. And a test asserting `claim`, `code`, `severity`, `detail` prefix and witness are **byte-identical** to today's with and without the field.

**Cost.** ~35 lines and one field. No new code, no new pattern, no doc-count movement — the cheapest honest thing in this plan. `guaranteed` is **not** caught by today's claims guard (§2.6); commit 4 adds the form.

**Census.** `{guaranteed, potential, notDecided}` per run — the observation that says whether the annotation ever separates anything on real models.

---

### 3.2a — Traps on `reach`: bottom strongly-connected components

**What it decides.** Which reachable configurations form a set nothing leaves — Stanley & Laski's condition (2), the half `reach` does not ship. *"If condition (2) is violated, program exit is never reached if the node is reached on some computation."*

**Command and flags.** `npm run sysprose -- reach <file> [--element REF] [--max-configs N]` — **unchanged**. No new flag, no new subcommand: the engineer changes nothing about what they type.

**Engine.** In-process TypeScript. One iterative Tarjan pass (commit 3) over `ExploreResult.successors`, then the bottom-component filter.

**The report.** `MachineReach` (:653-684) gains one field, gated on **`walkIsExact`** — *not* on the `publishable` the unreachable and dead lists use, which is `decreasingOk` and which register row A6 (polarity `increasing`) may not read. A bound or an unsupported construct **empties** the field rather than shortening it, exactly as `states.unreachable` is emptied; the other three clauses produce the named `inconclusive` rows of the verdict table below. **The draft's sentence here said `publishable`, and it is the one place in this section that contradicted its own table** — an implementer writing the field from this paragraph lands `semantics.mc.publishable.test.ts` red on the reflection rule *"no `increasing` row reads `decreasingOk`"*, on the very commit that introduces the register's first new increasing consumer, and the cheap repair under a red gate is to weaken the reflection test:

```ts
traps: readonly {
  configs: number;
  states: readonly StateRef[];   // the union of the ACTIVE STACKS, not the leaves
  entry: readonly StateRef[];    // shortest path from the opening
}[];
```

**Three exemptions, each with code already in the tree, and the third is the one that keeps the shipped examples silent.**

1. **A trivial bottom SCC with no self-loop is already `verification/deadlock`.** It is a configuration with no successors — precisely `recordDeadlock`'s subject (`explore.ts`:631-648). Same defect, two codes, two rows, and the report looks like it found twice as much. **This exemption hands the claim to another producer, so that producer has to be gated too**, which is register row **A0** (§2.3): without it, this section's clause-(d) refusal — *"a set it calls inescapable may not be"* — prints two lines above an ungated deadlock row asserting the configuration has no way out, on a file whose model states the escape. The exemption is what makes that structural rather than incidental, and A0 is what closes it.
2. **An ending is not a trap.** A bottom SCC whose configurations are final or terminal is where the machine is *meant* to stop. Reuse `isFinalState` and `isTerminalNode` **verbatim** — `recordDeadlock` already calls both at :639-640 with the comments *"an ending, not a deadlock"* and *"`done` — the same ending, spelled in the notation"*. Measured on a four-state mission DAG into `done`: its only bottom SCC is `{landed}`.
3. **A bottom SCC containing the opening configuration is the machine's recoverable core, not a trap.** Nothing leaves a bottom SCC, so if the opening lies inside one, the reachable set *is* that component: there is no configuration to be trapped *from*. **This exemption is load-bearing, not decorative.** Measured: `FlightModes` is one SCC of all four configurations (`standby → manual → autonomous → failsafe → standby` closes the cycle), it is its own bottom SCC, and **none** of its four states is final or terminal — so exemptions 1 and 2 alone would file a `trap` row saying *"no run leaves them once entered"* about the flagship example's entire normal operating region, and would move `cli.sysprose.test.ts`:3175's exact diagnostics array. All three shipped machines are single SCCs and all three are silent for exactly this reason.

The genuinely new information over shipped `reach` is therefore: **non-trivial bottom SCCs that do not contain the opening, plus trivial self-looping ones that are neither final nor terminal.** The trap probe of §1 — `failsafe ⇄ failsafeHold` absorbing, completion-driven throughout — is exactly that shape, and it is the feature's positive fixture.

**And the exactness gate takes the draft's second positive fixture away, which is stated here rather than discovered in the commit.** The draft also offered a latch machine (`nominal ⇄ degraded → locked`, `locked → locked accept latch`) as a trap this section finds. Under clause (c) it is **refused**, because `latch` is a named trigger and the walk supplies it: `locked → locked` is a self-loop the environment causes, and whether `{locked}` is inescapable is a question about the environment this engine has no carrier for. That is the correct answer and it is a real narrowing of the feature — the trigger-driven trap, which is the intuitive one, is the one the gate withholds. What survives is the completion-driven trap, which is what the trap probe is and what the corpus contains. Whether that is enough to justify the feature is what the trap census is for (below), and it is now the honest question.

**Verdict vocabulary.**

| Sentence | Condition |
|---|---|
| `trap — 2 configuration(s) form a set nothing leaves: {failsafe, failsafeHold}. Entered in 3 step(s) from \`standby\`.` + §2.4(d) when the machine has a choice point | **A6** — `walkIsExact` holds — and the component survives all three exemptions. **§2.4(a) no longer appears on this row**: the environment is now clause (c) of the gate, so a machine with a non-empty alphabet does not get a trap row with a disclosure beside it, it gets the refusal below |
| `no component of this walk is inescapable beyond its endings — 1 ending, 0 deadlock row(s) and 1 whole-graph component were exempted, exhaustive under {…}` | A6 holds and no component qualifies. **`--json` only, never a text row.** The counts come from the census fields this section already specifies (`exemptedAsEnding`, `exemptedAsDeadlock`, `exemptedAsCore`) |
| `inconclusive: this machine names triggers this walk offers at every configuration, so an escape it found may be one the environment never supplies and no absence of an escape is claimed` + §2.4(a) | **`failedClause === 'environment'`.** The trap list is suppressed **and so is the no-trap sentence**. Measured, this is the row the latch machine takes — `state locked; transition locked accept unlatch then nominal;` walks as one SCC of two containing the opening, so without this row exemption 3 fires and the tool publishes *"no component of this walk is inescapable"* about a design in which `locked` is inescapable unless somebody sends `unlatch` |
| `inconclusive: this machine carries \`after(n)\` dwell transitions this walk takes without advancing a clock, so the escape relation is an over-approximation and no absence of an escape is claimed` + §2.4(c) | **`failedClause === 'time'`.** Both the list and the sentence are suppressed |
| `inconclusive: a transition of this machine is guarded by an attribute with no declared value, so this walk never offered an edge the model states and a set it calls inescapable may not be` + the store sentence | **`failedClause === 'store'`.** Measured on the trapguard machine: without this row the tool publishes `trap — 2 configuration(s) form a set nothing leaves: {degradedA, degradedB}. Entered in 1 step(s) from \`nominal\`` as `verification/unrecoverable-mode`, at exit 0, about a design that states the escape edge |
| *(nothing at all)* | `!seenWhole` — suppressed, exactly as `states.unreachable` is |

**Why the "no trap" sentence is two sentences and not one, and why it counts.** The draft printed `every reachable configuration lies in one component — nothing this walk found is inescapable`. Both clauses are false on this section's **own fixtures**. (a) The mission DAG into `done` has four configurations in four **trivial** SCCs, so "lies in one component" is plainly false — that wording is only ever true of the single-SCC shape the three shipped machines happen to have, while the condition that triggers it (no component qualifies) is satisfied by every DAG whose ending is exempted. (b) On the two-state sink fixture the report would carry a `verification/deadlock` row saying a configuration has no way out **and** this sentence saying nothing is inescapable, in one `reach` output — the tool contradicting itself in one report, which is the identical defect §3.5a forbids for the *"every run of this machine terminates"* clause and which the draft did not carry over to here. The replacement asserts no component cardinality and names what was exempted, so the deadlock row and the sentence are two halves of one accounting rather than two answers. **It is asserted byte-identically on the mission DAG and on the sink**, which is where the old wording failed.

**MUST NEVER print, and why.**

- **"the system can always recover"** — escaping a trap may need a trigger the real environment never offers.
- **"livelock"**, **"deadlock-free"** — the second is a bare-word ban with no allowance.
- **A trap row on a non-exhaustive walk** — A6.
- **Any trap row, and any "nothing is inescapable" sentence, on a machine `walkIsExact` refuses** — A6, all four clauses, and this is where the collapse earns its keep. Four separate measured shapes reach this section and the draft's single time condition caught exactly one of them — and then only in the spelling it was written for. (i) **Time:** `failsafe` with `after(5) → failsafeHold` declared before `after(60) → standby` — **built through `ModelFactory`, since `accept after(n)` is a parse error (correction 22)** — is a genuine inescapable trap that the walk sees an escape from, on a walk that is exhaustive, alphabet-offered and fully supported, with no `verification/nondeterministic-choice` row to hint at it. (ii) **The environment:** the latch machine, an ordinary `.sysml` file, is one whole-graph SCC only because the walk supplies `unlatch`; without clause (c) exemption 3 fires and the no-trap sentence publishes. (iii) **The store:** the trapguard machine, also an ordinary `.sysml` file, publishes a `trap` row naming a component the model states an escape from. (iv) **Time again, in a second spelling:** the numeric-`attrs.after` machine of §2.3, whose three dwells contribute no alphabet member at all, so an alphabet-scoped clause (b) reads clean and this section publishes *"no component of this walk is inescapable beyond its endings"* about a machine whose `{failsafe, failsafeHold}` no run leaves. Enumerating one condition per shape is what failed twice, and scoping a clause to the producer that happened to be measured is what failed the third time; the gate plus a producer defined over the **transitions** is what stops a fifth shape needing a fifth MUST-NEVER bullet.
- **A trap `entry` witness whose path crossed a dwell transition, stated as a run the semantics admits.** The entry path is a positive claim and takes §3.1's re-wording: `Entered in 3 step(s) from \`standby\` — on a run this WALK admits: step 2 fires \`after(10)\` …`. In practice A6's gate already suppresses the whole row, so this clause exists for the day the gate is relaxed per-claim rather than per-walk, and the sentence is written now so that relaxation cannot be made silently.
- **A trap row for an SCC of final states**, or **a trap row and a `verification/deadlock` row about the same configuration** — exemptions 2 and 1.
- **A trap row about the whole reachable graph** — exemption 3.
- **A trap named by leaf state** — one state spans many configurations; `states` is the union of `configStates`, never `leafOf()`.
- **The bottom-SCC cardinality printed under the recovery question.** Measured on the trap probe: the bottom SCC is **2** configurations while the set that cannot reach `standby` is **4**, because that machine has no edge back to `standby` at all. `docs/05`'s own verdict sentence fuses the two and prints the first number under the second question. They are separate answers, in separate sections, with separate sentences.

**Exit-code contract.** `reach` stays `report`: **exit 0, with findings.** A trap is `verification/unrecoverable-mode`, a **warning** — the severity `explore.ts`:114-119 reserves for *"findings about a MACHINE — a state nothing reaches, a transition nothing fires, a state nothing leaves"*, which is exactly what this is.

**Tests.** The trap probe (one trap of 2 configurations, 3-step entry, completion-driven so the gate holds) · the mission DAG into `done` (**none** — exemption 2, **with the no-trap sentence asserted byte-identically**, naming 1 ending exempted) · a two-state sink (`verification/deadlock` **only**, trap list asserted empty — exemption 1, **and the no-trap sentence asserted byte-identically in the same output as the deadlock row**, naming 1 deadlock exempted) · `examples/uav-isr.sysml`, `vehicle.sysml`, `views-tour.sysml` (**none** — exemption 3) with `cli.sysprose.test.ts`:3175's exact array **unchanged and re-run** · `--max-configs 2` on the trap probe (list **empty**, not shortened).

**The three gate fixtures are the commit's named negatives, one per mechanism, and two of the three are ordinary `.sysml` files.**

- **The preempted-dwell fixture** (`after(5) → failsafeHold` before `after(60) → standby`), **built through `ModelFactory` in the test file and driven through `reachReport` in-process — not a corpus `.sysml` file and not through argv, because the text spelling does not parse (correction 22)**: the trap list is **suppressed** with the time sentence, and the test names the wrong answer explicitly — a `recoverable`/no-trap pair on a machine whose failsafe is genuinely inescapable.
- **The latch fixture**, `state nominal; state locked; transition nominal then locked; transition locked accept unlatch then nominal;` — a `.sysml` file, asserted through argv **and** in-process. Measured today it walks `2 configuration(s) explored, depth 1 — exhaustive under {…, alphabet unlatch}`, `2 of 2 state(s) reachable; 2 of 2 transition(s) fired, 0 dead`, rc=0, **no findings**. The test asserts the clause-(c) refusal and names `no component of this walk is inescapable beyond its endings … 1 whole-graph component was exempted` as the wrong answer — the sentence the draft would have published about a machine whose `locked` is a mode nothing leaves unless an operator acts.
- **The trapguard fixture, in THREE variants and not two**, and the arithmetic of which variant proves what is where an earlier revision was wrong. The base file is `attribute resetOk : Boolean;` with `nominal → degradedA → degradedB → degradedA` and `degradedA if resetOk then nominal` — a `.sysml` file, asserted through argv and in-process. Re-measured at `dc6662c`: `3 configuration(s) explored, depth 2 — undetermined under {…, alphabet no named trigger} — 1 guard(s) the walk could not evaluate; the unreachable, dead and no-way-out lists are WITHHELD`, `3 of 3 state(s) reachable; 3 of 4 transition(s) fired, **dead withheld**`, one `verification/guard-undetermined`, rc=0. It asserts the clause-(d) refusal and names `trap — 2 configuration(s) form a set nothing leaves: {degradedA, degradedB}. Entered in 1 step(s) from \`nominal\`` as the wrong answer. **`= false` is the variant that asserts the trap row IS published** — measured `3 of 4 transition(s) fired, 1 dead`, `dead degradedA -> nominal`, `exhaustive under {…}`: the guard is present and *decided*, `undeterminedGuards` is empty, the gate holds, and `{degradedA, degradedB}` is a genuine bottom SCC that survives all three exemptions. **`= true` is the THIRD variant and it asserts the exemption-3 sentence, not a trap** — with the guard true the escape edge is retained, the relation is one SCC of all three configurations containing the opening, and the correct output is `no component of this walk is inescapable beyond its endings … 1 whole-graph component was exempted`. Written as two fixtures with `= true` in the positive slot, this test lands **red on commit 8**, and the cheap repair under a red gate is to weaken exemption 3 — the exemption §3.2a calls load-bearing, without which the flagship `FlightModes` gets a trap row over its entire normal operating region. Written as three, the pair proves both halves of the intended claim: **the clause tracks the guard's decidedness, not the guard's presence** (`= false` publishes a trap, the unset variant refuses), and **a decided-false guard is not a withheld edge**. The code-set guard — `it('names the seven codes this engine can raise')` in `test/unit/semantics.mc.reach.test.ts`, an exact sorted array — goes to **eight**, title string included; see commit 8.

**Cost.** One `MachineReach` field, one Tarjan consumer, **one code (+1; `npm run codes` regenerates the figure, which is why no absolute catalogue count appears in this plan)**, one renderer block in `machineLines` (`scripts/sysprose.ts`:3085-3111), `npm run codes`. the guide's `reach` transcript is **not machine-checked** — `docs-counts.test.ts` pins its figures from the shipped example and not one of them is a `reach` number — so it is re-pasted by hand here and added to the claims list rather than trusted. Grep the guide for `configuration(s) explored`.

**Census.** `{sccs, bottomSccs, trapsAfterExemptions, exemptedAsEnding, exemptedAsDeadlock, exemptedAsCore, refusedByGate}` per machine, the last carrying the failed clause. The kill measurement — *three consecutive real models that are one SCC or a DAG into final states* — becomes a query over recorded runs, and the gate adds a second, sharper one: **if `refusedByGate` reads `'environment'` on most machines carrying a trigger, the feature answers only completion-driven designs and the honest question is whether that population exists.** Measured, from §5(d): **3 of 3** shipped examples are exempted as core, and of the eleven machines in the tree only `GuardProbe::Ctrl` would be refused by the gate, on clause (d) — every machine has an empty alphabet and no dwell transition — so the gate costs this feature nothing on the shipped examples and the whole exposure is in models nobody in this tree has written. That is a pass and not evidence; the census is what turns it into data.

---

### 3.2b — The `recovery` pattern: reverse reachability to a named state

**What it decides.** Once the system enters degraded / failsafe / maintenance, can it get back to a state the engineer **names**? `AG EF p`, over any atom and any scope — which is what §3.2a's structural report cannot ask.

**Command and flags.** `check-behaviour <file> --element REF --pattern "pattern=recovery, scope=globally, p=state standby"`, or the same fields on a `PropertyPattern` carrier. `p` is read by the existing `readAtom` — **but only two of the five atom kinds are accepted, and the gate is part of the arm.** `AtomKind` is `'state' | 'trigger' | 'fires' | 'node' | 'expression'` (`atoms.ts`:58) and `resolveProperty` applies no per-pattern filter: it calls `readAtom` for every field in `pattern.fields ++ scope.fields` (`patterns.ts`:539-550), so `p=fires T` and `p=(mode == 3)` both resolve today. They must not reach the reverse-reachability pass. `configStates[i]` holds state ElementIds only (the stack, from `MachineConfig.stack`), §3.P deliberately retains no `MachineConfig`, and `trigger`/`fires` hold on a **step** rather than at a configuration (`Observation`, `atoms.ts`:93-99). Left ungated the target set is **empty**, and an empty target set lands on this section's own second `fail` row — `not recoverable: \`fires T\` holds at no reachable configuration at all`, `verification/refuted`, **error, exit 1**, printed about a well-formed model on the strength of an atom kind the engine cannot observe. So the arm accepts `state` and `node`, and refuses the other three with `verification/malformed-property`, **each with the reason its own kind earns**: `a trigger is observed on a step, not at a configuration, so \`recovery\` has no target set to reverse from` for `trigger` and `fires`, and `this atom reads the store, which this walk does not retain` for `expression`. **§3.R takes the identical gate in the identical wording** — the draft had §3.R accepting `expression` and this section refusing it, two sections stating opposite gates for one restriction, and §3.R is the one that moved (§3.R restriction 3), because §6.1 and §7 decision 12 already said the store is what `expression` needs. (The larger alternative — retaining a per-index atom valuation in `ExploreResult` instead of just `configStates` — is a much bigger change than §3.P describes and is not taken here; it is what would release the other three kinds, and §7 decision 12 records that.)

**The carrier value is a forward-compatibility commitment**, exactly as `"cover"` is (§3.1): a file carrying `pattern = "recovery"` is refused by any build before commit 9 as `verification/malformed-property` and exits 2 there. Recorded in `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` in this commit.

**Engine.** Reverse reachability over P's relation from `{configurations whose stack contains p}`, read **reflexively** — a configuration already holding `p` reaches it in zero steps, or `standby`'s own configuration is reported as unable to reach `standby`.

**The dispatch arm, which is not optional, and its position is load-bearing.** `recovery` is **branching-time**, and this engine is a bad-prefix search: `search` finds no bad prefix for `AG EF p` on *any* graph and would return **`pass`** for free — the exact failure mode the liveness refusal at :1122-1146 exists to prevent. `PatternClass` gains `'branching'` and `checkProperty` gains a **third dispatch arm placed AFTER the unsupported-construct refusal and BEFORE the `search` call** — by anchor, and only by anchor: after the `if (walk.unsupported.length > 0) { … }` block, and before `const found = search(…)`. An earlier revision named a line number for this, and the line it named held a **comment** sitting above the `const walk = exploreMachine(…)` call; an arm placed there would have **no walk, no `successors` and no `configStates`** and could only ever return `inconclusive`. That is the whole reason §0.0's contract exists, committed in the one instruction where the position is the specification. Calling `exploreMachine` a second time inside the arm to make it work is worse: the arm then sits **ahead of** the unsupported refusal, which is the only enforcement point on this function for the **unsupported clause of `seenWhole`** (`walk.unsupported.length === 0`, one of the two conjuncts §2.3 spells out because the early return sets `exhaustive: false` with `boundHit: 'none'`) — A7 would read a gate half nothing on this path enforces. The stated position is the only one where the walk exists, that clause has been enforced, and no bad-prefix search has run, which is what the commit's headline test asserts.

**The target predicate, and the draft got it wrong in a way that flips an exit code.** The draft mandated one predicate for both accepted kinds — *"reaches p" is `configStates[i].includes(p)`, never `leafOf() === p`* — and that is `state`'s observation function, not `node`'s. `atomHolds` (`atoms.ts`:341-345) reads a `state` atom as `obs.config.stack.includes(...)` and a `node` atom as `leafOf(obs.config) === ...`, and `nodesOf` (:137-151) admits **every** `StateUsage` in the machine, composites included, so the two readings diverge on any composite state. Measured this session on `state nominal; state degraded { state sub1; state sub2; transition sub1 -> sub2; } transition nominal -> degraded;` — same command, same walk, one atom spelled two ways: `--pattern "pattern=absence, scope=globally, p=node degraded"` → **PASS**, `` `node degraded` never holds, over the whole run ``; `p=state degraded` → **FAIL** with a 1-step witness.

The consequence in this section is a flipped verdict **and** a flipped exit code. Under the mandated `configStates[i].includes(p)`, `recovery p = node degraded` sees 2 target configurations and prints `recoverable`, exit 0. Under the shipped reading the target set is empty and the row is this section's own second `fail` — `not recoverable: \`node degraded\` holds at no reachable configuration at all`, `verification/refuted`, **error, exit 1**. Whichever is implemented, `recovery` and `absence` then disagree about the same atom inside one command, which is what this section's MUST-NEVER (*"a claim word rendered as a different word"*) and its *one gate, one wording* discipline exist to stop.

**So `p` is read through `atomHolds`'s own selection, never through a hand-written predicate.** §3.P retains `configLeaves` beside `configStates` — one integer array — and the arm selects `stack.includes(p)` for a `state` atom and `configLeaves[i] === p` for a `node` atom, exactly as `atomHolds` does. The selection is asserted **against `atomHolds`** on the composite fixture in commit 9's test file, on every configuration of the walk, so the two cannot drift. **The fallback, if `configLeaves` is judged not worth its array:** restrict the gate to `state` alone and refuse `node` with the reason its own kind earns — `a \`node\` atom names the active leaf, which this walk does not retain per configuration` — in this arm **and** in §3.R's dispatch, in the identical wording the plan already requires for `expression`. What is not available is keeping `node` and reading it as `state`. And **do not reuse `walk.reachable`** under either reading: it is fed from `next.effects.visited` (the entry cascade), so a state can be in it without being any configuration's leaf or on any configuration's stack.

**Verdict vocabulary.**

| Claim | Sentence | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| `pass` | `recoverable: \`state standby\` is reachable from every reachable configuration, exhaustive under {…}` + §2.4(d) when the machine has a choice point | **A7** — `walkIsExact`, and **no product-search condition**: no search runs on this path, `Publishability.searchComplete` is absent rather than false, and the gate has no field for it — and the reverse-reachable set is everything. **§2.4(a) no longer appears on this row**: the environment is clause (c) of the gate, so a machine that names triggers is refused rather than qualified |
| `fail` | `not recoverable: 4 configuration(s) cannot reach \`state standby\`: {manual, autonomous, failsafe, failsafeHold}; entered in 3 step(s). Of these, 2 form a set nothing leaves — see the \`verification/unrecoverable-mode\` row.` | A7 holds and the complement is non-empty. Refutation is monotone-**decreasing** in edges, so on the time and environment mechanisms it would survive without the gate — but **not on the store**, which removes edges and therefore manufactures this row directly (the trapguard measurement, §2.3, prints exactly this sentence at exit 1 on a design that states the escape). The row keeps the whole gate; see the note below |
| `fail` | `not recoverable: \`state standby\` holds at no reachable configuration at all — see the \`verification/unreachable-state\` row.` | A7 holds, the atom kind was accepted, **and the target set is empty for a reason other than the atom kind** |
| `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: bound exhausted — the recovery claim is not made` | **`!seenWhole`** |
| `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: this machine names triggers this walk offers at every configuration, so "reachable from every configuration" would be a claim about an environment this walk has no carrier for` + §2.4(a) | **`failedClause === 'environment'`.** Measured on the latch machine, this is the row that replaces the draft's `recoverable`, exit 0, for `recovery p = state nominal` — a `pass` about a design in which `locked → nominal` exists only because the walk supplies `unlatch` |
| `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: this machine carries \`after(n)\` dwell transitions this walk takes without advancing a clock, so "reachable from every configuration" would be a claim about escapes this walk invented` + §2.4(c) | **`failedClause === 'time'`** |
| `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: a transition of this machine is guarded by an attribute with no declared value, so this walk never offered an edge the model states and neither direction of this claim is made` + the store sentence | **`failedClause === 'store'`.** This one refuses the `fail` as well as the `pass`, which is the asymmetry note below made concrete: the store is the one mechanism that manufactures the refutation |
| `inconclusive` | `verification/malformed-property`: `a trigger is observed on a step, not at a configuration, so \`recovery\` has no target set to reverse from` | the atom kind is `trigger` or `fires` |
| `inconclusive` | `verification/malformed-property`: `this atom reads the store, which this walk does not retain` | the atom kind is `expression` — the same wording §3.R's row 4 prints |

**A note on the asymmetry, stated rather than left to the implementer.** `recoverable` is monotone-increasing and `not recoverable` is monotone-decreasing, so a strictly minimal design would gate only the first on the time and environment clauses. This section gates **both**, for two reasons and not one. First, the two sentences print from one arm under one `qualification` string, and a per-direction condition would put two different exhaustiveness promises on one row — the shape §2.2 refuses. Second, and this is the third review's finding rather than an aesthetic: **the store clause is not decreasing-safe in either direction.** An edge the model states and the walk never offered makes `not recoverable` *easier* to state, and measured on the trapguard machine it produces `not recoverable: 2 configuration(s) cannot reach \`state nominal\``, `verification/refuted`, error, **exit 1**, on a design that declares the recovery transition. A per-direction gate would have kept exactly that row. The cost of gating both is one lost refutation on a timed or triggered machine; the alternative is a row whose qualification means one thing when it says `pass` and another when it says `fail`, and a correct design at exit 1 — which is the shape §3.1 exists to abolish, re-entering through the store.

**MUST NEVER print, and why.**

- **`vacuous` when `p` is unreachable.** `AG EF p` over a non-empty reachable set with an empty target set is decidedly **false**: there is no antecedent left unopened and no scope left un-entered. Printing it as undecided would file a refutation at exit 2.
- **The bottom-SCC cardinality under the cannot-reach question** — 2 vs 4, measured.
- **A recoverability claim, in either direction, from a walk `walkIsExact` refuses** — A7. Three measured shapes, one gate: the latch machine (environment), the preempted-dwell machine (time), the trapguard machine (store).
- **A `node` atom read as a `state` atom.** Measured, the two disagree on any composite state and the disagreement flips both the verdict and the exit code; `p` is read through `atomHolds`'s own selection or `node` is refused outright.
- **A refutation whose target set is empty because the atom kind cannot be observed.** That is the atom-kind gate above, and it is a MUST-NEVER and not only a design note: it is the one path in this plan where a well-formed model earns `verification/refuted` and exit 1 from a tool limitation.
- **"the system can always recover"** — §2.4(a).
- **A claim word rendered as a different word.** The JSON says `fail` and the text says `not recoverable`; the text is a *detail*, and no reader or downstream consumer is shown two different claims for one row. **The same rule applies to numbers:** where a sentence quotes a configuration count it quotes the named `machineConfigs` field (§3.1), never the `configs` field `base` fills from the product search.

**Exit-code contract.** `behaviour`. `recoverable` counts as `pass` ⇒ 0. `not recoverable` is a refuted assertion the author wrote: `verification/refuted`, **error**, exit **1**. This is the substantive difference from `cover`, and it is the right one — `cover` asks whether the design *admits* a behaviour, `recovery` asserts that it *always* does. **`BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` does not move for this feature, and that was checked rather than assumed:** `recoverable` is a property that holds **at every reachable configuration** (`AG EF p` is configuration-wise), so the 0 row's shipped sentence is true of it as written — unlike `covered` (§3.1) and 5a's `pass` (§3.5a), which are run-wise and forced the two edits §2.1 lists. The atom-kind refusal exits **2** as `verification/malformed-property` already does.

**Tests.** The trap probe ⇒ `not recoverable` naming **4** configurations, with the trap row's **2** in its own clause. `uav-isr` with `p = state standby` ⇒ `recoverable`, exit 0 — **and `searchComplete` asserted absent, not false**, which is the register's half of the dispatch test. A machine whose `p` is unreachable ⇒ `fail` with the second sentence, **not** `vacuous`. **Reflexivity:** `recovery` with `p` = the opening state does not report the opening configuration. **Composite, and it is now a pair rather than one assertion:** `p = state degraded` on `state nominal; state degraded { state sub1; state sub2; transition sub1 -> sub2; } transition nominal -> degraded;` matches every configuration inside it, **and `p = node degraded` matches none** — asserted against `atomHolds` on every configuration of the same walk, with the `absence` pair (`node degraded` PASS / `state degraded` FAIL, both measured today) asserted in the same test as the thing the two readings must keep agreeing with. Under the fallback design the same fixture asserts `node` is refused with the leaf reason instead. What must go red either way is `recovery p = node degraded` answering `recoverable` at exit 0 while `absence p = node degraded` passes. **The dispatch test:** a `recovery` row never reaches `search`, asserted by a spy, and the arm's position asserted by a fixture with an unsupported construct ⇒ the unsupported refusal wins, never the recovery arm. **The atom-kind tests, one per refused kind:** `p=fires T`, `p=trigger abort` and `p=(mode == 3)` each ⇒ `verification/malformed-property` and **never** `verification/refuted` — with `not recoverable … holds at no reachable configuration at all` named as the wrong answer, and with the `expression` row's reason asserted **byte-identical to §3.R's row 4**, so the two sections cannot drift back apart. **The three gate fixtures, one per mechanism, with the wrong answer named in each:** the **preempted-dwell** machine — factory-built and driven through `checkProperty` in-process (correction 22) — ⇒ the time sentence, with `recoverable` named as the wrong answer; the **latch** machine, an ordinary `.sysml` file asserted through argv and in-process, with `p = state nominal` ⇒ the environment sentence, with `recoverable`, **exit 0** named as the wrong answer (measured: the walk is `2 configuration(s) … exhaustive under {…, alphabet unlatch}`, one whole-graph SCC, so the draft's A7 held and published it); the **trapguard** machine, also a `.sysml` file both ways, with `p = state nominal` ⇒ the store sentence, with `not recoverable: 2 configuration(s) cannot reach \`state nominal\``, `verification/refuted`, **exit 1** named as the wrong answer — the one gate fixture whose wrong answer is a refutation rather than a pass, and the reason the arm gates both directions. `--max-configs 2` ⇒ `inconclusive`.

**Cost.** One `PATTERNS` entry, one `PatternClass` member, one dispatch arm, one reverse-reachability pass, one atom-kind gate. **No new claim word** — it reuses `verification/refuted`, `verification/bound-exhausted` and `verification/malformed-property`. The catalogue prose moves in four documents (six patterns → eight).

**Census.** `check-behaviour --json` records, per `recovery` row, `{atomKind, targetConfigs, cannotReach, bottomSccOverlap, refusedByGate}` — the last carrying the failed clause, or `null`. The kill measurement: **if `cannotReach` is always either 0 or the whole complement of the opening's component, `recovery` is answering the question `reach`'s trap row already answers** and the pattern should be retired in favour of the structural report — which is the same escape hatch §3.2a's census offers from the other side, and the two are read together at Checkpoint A. **`refusedByGate` replaces the draft's `timeSound` and asks a wider question**: it records how often the pattern refuses and on which clause, which is the number that says whether the gate is a guard or a wall. Measured baseline from §5(d): on this tree it would read `null` on ten of the eleven machines and `'store'` on `GuardProbe::Ctrl` — no dwell and no trigger anywhere — so the honest reading today is that the gate costs `recovery` nothing on the shipped examples, that clause (d) has one corpus witness, and that clauses (b) and (c) are still fixture-only.

---

### 3.3a — `verify --why`: the axioms a proof used

**What it decides.** Nothing new. It publishes a value the tool computes on every run and throws away.

**Command and flags.** `npm run sysprose -- verify <file> [--case REF] --engine smt --why`. A flag on the existing row, so `COMMANDS.length` stays 22.

**Engine.** z3, already the `--engine smt` dependency, and **zero new solver calls.** Half the display already ships: `axioms-inconsistent` prints the core at `smt.ts`:676-678 and `vacuous` at :784, both through `coreSentence` (:1014), which **sorts** because the order was measured to move under worker-pool contention (:1003-1013). Only the `proved` path (:810-834) drops it.

**Verdict vocabulary.**

| Sentence | Condition |
|---|---|
| `proved (negation unsat, QF_LRA) using 4 of 12 axiom(s) in the core: AirVehicle::cruisePower, AirVehicle::endurance, AirVehicle::usableEnergyFraction, BatteryPack::capacity — and 1 side condition (AirVehicle::endurance, divisor non-zero)` | outcome `proved`, `negation.core` non-empty. Numerator = `axiom:` members; denominator = the **model's** axiom count (12 on `uav-isr`), not the script's |
| `the set z3 returned is sufficient, not minimal: an axiom listed here may not have been needed, and an axiom NOT listed may still carry the claim` | **every `--why` line** |
| `the core names no premise; the non-vacuity step stands on the premises separately` | premises exist and none is in the core |
| `no core: the solver named none for this check` | `core.length === 0` on unsat |

**MUST NEVER print, and why.**

- **"minimal core"** — there is no deletion loop here. `consistency.ts` owns that machinery (`minimized`, `coreLabel`, `DEFAULT_MAX_CORE = 8`, the loop at :107-118) and `--minimize` is what earns the word.
- **"the axioms this proof depends on"** / **"this proof does not depend on X".** Measured: with `mtow = 18.5`, `mtowCapA {mtow <= 20}` and `mtowCapB {mtow <= 22}` all in scope, z3's core for `mtow <= 25.0 [kg]` is `{axiom:…mtowCapB, goal:…}` — cardinality-minimal, naming the **weakest** of three sufficient reasons, and **not naming the model's own value at all.** Swapping the two caps' declaration order did not change the pick, so this is not an ordering artefact. The permitted line in `docs/05` covers the false-**positive** direction only; the measured hazard is the false-**negative** one.
- **A display filtered to the `axiom:` prefix.** R-UAV-001's core is **six** labels, not three: four `axiom:`, one `goal:`, and **`side:…AirVehicle::endurance`** — the divisor-non-zero condition on `capacity * fraction / cruisePower` (`encode.ts`:169-176, emitted at `smt.ts`:449/:658), a real proof dependency that **shares its qualified name** with an axiom. A filter drops it; a name mapping must dedupe.
- **"what the verdict stands on".** Measured on `examples/uav-power-budget.sysml`: `ComputerDraw`, `PropulsionDraw` and `RadioDraw` each assert a `premise:` and **none** of the three cores contains it — yet the non-vacuity step (`smt.ts`:768-800) that separates `proved` from `vacuous` is exactly a check on those premises.

**Exit-code contract.** Unchanged. `--why` changes what is printed and nothing about what is decided.

**No schema change here — a deliberate ordering decision.** The core is *displayed*, and `EvidenceRecord` is not touched. `docs/schemas/evidence-record.schema.json` is 184 lines of draft-07 with `additionalProperties: false` at **all ten levels**, compiled with ajv in **two** places (`test/campaign/verification.test.ts`:958 and `loadRecords` in `scripts/sysprose.ts`, which refuses the **whole** attach if any record fails), so a field added there is one-directional: a record written by the new build is refused by `evidence-attach` on an older build. It is not spent until the census that justifies it comes back positive, and it lands with 3b, which is the feature that actually needs a *recorded* core.

**It is not, however, the only forward commitment in this plan, and the draft's "only" was wrong.** Three others have the same direction and the same permanence, and all three are recorded in `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` in the commit that spends them: a `.sysml` carrying `pattern = "cover"` (commit 6) or `pattern = "recovery"` (commit 9) is refused by any older build as `verification/malformed-property` and exits 2 there — reproduced today, rc=2 — and the two `metadata def`s of commits 17 and 22 are compatibility commitments by hard constraint 6. What is true of the evidence field, and distinguishes it, is narrower: it is the only one a later commit cannot undo **by re-recording the artifact**, because the artifact is validated by ajv in two places with `additionalProperties: false`. A model file's pattern value can be edited back; a written record cannot be un-written on a build that refuses to read it.

**Tests.** `uav-isr --why`: R-UAV-001's core is the six labels, rendered as *"4 of 12 axioms plus 1 side condition"*, with the `side:` member present and deduped against the same-named axiom; R-UAV-002 is 1 of 12. `uav-power-budget --why`: BatterySupply 5 script axioms → **1** core axiom; ComputerDraw 6 + 1 premise → 1 core axiom with the **premise absent**; aggregate **24 → 9** over six obligations. The three-reasons fixture asserts the core is `{mtowCapB, goal}` **and** that the sufficiency sentence prints — the test that stops the false-negative sentence being written later. **Byte-stability across two processes**, because the tool's own comment at :1003-1013 records that the core's order already moved between a solo run and the full suite. The three `proved` fixtures' `detail` re-recorded here with the reason.

**The trap that makes this look pointless if measured in the wrong place.** On `uav-isr` the core **equals** the syntactic footprint `relevantAxioms` (`smt.ts`:643, :956-983) already computes — 4 of 4 and 1 of 1, zero further narrowing — and the four symbols are already printed as `witnessSymbols` in the golden. **Any measurement of this feature against the flagship example alone will conclude it does nothing.**

**Cost.** ~30 lines of display, one field on `SmtJudgement` (:133-156) carried to `ObligationVerdict` (`api/verification.ts`:850-898). Blast radius: the `proved` detail string (:818-825) is pinned in six places, of which only **3 of 38** verification fixtures carry a `proved` claim.

**Census.** `verify --json` records, per obligation, `{scriptAxioms, coreAxioms, footprintAxioms}` — the three numbers that decide 3b.

---

### 3.3b — Staleness scoped to a proof's core — *gated on 3a's census*

**Gate.** 3a's census shows real narrowing (core axioms < footprint axioms) on a model the user runs. Measured today: **zero** narrowing on `uav-isr`, **24 → 9** on `uav-power-budget`.

**What it decides.** Whether an edit could have invalidated *this* proof, rather than whether the model changed at all.

**The kill measurement, run, and it survives wide.** One edit at a time, whole-model digest vs a digest over the canonical form of exactly the elements the core names: `uav-isr`, 19 edits × 2 proofs — whole-model stale **38/38 = 100 %**, core-scoped **4/38 = 11 %**. `uav-power-budget`, 8 edits × 5 proofs — **40/40** vs **8/40 = 20 %**. Combined **12/78 = 15 %** against a stated 90 % kill threshold.

**And it is unsound as sketched.** Measured via the CLI: adding `assert constraint powerCap { uav.cruisePower <= 100.0 [W] }` to `uav-isr` flips **both** obligations to `verification/inconsistent-axioms` — `MassRequirement` included, whose core is `{AirVehicle::mtow}` and whose read-closure never touches `cruisePower`. Cause: **STEP 0 is global.** `smt.ts`:364 checks the satisfiability of *every* axiom in the model, once per run, and :667 makes every obligation stand on that one answer. `proved` here means *"negation unsat **under a satisfiable axiom set**"*, and the second conjunct is a whole-model property no per-proof scope can capture. `docs/05`'s MUST-NEVER list names the per-obligation vacuity hazard and **not this one**, and this one is worse: the vacuity check at least overlaps the core, and STEP 0 shares nothing with it.

**So it ships as three parts, all solver-free and inside `ValidationRule.run`'s synchronous contract (`src/validation/types.ts`:97) — and part (ii) needs a function that does not exist yet.** The draft budgeted one line, "export `relevantAxioms`". Measured, that gives `src/validation/rules.ts` nothing it can call: `relevantAxioms(axioms: readonly EncodedRow[], premises: readonly EncodedRow[], goal: EncodedRelation)` (`smt.ts`:956-960) is typed over SMT-**encoded** artefacts, `EncodedRow` (:309) is a non-exported local interface and `encodeRow` (:406) a non-exported local function, so the rule cannot construct an argument. Exporting the three of them alongside it would make every `npm run check` of any file carrying an evidence record run the encode half of the verify pipeline synchronously — and, because `RULES` is reached from `safeValidate` in `src/ui/store.ts`, would pull the SMT encoder into the **browser bundle**. That cost is real, it is not in this section's cost paragraph, and it is not paid. **What ships instead:** `footprintOf(model, obligation): ReadonlySet<ElementId>` — the same read-closure computed over `Obligation` rows at the model level, with no encoding — placed beside the obligation worklist and exported from `src/api/index.ts`. It is not asserted to be *cheaper*; it is asserted to be **the same answer**: a test runs it against `relevantAxioms`' membership on all 38 verification fixtures and requires set equality, so the two cannot drift and the rule reads the model-level one. The rejected alternative is written down here, with its bundle impact, so nobody re-derives it as a shortcut in the commit.

| Part | What it catches | Measured (uav-isr, 19 edits, EnduranceRequirement) |
|---|---|---|
| (i) canonical digest of exactly the elements the core names | an edit to a load-bearing axiom | 3/19 — misses both adversarial additions |
| (ii) the footprint **membership** recomputed on the **current** model (`relevantAxioms`) | a new axiom entering the read-closure | 5/19 — catches `assert constraint packFloor { uav.battery.capacity >= 100.0 [Wh] }`, and does **not** fire on an unrelated `+attribute txPower` |
| (iii) a digest over **STEP 0's inputs** — the set of axiom rows | the global-consistency flip measured above | on `uav-isr` all 12 axioms are feature-value bindings, so **any numeric edit anywhere moves it** |
| **(iv) a digest over this obligation's PREMISE ROWS in canonical form** — the rows `premisesByRequirement` (`smt.ts`:350-356) hands STEP 2 | **the non-vacuity check**, which is what separates `proved` from `vacuous` and which parts (i)–(iii) all miss | per-requirement, so unlike (iii) it does not fire on every numeric edit anywhere |

(ii) is KeY §9.3.1 made mechanical — *"a specification cannot possibly talk about memory entities which are only to be included in an extension"*. (iii) is the price of honesty, and it is the trade this candidate hides: it is measurable per model **before** anything is built, which is what 3a's census is for.

**(iv) is the third review's finding, and without it this feature ships a green absence claim over a proof the tool itself calls vacuous.** Part (iii) closes STEP 0 and leaves **STEP 2** open, and STEP 2 is the check that separates `proved` from `vacuous`. The premise set is in none of the other three digests: STEP 0's script is built from `axioms` alone (`smt.ts`:347-366), `relevantAxioms` returns `kept` over axioms and never returns a premise — premises only seed the symbol closure (:950-983) — and §3.3a's own measurement records premises absent from the cores (`uav-power-budget`: *"ComputerDraw 6 + 1 premise → 1 core axiom with the premise absent"*). So a premise edit that changes no symbol moves nothing (i)–(iii) look at. **Executed this session on a copy of `examples/uav-power-budget.sysml`, one edit, `assume constraint { fc.supplyVoltage >= 20.0 [V] }` → `>= 200.0 [V]`** — same feature, same symbols, no axiom touched: the run goes from `0 inconclusive, 6 discharged` with `UAVPowerBudget::ComputerDraw … proved` to `1 inconclusive, 5 discharged` with `vacuous: the premises are unsatisfiable under the axioms … verification/vacuous`. Under the three-part design the stored evidence for that obligation reports `current within this proof's scope` after that edit. §3.3b's own MUST-NEVER names the hazard (*"the non-vacuity precondition can break and turn `proved` into `vacuous`"*) and trap 18 names it again; three conjuncts that do not reach it are not a scope, they are a gap with a sentence in front of it.

**Verdict vocabulary.**

| Sentence | Condition |
|---|---|
| `current within this proof's scope — no element in its core moved, its axiom footprint is unchanged in membership, its premises are unchanged, and the model's axiom set is unchanged` | **A9**, now **four** digests unmoved rather than three |
| `stale — \`BatteryPack::capacity\` is in this proof's core and moved` | (i) moved |
| `stale — \`packFloor\` entered this proof's axiom footprint since the record was written` | (ii) moved |
| `stale — the model's axiom set changed, so the consistency check every proof in this file stands on was not the one that ran` | (iii) moved |
| `stale — this proof's premises changed, so the satisfiable-assumptions check that separated \`proved\` from \`vacuous\` was not the one that ran` | **(iv) moved** |
| `partially current — the whole-model digest moved; no element in this proof's core moved and the axiom set is unchanged in membership, so the negation is still unsat. Non-vacuity and axiom-set consistency were NOT re-checked.` | the two-tier fallback, if (iii) proves too coarse to be worth its false-positive rate on a real model |
| today's whole-model sentence, verbatim | the record carries no core (`--engine literal`, or a record written before this commit) |

**MUST NEVER print.** Plain **`current`** — the claim is scoped and the words say so. **"this edit cannot affect the claim"** — UNSAT of a subset survives, so the negation stays unsat, but the non-vacuity precondition can break and turn `proved` into `vacuous`; part (iv) is what makes that sentence checkable rather than a warning. A verdict from part (i) alone, or from any three of the four. **`current within this proof's scope` after a premise edit** — measured, one `>= 20.0 [V]` → `>= 200.0 [V]` and the obligation the record covers is `vacuous`. **"the axioms this proof used"** as a synonym for "what the verdict stands on".

**Exit-code contract.** `validation/stale-evidence` stays a **warning** under `npm run check`. **`RULES.length` stays 25** — no new rule — so the eight `docs-counts` assertions keyed on it stay green.

**Tests.** The 19-edit and 8-edit sweeps as golden tables — and, separately, the **census fields** asserted on a single run of each sweep member, because the goldens are a one-off pre-build measurement and the census is the instrumentation. `footprintOf` equals `relevantAxioms`' membership on all 38 verification fixtures, asserted as set equality. `powerCap` ⇒ **stale** via (iii), on a proof whose core it never touches. `packFloor` ⇒ stale via (ii) where (i) says current. `txPower` ⇒ **nothing fires**. **`supplyVoltage >= 20.0 [V]` → `>= 200.0 [V]` on `uav-power-budget` ⇒ stale via (iv)**, where (i), (ii) and (iii) all say current — added to the 8-edit sweep as its ninth member, and it is the row that must never read `current`; the same run asserts the obligation itself has gone `vacuous`, so the fixture pins the two halves together. A record with no core ⇒ today's sentence, verbatim. Byte-stability across two processes. `api.evidence.test.ts`:668 and `test/fixtures/agent-authoring/L8-evidence-stale/` re-recorded, deliberately. **Probe hygiene, learned expensively:** after ~130 `check` calls in one process the z3 WASM worker aborted with *"the application has corrupted its heap memory area"*; any sweep judging many variants batches across processes.

**Cost, and the one-directional commitment.** `EvidenceRecord` (`api/evidence.ts`:563-616, 14 fields, 11 required) gains **optional** core and digest fields, plus the matching schema properties. **Keep `schema` at the const `'sysprose-evidence/1'`:** bumping it makes `recordOfCarrier` (:740) treat every already-attached carrier in the wild as `unreadable`. Old records still validate against the new schema because the fields are optional — and they must be optional anyway, since `--engine literal --record` produces records with no core. A record written after this commit is refused by `evidence-attach` on any build before it, and that is stated in the commit message and in `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` as a measured fact. Second commitment: `canonicalElement` (:414) is private and `canonicalElements` (:280) returns an **unkeyed** sorted array behind a comment that deliberately narrows it (*"EXPORTED FOR THE GUARD, not for callers"*), so a keyed per-element export is required against that comment — the one API-shape argument in this lane. Plus `footprintOf` and its equality test against `relevantAxioms` (above), which is the part the draft mis-sized.

**Census — and it is per-run, not per-edit, which is the correction.** §5's table promised commit 11 would record a "whole-model vs core-scoped vs three-part stale **rate over edits**". That quantity cannot be a census field under §2.5's own rule — every census is `--json`-only and lands inside the existing payload object — because a rate over edits is longitudinal and one `npm run check -- model.sysml --json` sees a single model version and emits `{ok, files: CheckReport[]}` (`sysml-check.ts`:141). Left as written, this plan's only one-directional schema commitment would have been spent with **no artifact from which "the second digest earns nothing" could ever be read back**, so the commitment could neither be justified nor unwound by data. What ships instead: `staleEvidence` emits, inside the check payload and **per evidence record**, `{wholeModelMoved, coreMoved, footprintMembershipMoved, axiomSetMoved, premisesMoved}` — five booleans, no new top-level key. The >90 %-agreement kill measurement is then an **aggregation over recorded runs**, exactly the way commit 8's trap census turns "three consecutive real models" into a query. §5's row for commit 11 is corrected to match.

---

### 3.4a — Inheritance: disclose the clause a child did not write

**What it decides.** Nothing new. It discloses what the tool already withholds.

**The defect, reproduced on a 20-line probe.** `contracts` prints `SpecInherit::StrictMassLimit [RequirementDefinition]` / `subject v : Vehicle (inherited)` / `require v.topSpeed <= 60 [m/s]` — **one** guarantee — and does not list the inherited `v.mass <= 1500 [kg]`. `verify --engine literal` reports the child's own clause and refutes the parent separately (exit 1, so the run is not silently green). `clausesInheritedFrom` is populated only on the branch `clauses.length === 0 ? inheritedClauseOwners(model, el) : []` (`contracts.ts`:1085). **The asymmetry:** the same block already prints `(inherited)` for the *subject* — the tool owns the disclosure idiom and withholds it from clauses only.

**Command and flags.** `contracts <file> [--element REF] [--json]` — unchanged.

**The change.** Drop the ternary at :1085 so `clausesInheritedFrom` is populated unconditionally; `ContractClause` (**:193-209** — :193-220 as first written runs into `ContractSubject`'s docstring) gains `origin: 'declared' | 'inherited'` (the word already exists on `ContractSubject` at :233); add one renderer line.

**Safe at three of four consumers by guard order, measured.** `obligations.ts`:487 (`if (withClause.has(contract.id)) continue;`) runs **before** :494's `clausesInheritedFrom.length > 0` skip; `api/verification.ts`:418 tests `assumptions.length + guarantees.length === 0` first; `scripts/sysprose.ts`:1124 `emptyClauseLine` is called only from :1243 under the same emptiness test. The **fourth** — `diagram/contracts-table.ts`:114 → `ui/panels/ContractsTable.tsx`:84-86 — renders `clauses filed on X` unconditionally and needs its string split in two. That is the only behaviour change in this commit.

**Verdict vocabulary.** `2 guarantee(s): 1 declared, 1 inherited from MassLimit` when the contract owns clauses and inherits some; today's `no clause of its own; clauses filed on MassLimit` when it owns none. Reports only — never a truth verdict.

**MUST NEVER.** Show two clauses without saying where the second came from. Say **"redefines"** where the names do not mask — `effectiveFeatures` masks by `declaredName` (`inheritance.ts`:70-74) and the shipped idiom `require constraint { … }` builds an **anonymous** ConstraintUsage, for which masking never fires. Say **"behavioural subtype"** — KeY p.220: *"Respecting inherited specifications is a good practice, but it does not guarantee behavioral subtyping per se."* Change what enters the worklist: nothing does here.

**Exit-code contract.** `report`. Nothing moves.

**Tests.** The probe in **both** spellings (bare-Real and `[kg]`), asserting identical obligation digests — measured identical, because `obligationDigest` is over the SI-lowered normal form. The five tests that pin today's non-inheritance behaviour stay **green, unchanged**: `api.verification.test.ts`:274, `contracts-table.test.ts`:140, `ui.contracts-table.test.ts`:156, `campaign/cli.sysprose.test.ts`:1200, `property.test.ts`:455. **Zero of 120 goldens move** — measured: no model in every `.sysml` file in the tree inherits a non-empty clause, and the 37 verification goldens are a field-selected projection carrying no clause-origin field. Plus `npm run test:e2e` (touches `src/ui/**`).

**Cost.** One ternary, one field, one renderer line, one UI string split, one census. **Do not build `verification/inherited-clause-not-read` as the cheap fallback:** measured, it fires on **0 of every model in the tree**  while costing a code bump, a `npm run codes` regeneration and a `docs-counts` claim. The disclosure costs less and says more.

**Census — this is the deliverable.** `contracts --json` gains `{contractsWithInheritedClauses, byEdgeKind: {Subclassification, FeatureTyping}, namedClausesMasked, anonymousClausesInherited}`. Measured today across every `.sysml` file in the tree: **0, 0/0, 0, 0**. The one typed usage in the corpus (`test/fixtures/agent-authoring/L4-requirement-subject-inherited/input.sysml`:8) inherits a **subject**, not a clause. The `byEdgeKind` split is the whole gate for 4b.

---

### 3.4b — `refine --via specialize` — **HELD**

**Release condition.** 4a's census reports `contractsWithInheritedClauses ≥ 1` **by Subclassification**, with `namedClausesMasked ≥ 1`, on a real model.

**What it would decide.** Whether `requirement def X :> Y` is a contract refinement: parent assumption ⊨ child assumption, child guarantees ⊨ parent guarantees — the two obligations `judgeDerivation` (`refinement.ts`:**1180**, docstring **:1156-1179**) already computes as `A_R ∧ γ ⊨ A_D` and `A_R ∧ ⋀ nf(C_D) ∧ γ ⊨ G_R`.

**Command.** `refine <file> [--element REF] --via specialize` — a new **value** of an existing flag (`VIA_VALUES`, `scripts/sysprose.ts`:2072), never a subcommand. It moves the `--via` doc string (`sysprose-spec.ts`:707-710), the error message at :2087, the byte-pinned `CLI-REFERENCE.md`, the family pin at `cli-reference.test.ts`:181-191, and `cli.sysprose.test.ts`:2053 whose **name** says "the four families".

**Engine.** z3. Orientation is measured, not guessed: `requirement def X :> Y` stores `Subclassification src=X tgt=Y` — the child points at the parent.

**MAY print.** `specialization refines: the child assumes no more and guarantees at least as much — over 1 declared clause; 1 inherited clause was not re-checked` · `not a refinement: StrictMassLimit redefines \`massOk\` and weakens it — witness v.mass = 1600 kg` · `not evaluated: both clauses are anonymous, so redefinition cannot be expressed and no weakening could have been detected` · `vacuous` · `inconclusive`.

**MUST NEVER.** **"behavioural subtype."** · Any verdict over `origin === 'inherited'` clauses — if the inventory folds the parent's guarantees into the child, the guarantee obligation becomes true by construction wherever masking did not fire: a pass manufactured by the tool's own bookkeeping. · Any claim that the check *can* refute on the shipped idiom — **weakening is inexpressible without a clause name**, so on anonymous clauses it can only ever pass, and a check that cannot fail on real input is vacuous. That fact goes on the verdict line, not in a footnote.

**Three traps, all measured, and the second is why inherited clauses stay on the display side forever.**

- **Double-filing.** `generalizationsOf` follows the whole specialization family **including FeatureTyping**, so `requirement massOk : MassLimit;` — the OMG's ordinary apply-shape — returns the definition's clauses too. Gate on the edge **kind**, not on `effectiveFeatures` alone. A test asserts that shape files exactly one obligation.
- **Obligation identity collides.** `obligationDigest` (`api/evidence.ts`:473-483) is over the SI-lowered normal form and nothing else; the evidence key is `clause` + `obligationDigest` (:830); an inherited clause's `clause` is the **parent's** qualified name, because the ConstraintUsage lives in the parent's body. `evidence-attach` would write the child's evidence into the parent's braces. Fixing that by adding the owning requirement to the digest input moves **every digest in the tree** — all 37 verification goldens and every stored record stale at once.
- **Masking is by bare name, with no `redefines` keyword required.** Two clauses sharing a `declaredName` across a chain mask silently, and `effectiveFeatures` returns the **survivor** — which is the *weaker* one, i.e. the violation to report. Whatever prints must name the **masked** clause and its owner, not only the survivor.

**Exit-code contract.** `refine`, and **no clause of `REFINE_EXIT_CODES` moves for this feature.** Every verdict above lands in a bucket that already exists: a refinement counts as `refined` ⇒ 0; `not a refinement` is `notRefined` ⇒ 1 with a witness the tool re-read, which is exactly what the published 1 row promises; `not evaluated` (both clauses anonymous) and `vacuous` are `inconclusive`/`vacuous` ⇒ 2, and neither is lowerable. This is stated here rather than assumed, because §3.6 shares the `--via specialize` value and its verdict does **not** land in an existing bucket (see there).

**Tests, if built.** The `--via` family pin and `cli.sysprose.test.ts`:2053 re-recorded with the fifth value · the FeatureTyping apply-shape files exactly **one** obligation · the anonymous-clause case prints the could-not-have-been-detected sentence and exits 2, never 0 · a masked named clause names the **masked** clause and its owner, not the survivor · **no `obligationDigest` in the tree moves**, asserted across all 37 verification goldens · a verdict is refused over any `origin === 'inherited'` clause, asserted by the absence of the parent's qualified name in the obligation list.

**Cost.** Low code, **high blast radius**: `contractsOf`/`contractOf` has 9 production callers outside `contracts.ts` and sits under 9 of 22 subcommands; `Contract.guarantees`/`assumptions` is read at 16 sites in 6 files. New code: `verification/specialization-weakens` (error) — which must join `VERIFICATION_ERROR_CODES` (`api/verification.ts`:1078) in the same commit or `diagnostic-codes.test.ts`:71-74 goes red on landing.
