### 3.7a — The signature census

**What it decides.** Whether there is a dependency template in these models to read at all.

**Why the proposed check cannot be built as written.** The Dependency Template is **intra-procedure**: transposed, *"this part's `in` port may be used inside it to obtain that `out` port"*. Every candidate source of that relation reads **zero** on `examples/uav-isr.sysml` (correction 11), and the connector graph is the **composition** of the parts' templates across boundaries — never any part's own — which is why its longest directed path is **1 hop** with **0** intra-part edges. With DT = ∅ the arithmetic is 16 findings over 15 ports on `uav-isr`, 4/4 on `vehicle`, 6/6 on `views-tour`: **100 % of ports flagged on every model in the tree.** A check that fires on everything reports nothing. And the obvious retreat — "a part with an empty export set fails condition 2" — flags 1 pure source (`BatteryPack`) and 1 pure sink (`PropulsionUnit`); in the book's setting an export-less procedure is a bug, in SysML a leaf actuator with `in` ports and no `out` ports is a correct model.

**A validation rule is ruled out outright.** All six shipped examples check **0 errors / 0 warnings / 0 info**, measured. A condition-1/2 rule emits 26 findings across three of them and breaks that guard on contact. If it ever ships it is a **report**.

**Command and flags.** `npm run sysprose -- connectivity <file> [--signature]` — a **mode on the existing command**, never a subcommand. The cost comparison is the argument: a flag adds one entry to `connectivity`'s (today empty) `flags` array, turns `backedBy` into the `fn1 + fn2 (path)` form the README parser already accepts (`readme.test.ts`:280-289), and moves **no count guard**. A subcommand moves `COMMANDS.length` 22→23, two `docs-counts` claims, a README row in order, and the generated exit-contract sentence — and would then have to explain why two commands disagree about what a port is.

**What it reports (`--json`, inside the `connectivity` object).**

```
census.signature: {
  partsWithBoundaryPortsAndInternalParts,   // uav-isr 0 · vehicle 1 (degenerate)
  sameOwnerPortRelations,                   // 0
  directedFeaturesInPortDefs,               // 0
  partsOwningBehaviour,                     // 0 of 14
  userItemFlows,                            // 0
  nonLibraryBindingClasses,                 // 0
  constraintParametersCountedAsPorts,       // views-tour 4 of 6
  inoutPorts                                // uav-isr 1 (DataLink::antenna)
}
```

Plus, on the text path, the realised-connection line: `9 of 9 declared connections realised; uav.battery.powerOut → uav.gimbal.powerIn in 1 hop`, and — on **every** verdict, findings or not — `structural only — a \`connect\` is not a guarantee that anything is transported`.

**MUST NEVER.** Call it verification. Call a port an anomaly. Read a bare `connect` as value flow — that is `refine`'s standing refusal (`refinement.ts`:187, :845; measured, all 9 edges file as `verification/unsupported-expression … a connection is not an equality`), and two commands contradicting each other on the same nine edges is not an option. Read `attrs.direction` — `portFacets` (`rules-connection.ts`:60-70) is the only correct reading in the tree: declared-or-inherited, **net conjugation** where `~` cancels twice, plus the PortDefinition generalization closure. Present the port inventory as a port inventory: on `views-tour`, 4 of 6 "ports" are `in cap` / `in p` constraint parameters and all four reported unconnected ports are those parameters.

**One defect in `connectivity`'s own inventory that this census surfaces, and one that is already fixed and is this section's baseline.** `analytics.ts` reads the graph through `CONNECTION_KINDS` (8 kinds) while `connectors.ts` uses `CONNECTOR_KINDS` (10) — the delta is `BindingConnector` and `ItemFlow` — and that one is reported as a **census row**, not as a finding.

**The three false danglers on `vehicle.sysml` were FIXED at `c37eb00`, and this section's job is to hold the fix rather than to report the defect.** An earlier revision filed them as a `--json` census row named for the enumeration defect, and that was the wrong instrument: both halves of the contradiction printed on the **default text path with no flag** — `every declared port is wired` immediately above an `unconnected port usages` block listing three ports the `connections` block two lines earlier showed wired — so a `--json` census row would have neither removed nor qualified them, and after the census landed `connectivity` would still have contradicted itself in its default output on a shipped example. The mechanism was the one the mission brief named: the wired side keyed on `${ownerId}\0${liftImplicitEndpoint(model, id)}` with the port lifted and the owner raw, while the occurrence side keyed on `${o.part.id}\0${o.port.id}` off `portOccurrences`, which enumerates declaration-level usages. The fix normalised both sides the same way, lifting an implicit part copy back to the declaration exactly as the port already was. **Re-executed at `dc6662c`:** `connectivity examples/vehicle.sysml` prints `4 port(s), 2 connection(s), 4 connected, 0 unconnected`, the two connections, `every declared port is wired`, and `4 endpoint(s) lifted onto the port they redefine; 8 library and 4 re-derived candidate(s) excluded` — **no `unconnected port usages` block**, exit 0. The report also carries `ConnectivityReport.unreconciledPorts`, which is the fix's own standing check that the two readings agree; it is `[]` on the examples. What this section owes it is a **baseline, not a census row**: §3.7a's tests re-run that behaviour and assert it did not move, §3.7b's condition-2 fixture asserts against the fixed enumeration, and both cite `unreconciledPorts` by name rather than asserting the absence of a sentence.

**Exit-code contract.** `report`, exit 0. Two hard constraints: the **default first line stays byte-identical** (four `docs-counts` claims regex it off `USER-GUIDE.md`:503 — measured `15 port(s), 9 connection(s), 14 connected, 1 unconnected`), and the `--json` top level stays exactly `['connectivity','file','ok']`.

**Tests.** The four `docs-counts` figures and the campaign JSON key list re-run **unchanged**. The census values pinned on all six examples. The `inout` port counted once in each of import and export. The constraint parameters labelled as parameters. **The `c37eb00` baseline asserted rather than the defect re-reported:** on `examples/vehicle.sysml`, `unconnectedPortUsages` is **empty** and `unreconciledPorts` is **empty**, and `every declared port is wired` is asserted never to appear in the same output as an `unconnected port usages` block — on any example, which is the invariant rather than the instance. All three hold at `dc6662c` before this commit is written, so each is a **regression** assertion; a commit that reports them as new findings is reporting somebody else's fix. `uav-isr`'s `15 port(s), 9 connection(s), 14 connected, 1 unconnected` first line is asserted **byte-identical** through this commit.

**Cost.** ~60 lines, one flag, one `ConnectivityReport` nested field, one `portFacets` export.

---

### 3.7b — Conditions 1 and 2 — **HELD**

**Release condition.** `partsWithBoundaryPortsAndInternalParts ≥ 1` **and** a non-empty dependency template derivable from that part. Measured today: **1**, and degenerate — `Vehicle` has 1 `in` port, 2 internal parts and **no `out` port at all**.

**MAY print, if built.** `condition 2 not satisfied: in port \`radio.videoIn\` influences no out port of \`DataLink\`` · `conditions 1 and 2 not evaluated for DataLink: the model states no dependency inside it` · the structural-only line on every verdict.

**MUST NEVER.** Say **which side is wrong** — the book's own triage (p.159): *"perhaps the code is correct and only the signature should be adjusted."* The finding says the declaration and the structure **disagree**. Let an `inout` port satisfy both conditions through itself — `DataLink::antenna` sits in import and export at once and is *simultaneously* the one genuinely dangling port `connectivity` already reports; it will be the first port any implementation gets wrong, in both directions. Mix granularities in one sentence — `connectivity` keeps `unconnectedPorts` (per declaration) and `unconnectedPortUsages` (per occurrence) strictly apart, and the comment block at `analytics.ts`:1278-1300 exists because collapsing them hid real dangling ends. Claim **conditions 3 and 4**: both presuppose a procedure *activation* — an entry at which an `out` parameter is undefined and a program order over which it becomes defined — and a SysML part is a continuously existing structure with no entry. The nearest analogue is an `action def` with directed parameters, of which there are **0 across all six examples**. `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` records "3 and 4 not transposed" as a **declared limit**; silence would read as coverage.

**One design axis to state rather than leave to silence — and the warrant for half of it is a page nobody opened.** The axis: an author-written dependency annotation (SPARK's `DERIVES` is the canonical example) versus a template derived from the code. `docs/05` puts Sysprose on the derived side and rules out new vocabulary — and on this corpus there is nothing to derive from. **Candidate 7 as written cannot be built without either contradicting its own design axis or waiting for models with internal structure behind boundary ports.**

**What goes in `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` is the measured half only:** *"there is no intra-part dependency relation in these models to derive a template from, so conditions 1 and 2 are not evaluated and conditions 3 and 4 are not transposed."* The SPARK contrast **stays out of the conformance record**, because its only warrant in this lane is `docs/05` §5 item 5 — book pp.153-154, listed by `docs/05`'s own author as a supporting passage they did not verify — and `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` is the record readers treat as measured. The axis sentence keeps its place *here*, in the plan, labelled: *SPARK's `DERIVES` is cited from `docs/05` §5 item 5, unverified on the page; the design decision does not rest on it, and the measurement on this tree does not speak to it.* The failure direction is conservative either way — no verdict is affected — so the fix is to put the unverified sentence where an unverified sentence belongs, not to re-derive a book page to justify a hold. §0's accounting is corrected to match: item 5 **is** reused in this plan's prose, with the label, and nowhere in a shipped doc.

**Exit-code contract, if built.** `report`, exit **0 with findings**, exactly as §3.7a. A structural disagreement between a declared signature and the connector graph is not a refutation and never spends the 1 — which is also why it is a `connectivity` mode and never a `ValidationRule` (a rule would break the six examples' 0/0/0 guard on contact, measured).

**Tests, if built.** The structural-only line on **every** verdict, findings or not · the `inout` self-dependency excluded in both directions, on `DataLink::antenna`, which is simultaneously the one genuinely dangling port `connectivity` already reports · a bare `connect` never read as value flow, asserted against `refine`'s census on the same nine edges · per-port and per-occurrence granularities never mixed in one sentence · **and the `vehicle.sysml` regression, against the `c37eb00` tree**: the three false danglers were an enumeration defect in `portOccurrences` that `c37eb00` fixed (§0.1, §3.7a), so a condition-2 finding built on that enumeration does not inherit it — and the fixture asserts the fixed baseline (`unconnectedPortUsages` empty, `unreconciledPorts` empty, no condition-2 finding on any of the three) rather than asserting the absence of something this commit never produced. An earlier revision of this fixture could not have passed: it promised a regression against a defect no commit in §4 fixes, because the fix is not in §4 at all.

---

### 3.5a — Liveness on an acyclic graph — **HELD**

**Release condition.** Commit 3's census reports ≥1 machine with **`acyclic === true` on a walk where `walkIsExact` holds** — the three-valued field of §2.3, which reads `null` whenever the gate fails, so neither an unsupported-construct walk over an empty relation nor a machine that is acyclic only because a guard was unbound can flip this gate. Measured today: **0 of 3** shipped; 2 of every `.sysml` file in the tree, both `off -> on` fixtures whose terminal configuration is already a `verification/deadlock` row.

**What it would decide.** `existence` and `response` on a machine whose reachable configuration graph is acyclic, where every run is finite and no fairness assumption is needed.

**It is not an acceptance flip.** The refusal returns **above** the `const walk = exploreMachine(…)` call in `checkProperty`, so today a liveness row carries `configs: 0, exhaustive: false, witness: [], activated: false` — no graph is ever built. 5a is *walk first, then classify*: **move the class check below the `exploreMachine` call and below the unsupported-construct refusal that guards it** (the block that refuses when `walk.unsupported.length > 0`), and decide only when the walk is exhaustive **and** `acyclic === true`. The instruction names the two constructs it must sit between and no position; an earlier revision named a line, and the line held a comment.

**It must ship a finite-run semantics, because the engine has none.** `patterns.ts` reasons only about finite **bad prefixes**; `existence`/`response` over finite maximal runs is not merely unimplemented, it is **undefined** here. The commitment, printed on the verdict line and added to the semantic profile as a seventh field — **and it defines two run classes, not one**, because 5b's fair arm needs the second and the draft's single-class wording would have made 5b's own refutation a sentence about a run its profile says does not exist:

> This engine admits two classes of run, and every liveness verdict says which one it is about. A **finite maximal run** is a finite path from the opening configuration ending at a configuration nothing leaves; it is the only class the acyclic arm (5a) uses. An **ω-run** is an infinite path, presented as a *lasso* — a finite prefix plus a named cycle; it is admitted only by the fair arm (5b), only over a declared `FairnessAssumption`, and only on a cyclic graph. `F p` is refuted by a finite maximal run on which `p` never holds, or by a fair lasso whose cycle never holds `p`; a `response` is refuted by either class carrying a `p` with no later `s`.

**The seventh field is named `run classes`, and adding it moves `reach`'s published output as well — which the draft did not say and which is not free.** `SEMANTIC_PROFILE` is **one** constant, rendered into **both** reports — `patterns.ts`:1416 and `explore.ts`:916 — so a seventh field changes `reach`'s text and JSON in a commit whose lane is liveness. That is taken here as a **deliberate re-record** rather than dodged with a behaviour-only profile extension, because a reader who runs `reach` and `check-behaviour` on the same machine must not be shown two different readings of it. The price is named in commit 16 rather than discovered on landing — three **exact six-element arrays**, each becoming the same seven-element array ending `…, 'deferred events', 'time', 'run classes'`:

- `test/campaign/cli.sysprose.test.ts` — the `expect(body.reach.profile.map((f) => f.field)).toEqual([…])` array
- `test/campaign/cli.sysprose.test.ts` — the `expect(body.behaviour.profile.map((f) => f.field)).toEqual([…])` array, **a different `it(…)` some hundreds of lines further down; the two are found by their expression and not by their position, and an earlier revision pointed the second at a `--max-configs` refusal case that has no profile array in it**
- `test/unit/semantics.mc.reach.test.ts` — the `expect(r.profile.map((f) => f.field)).toEqual([…])` array, inside `it('carries the semantic profile on the report')`

plus the `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` paragraph whose prose reads *"names **six** things every published formalisation of state machines disagrees about"* and then enumerates them — grep for that sentence; it becomes **seven**, with the run classes spelled out beside `time`. And `reach`'s text report grows one row under *"semantic profile (the reading every figure above holds under)"* for a distinction no `reach` verdict uses — the cost of one constant serving two reports, stated rather than absorbed.

**Where the two classes touch the exit contract.** `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES`' 1 row spends the 1 on *"a witness trace of a run this semantics admits"*, and after this commit that sentence is true of both classes — which is the whole reason the profile field names both rather than committing to finiteness and then contradicting itself two lines later on 5b's lasso row.

**Verdict vocabulary.**

**Evaluated top to bottom, like §3.R's — the precedence is part of the specification.**

| # | Claim | Sentence | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `inconclusive` | today's refusal sentence, **verbatim** | `acyclic !== true`, or **`!seenWhole`** — every cyclic machine, and every walk not seen whole |
| 2 | `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: this machine names triggers, so "every maximal run" would be a claim about an environment this walk has no carrier for — see \`FairnessAssumption\`` | acyclic, `seenWhole`, **`failedClause === 'environment'`** — a real trigger, not a dwell |
| 3 | `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: this machine carries \`after(n)\` dwell transitions this walk takes without advancing a clock, so both "every maximal run" and "a run on which \`p\` never holds" are claims about runs the interpreter may never take` + §2.4(c) | acyclic, `seenWhole`, **`failedClause === 'time'`**, **and** the surviving verdict is either a `pass` or a `fail` **whose witness crossed one of those dwell transitions** (**W4**, and the per-step test is `afterDuration(step.transition)`, never the recorded label — §2.3). **No `FairnessAssumption` pointer** — a dwell is not a trigger an environment can withhold |
| 3b | `inconclusive` | `inconclusive: a transition of this machine is guarded by an attribute with no declared value, so this walk never offered an edge the model states and a run it calls maximal may not be` + the store sentence | acyclic, `seenWhole`, **`failedClause === 'store'`**. A missing edge is what makes a configuration look terminal, so the store manufactures a *maximal run* directly — it belongs in this dispatch for exactly the reason the other two do, and the draft had no row for it |
| 4 | `pass` | `holds on every finite maximal run — the configuration graph is acyclic, so every run of this machine ends and no fairness assumption was needed` + §2.4(d) when the machine has a choice point | **A10** — `walkIsExact` ∧ `acyclic === true` |
| 5 | `fail` | `refuted — finite maximal run of N step(s) on which \`state done\` never holds` + §2.4(d) | **W4**, a **maximality** row. Rows 1–3b passed, and a refuting finite maximal run was found — i.e. **`seenWhole`** ∧ `acyclic === true` ∧ (`relationIsTheMachines` ∨ the per-step disjunction of §2.3 **over the over-approximating clauses only**, clause (d) having been refused outright at row 3b). Row 1 spells the first conjunct `!seenWhole` and this row spells it `seenWhole`; they are the same field, and no row in this section names a `PC` |

**Why rows 2 and 3 are two rows.** `bounds.alphabet` **is** `machineAlphabet`, which contains every `after(n)` label by design (`explore.ts`:277), so the draft's single `alphabet.length > 0` row would print *"this machine names triggers"* about a machine whose only labelled transitions are dwells, and then send the author to `FairnessAssumption` for a hazard §2.4(c) says is not a fairness question at all. Worse downstream: §3.5b's carrier *"addresses states and triggers, never transitions"* and its census counts `fairTriggers`, so a dwell label recorded as a fair trigger would inflate Checkpoint B's `domainSize` — the one number the whole carrier is retired on. The split is by subtraction, and §3.R takes the identical split.

**MUST NEVER print, and why.**

- **A `pass` on a triggered machine.** A10 reads `walkIsExact`, whose clause (c) is `alphabet.length === 0`, for exactly the reason the rider's `guaranteed` does: on a triggered machine the real environment can stall the run wherever it stops offering, and `F p` can then fail on a run this walk never considered maximal. Making the claim in one lane and refusing it in the other would be the plan contradicting itself. **The refutation direction needs no restriction of the ENVIRONMENT, and that asymmetry is sound — but the draft over-generalised it, and this is the correction:** any prefix of a `p`-free run is `p`-free, so an environment that *offers less* either admits the same refuting run or stalls earlier on a `p`-free maximal run of its own. Refutation transfers across **withheld triggers**; the universal claim does not. **It does not transfer across a preempted dwell**, and that is a different mechanism entirely: under a dwell the interpreter does not stall, it takes a **different edge**, and that edge may satisfy the property. Worked, factory-built (correction 22): `{trigger:'after(5)'} idle → done` declared before `{trigger:'after(60)'} idle → stuck`, `stuck` terminal, graph acyclic, walk exhaustive, `boundHit 'none'` — **`seenWhole` holds and only `relationIsTheMachines` fails**, and `existence (state done) globally` finds the walk-only maximal run `idle → stuck` and prints `fail — refuted — finite maximal run of 1 step(s)` at **exit 1**, on a design where `runStateMachine` fires `enabled[0]` at every advance (`execute.ts`:1305-1308) and `matches` tests the dwell only on the `timeout` branch (`config.ts`:601-604), so `stuck` is entered on **no run**. The existence of a `p`-free maximal run is monotone-**increasing** in the edge relation. Hence row 3, hence **W4**, and hence the same clause on 5b's lasso.

- **A `fail` whose witness run crossed a dwell transition** — W4, and row 3 is what prints instead. The `fail` row is a positive claim over the retained relation, not a bare absence, and the draft's MUST-NEVER list gated it on exhaustiveness alone.
- **A `fail` on a non-exhaustive walk.** A refutation of `F p` asserts that the witness run *has no successors*, which is an absence claim wearing a witness's clothes: on a truncated walk a configuration whose successors were dropped at a bound is indistinguishable from a terminal one. **Both directions of a maximal-run verdict read `seenWhole`** — which is why W4's `kind` is `maximality` and why it reads the whole gate rather than the relation half every existential witness reads (§2.3).
- **A liveness verdict on a cyclic graph without a declared assumption** — that is 5b, and the refusal at :1122-1146 exists to prevent exactly this.
- **A verdict that does not name its run class.** After this commit the engine admits two, and a `refuted` line that says only "run" leaves the reader to guess whether the witness is finite or a lasso.
- **"every run of this machine terminates"** beside a deadlock row without the clause tying them. Measured, on both acyclic machines in this tree the terminal configuration is **already** reported as *"no way out … not marked final"*. Both are true; printed together without a sentence tying them they read as the tool contradicting itself. **The clause is conditional on there being such a row** — a machine ending at a properly marked final state has none, and pointing at absent evidence is its own defect.
- **"deadlock-free"**, ever.

**Exit-code contract.** `behaviour`. `pass` ⇒ 0; `fail` ⇒ 1; both inconclusive rows ⇒ 2, and neither is lowerable by any flag. **And `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` moves again here, in both directions** (§2.1): the **0** row gains *"…or, on an acyclic graph, on every maximal run"*, because `pass` here holds over runs and not over configurations and the shipped 0 sentence would otherwise be false of it; and the **2** row's clause *"a liveness pattern no bad-prefix search decides"* narrows to *"a liveness pattern on a graph that is cyclic or was not seen whole"*, because this commit decides one. Both edits are asserted in `cli-reference.test.ts`'s `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` block at :206-214, and `verification/behaviour-unsupported-construct`'s "not lowerable by `--allow-inconclusive`" promise must survive both.

**Tests.** An acyclic mission machine ⇒ `pass` with the acyclic sentence **and** the deadlock row asserted in the same output; a variant ending at a marked final state ⇒ `pass` **without** the deadlock clause; a dead-ending variant ⇒ `fail` with the maximal-run witness; the same machine with a trigger ⇒ the environment refusal (row 2); every cyclic machine in the corpus ⇒ today's refusal text asserted **byte-identically** (row 1); `--max-configs 2` on the acyclic fixture ⇒ today's sentence, never a pass and never a fail.

**And one already-passing test flips on landing, which the draft did not name.** In `test/unit/semantics.mc.patterns.test.ts`, the `it(…)` that loops `PATTERNS.filter(p => p.kind === 'liveness')` over `buildChain()` — `A -a→ B -b→ C -c→ D` plus an orphan `E` — runs on a machine that is **acyclic and exhaustive with `bounds.alphabet = ['a','b','c']`**, which is precisely the one shape §3.5a's *"byte-identical on every cyclic machine in the corpus"* promise does not cover. After the move that row takes **row 2** rather than today's refusal. The block is identified by that loop and by `buildChain`, never by a line: it has moved twice already, and an earlier revision of this table was **off by one against it**, which mattered because the off-by-one re-recorded a `v.code` control and left a `v.detail` assertion in place — a red gate on this commit's own test. Re-measured at `dc6662c`, the assertions inside the loop, **in source order**, and what each does:

| Assertion, in the order it appears in the loop | Today | After commit 16 |
|---|---|---|
| `expect(v.claim, …).toBe('inconclusive')` | passes | **unchanged** — row 2 is still a refusal to decide |
| `expect(v.code).toBe(BEHAVIOUR_UNSUPPORTED_CODE)` | passes | **unchanged** |
| `expect(v.code).not.toBe('verification/unsupported-construct')` | passes | **unchanged — a CONTROL, and it must not be touched**: it is what keeps the catalogue's *"not lowerable by `--allow-inconclusive`"* promise true across the move, and an earlier revision re-recorded this line by miscount |
| `expect(v.detail).toContain('liveness not checked in-process')` | passes | **re-recorded** to `toContain('names triggers')` |
| `expect(v.detail).toContain('fairness')` | passes | **re-recorded** to `toContain('FairnessAssumption')` — `toContain` is case-sensitive and row 2's sentence carries the carrier's name, not the bare word. **This is the SECOND `detail` assertion, not the first**; leaving it in place while re-recording only the one above it is exactly how this commit reddens on its own gate |
| `expect(v.patternClass).toBe('liveness')` | passes | **unchanged** |
| `expect(v.configs).toBe(0)`, `expect(v.exhaustive).toBe(false)` | passes, under the comment *"no walk was paid for"* | **unchanged, deliberately** — `configs` and `exhaustive` are the **product-search** fields (`base` fills `configs` from `found.configs`) and a refused liveness row still runs **no** product search, so `0` / `false` remain true of it. The walk the row now pays for is reported through `machineConfigs` (§3.1's named field), which is the whole reason that field exists. The comment beside them is re-worded to *"a walk was paid for; no product search was"* |

The rule this table is written under: **re-record by the assertion's text, never by its position.** An implementer greps for `'liveness not checked in-process'` and for the `toContain('fairness')` beside it, and changes those two.

The two `detail` re-records are the commit's only assertion movement in that file, and they are named here so the diff reads as a re-record and not a fixup. **The three semantic-profile arrays and the USER-GUIDE prose** listed above move in the same commit, for the seventh field.

**Cost.** ~50 lines of engine. **The documentation is the work:** the refusal sentence is quoted in several places outside the source, and the *Files:* list is built by **grepping for the sentence** rather than from this enumeration — measured at `dc6662c` the sites are `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md` (generated, twice), `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` (the liveness walkthrough and its surrounding prose), `docs/CONFORMANCE.md`, `docs/DIAGNOSTIC-CODES.md` (generated, in `verification/behaviour-unsupported-construct`'s entry — **not** in `verification/bound-exhausted`'s, which an earlier revision pointed at), and `docs/AGENT-AUTHORING-CAMPAIGN.md` — plus the `--pattern` help row in `sysprose-spec.ts` whose text says the last two "report inconclusive", plus the liveness-pair guard in `cli-reference.test.ts`. Every one becomes conditionally false, and `verification/behaviour-unsupported-construct`'s published entry — explicitly **not** lowerable by `--allow-inconclusive` — changes meaning while that promise must survive the edit.

---

### 3.5b — A declared, checked fairness assumption, and fair-SCC — **HELD**

**Release condition.** 5a has shipped, and a real model carries a cyclic machine with a liveness question **and** an assumption an author can write that carries information. Measured warning about its own value: `bounds.alphabet` is `[]` on **every** machine in the tree (§5(d)), so environment justice has an empty domain exactly where it is most needed, and the only writable assumption is over choice points — which is the case `docs/05` itself flags as possibly carrying none.

**And the exactness gate makes that narrowing structural rather than incidental, which sharpens the hold rather than softening it.** A11 reads `walkIsExact`, whose clause (c) requires an empty alphabet, so **this arm can only ever run on a machine with no named trigger** — and on such a machine `fairTriggers` is empty by construction and the assumption quantifies over choice points alone. The carrier's `strength`/`fair` cells and its splitting reader are unchanged (a future build that gains a carrier for what the environment can supply would release clause (c) and the trigger half with it), but the honest statement of what 5b buys today is *strong fairness over declaration-order choice points*, and `domainSize` — the number Checkpoint B retires the carrier on — is now measurably the count of choice points and nothing else. If that reads 0, the carrier is a permanent compatibility commitment bought for nothing, and §7 decision 6's answer is already *be prepared to retire it*.

**The one new definition, and the naming argued.** `metadata def FairnessAssumption;` as a **fifth** definition in `SYSPROSE_VERIFICATION_LIBRARY` (`verification-vocabulary.ts`:160-166), whose doc string opens *"Four definitions SysML v2 does not express…"* and therefore moves to five — and `test/unit/semantics.keywords.test.ts` asserts that package text round-trips byte-identically, so text and test move together.

A **body carrier, not a tag**, by the file's own stated rule: it has a body (a strength and a set), and a bare `#FairnessAssumption` would be a tag claiming to be an assumption while carrying none — the reasoning already given there for `Evidence`, `PropertyPattern` and `FaultHypothesis`. It belongs in `CONFORMANCE.md` §8.6's **READ** table: the tool never authors it, exactly as it never authors a `FaultHypothesis`.

```
@SysproseVerification::FairnessAssumption {
    attribute strength = "strong";
    attribute fair = "state autonomous; trigger abort";
}
```

**The set-valued cell is ONE cell holding a delimited list, and the repeated-cell spelling the draft used is rejected on measurement.** `attribute fair = "state autonomous"; attribute fair = "trigger abort";` parses — the draft measured that, and stopped there, at the parser. The **tool's own validator refuses it**: `validation/duplicate-name` (`rules.ts`:181, `severity: 'error'` at :183) groups every non-library element by `ownerId` + `declaredName` and files an error on each sibling of a repeated name. Executed against this tree: `npx tsx scripts/sysml-check.ts` on a file carrying exactly that block prints `error validation/duplicate-name  Duplicate name "fair" within FairProbe::Modes::«MetadataUsage».` **twice**, and the file **FAILS** the check, rc=1. So the one new model vocabulary lane G commits to could not have appeared in any model that passes `npm run check`, and §3.5b's "collecting reader" was built to read a shape the tool calls malformed. (`#failureMode` was measured the same way and is clean; the defect is specific to the repeated-cell spelling.)

Of the three re-spellings available — one delimited cell, indexed cells (`fair1`, `fair2`), or a nested usage per constraint — this plan takes **the delimited cell**, for three stated reasons: sibling names stay unique so `validation/duplicate-name` is satisfied by construction; `propertiesOf`'s reader is `cells.set(name, value)`, i.e. last-wins, so one name per cell means nothing can be silently dropped (§6.2 trap 28 stops being reachable rather than being worked around); and the tool already owns a delimiter discipline in `--pattern`, so `;` inside a constraint is **refused** the way `--pattern` refuses an embedded delimiter, with `carrier-unreadable` provenance and the offending text quoted. The trade is written down: `;` becomes reserved inside a fairness constraint, and that is a compatibility statement in `docs/CONFORMANCE.md` §8.6 beside the definition.

**The measurement, and the layer it was taken at.** Parses with zero diagnostics **and passes `npm run check` with 0 errors** (the second half is the one the draft omitted, and it is what commit 17's test list now asserts); round-trips **idempotently from the second save**, with the delimiter and the internal spacing byte-identical.

**Rejected names, with reasons.** Anything containing **`realizable`** — a bare-word ban with no allowance, which is why `docs/05` needed a whole-file exemption merely to quote a theorem title. **`FairScheduling` / `Scheduler`** — there is no scheduler; the interpreter concatenates regions. **`LivenessAssumption`** — it constrains runs, not properties.

**Two reader rules that are load-bearing.** (1) **Split, do not collect.** The set arrives as one cell whose value is a `;`-delimited list; the reader splits it, trims, and refuses a member containing a second delimiter. The draft's rule — *collect* repeated same-named cells, because `propertiesOf`'s reader is last-wins — is retired with the spelling it was written for: with one name per cell there is nothing for last-wins to drop, which is a better outcome than a reader that works around it. An **empty member**, a **duplicate member** and an **unparseable member** are each `carrier-unreadable` provenance and **no verdict**, never a silently shortened set. (2) Read both cell spellings (`AttributeUsage` and the keyword-less `ReferenceUsage` with `attrs.keywordless === true`, `fault-tree.ts`:375-381) and report a seen-but-unreadable carrier as **provenance**, never as a default.

**The set addresses states and triggers, never transitions** (correction 8).

**Engine.** Tarjan plus the fair-SCC procedure, whose recursion depth is the number of constraints (1–3 here). **Not** nested depth-first search: that is the on-the-fly answer to a product you cannot store, and this walk stores four configurations.

**Verdict vocabulary.**

| Sentence | Condition |
|---|---|
| `holds under fairness {strong: state autonomous} — no fair cycle **and no finite maximal run** avoids \`state done\`; the four safety verdicts on this machine were re-checked under it and none changed` | **A11**, and **both run classes are checked, which is the third review's correction.** `F p` under an assumption is refuted by a fair lasso whose cycle never holds `p` **or** by a finite maximal run on which `p` never holds — those are the two classes §3.5a's own semantic profile commits to (`docs/05`-independent, this plan's own words at §3.5a), and a cyclic graph still admits the second. Concrete counter-example to the draft's row: `idle → busy`, `busy → idle`, `busy → done`, `idle → parked` with `parked` a dead end, completion transitions throughout. Under a strong-fairness assumption naming the choice point `busy` the `idle ⇄ busy` cycle is unfair and every fair lasso reaches `done`, so *"no fair cycle avoids `state done`"* is true and the draft prints it at exit 0 — while `idle → parked` reaches `done` on no continuation and is a finite maximal run this engine's profile admits. The second half is **one reachability query over the same retained relation** (from the opening, restricted to `p`-free configurations, to an `i` with `successors[i].length === 0`), so it costs nothing beyond the Tarjan pass this arm already runs, and it reuses §3.R's sink-scoping rule verbatim: *a sink is a sink in the full graph or it is not a sink.* **The assumption's example loses its `weak: trigger abort` member**, because `walkIsExact`'s clause (c) means this arm only ever runs on machines with an empty alphabet — see the release-condition note below |
| `…; and 1 changed: \`absence (state failsafe)\` is now inconclusive` | as above, with a moved safety verdict named |
| `refuted under fairness {…}: fair ω-run, presented as a lasso — prefix standby → manual, then the 2-step cycle (autonomous → manual) repeated` | **W5.** A fair cycle avoiding the promise, ∧ A11's gate ∧ (**`relationIsTheMachines` ∨ the per-step disjunction of §2.3 over the prefix and the cycle**). **The words "ω-run" and "lasso" are licensed by the second run class of §3.5a's semantic profile**, which this arm is the only user of; the draft printed this row under a profile that admitted finite runs only, so the verdict and the profile contradicted each other two lines apart in one output. **And the draft left it ungated in the other direction as well:** a lasso whose cycle is composed entirely of dwell edges is a run the interpreter never takes, and this row spends **exit 1** on it — the identical defect as §3.5a's `fail`, which is why W4 and W5 are one clause written twice rather than an argument made once |
| `inconclusive: the fair cycle this walk found is composed of \`after(n)\` dwell edges it takes without advancing a clock, so the refutation would rest on a run the interpreter may never take` + §2.4(c) | **¬W5** — a fair cycle was found and some step of its prefix or cycle crossed a transition for which `afterDuration` is defined (tested on the step's transition id, §2.3) |
| `inconclusive: the declared assumption has no fair continuation from configuration #3, so it may also change the safety verdicts and is not applied` | the fair-continuation condition fails |
| `inconclusive: the machine names no trigger and declares no choice point, so the declared assumption has an empty domain and carries no information` | the assumption quantifies over nothing |

**MUST NEVER.** A liveness verdict **without the assumption on the same line** — `CONFORMANCE.md` §8.5 already promises this. The reserved word, for this condition or anything else. **A fairness assumption applied to a safety verdict without re-running the four safety patterns under it and reporting any change** — the theorem that makes fairness irrelevant to safety holds only for assumptions with a fair continuation from every reachable state, and the counter-example in the same source shows a non-conforming assumption changing the verdict on "never a". The check is the same SCC pass, so it is free. `no fair cycle` from a non-exhaustive walk — A11. **A `holds under fairness` that quantified over fair cycles only** — the `idle → parked` machine above; the pass line names **both** run classes or it is a claim about one of the two the profile admits, which is the shape §3.5a's *"a verdict that does not name its run class"* forbids one section earlier. **A `refuted under fairness` whose lasso runs on dwell edges** — W5; the refutation is a positive claim over the retained relation and spends the 1, so it carries the same clause as 5a's `fail`. **A dwell label counted as a `fairTrigger`** — the census field feeds Checkpoint B's `domainSize`, and `machineAlphabet` puts every `after(n)` label in the alphabet, so `fairTriggers` is computed over `alphabet ∖ timedLabels` or the number that retires the carrier is inflated by labels no environment schedules.

**Exit-code contract — the subsection this section did not have, and its first version routed a verdict under a sentence that means something else.** `behaviour`. `holds under fairness {…}` counts as `pass` ⇒ **0**; `refuted under fairness {…}` is a `fail` ⇒ **1**, and the 1 row's *"a witness trace of a run this semantics admits"* is satisfied by the **ω-run** class §3.5a's profile defines — that is the whole reason the profile names two classes. Both inconclusive rows ⇒ **2**, neither lowerable.

**But the 0 row does NOT already cover the fair pass, and the draft said it did.** After commit 16 that row publishes three discharge shapes — *"every ASSERTION … shown to hold **on every reachable configuration, over a graph this walk saw whole**"*, *"every `cover` … witnessed on a run this walk saw"*, and *"or, **on an acyclic graph**, on every maximal run"* — and 5b's pass is none of them: it is run-wise rather than configuration-wise, it is not a cover, and this arm runs **only on cyclic graphs** (§3.5a row 1 refuses whenever `acyclic !== true`) over the ω-run class the profile invents for it. Left as the draft had it, `check-behaviour <cyclic fair model>` exits 0 while its own `--help` and the byte-pinned `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md`, rendered from this constant by `exitCodesFor`, publish a sentence asserting something the run did not establish — the exact laundering §2.2 forbids and the third instance of the defect §2.1 was rewritten to close.

**So `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` moves a third time, in commit 18** — the commit that creates the shape, per §2.1's rule that the edit lands in the commit that changes what the code means. The 0 row gains *"…or, under a declared `FairnessAssumption` named on the verdict line, on every fair run of a graph this walk saw whole"*, asserted in `cli-reference.test.ts`'s `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` block at :206-214 in the same commit, exactly as §2.1 requires of the other four edits. **And commit 18's byte-identity assertion is narrowed to what it was actually for:** it asserted the constant did not move between commits 17 and 18, which — with this edit — would be red on landing and would then be "fixed" by deleting the clause. It becomes an assertion over commits **16 and 17** (the constant does not move in the carrier commit), plus, in commit 18, an assertion that **only the 0 row moved and the 1 and 2 rows are byte-identical**. That keeps the trip-wire — nobody re-opens the constant quietly — without pinning the omission.

**Census — the field that decides Checkpoint B, and it did not exist.** `docs/05` candidate 5's kill measurement has two halves, and the draft instrumented only the first: *"If most machines carrying a liveness question turn out acyclic, the fairness machinery has no customer. If most are cyclic **and** the assumption a user would write is always 'everything is strongly fair', the assumption carries no information."* Commit 3's `acyclic` census answers the first. Nothing answered the second — no Census subsection here, no census in commits 17 or 18, no row in §5's table — so after commit 18 shipped a permanent compatibility commitment, Checkpoint B's instruction to *"re-read the fairness census"* would have pointed at nothing, and the retirement decision would have been taken on an argument rather than on a number, which is exactly what §2.5's rule forbids. What ships, inside the behaviour payload, per run: `{strength, fairStates, fairTriggers, domainSize, continuable, safetyVerdictsMoved}`. `domainSize` is the number of choice points and triggers the declared set actually quantifies over on this machine — **`0` is the measurement that retires the carrier**, and it is a recorded number rather than a judgement. `safetyVerdictsMoved` is free: the four-pattern re-check runs anyway. §5's table gains a row for commit 18 naming these fields, and Checkpoint B cites `domainSize` by name.

**Tests.** An acyclic / cyclic-fair / cyclic-unfair-lasso **trio**, the third asserting the ω-run wording and the run class named; **a fourth member, `idle → busy`, `busy → idle`, `busy → done`, `idle → parked` with `parked` a dead end**, under a strong-fairness assumption on the choice point `busy` — every fair lasso reaches `done` and `idle → parked` does not, so the test asserts the verdict is a **refutation over the finite-maximal-run class** and names `holds under fairness {…} — no fair cycle avoids \`state done\`` at exit 0 as the wrong answer (R3-12); a **non-conforming** assumption ⇒ the no-fair-continuation row **and** an assertion that no liveness verdict was printed; a fixture where a safety verdict moves ⇒ the change named. Carrier: package text byte-identical; **`npm run check` on the fixture asserts 0 errors** — the assertion whose absence let the repeated-cell spelling through — and a second fixture carrying the **repeated same-named cells is asserted to FAIL with `validation/duplicate-name`**, so the rejected spelling has a name in the suite; the delimited list splits into both members; an embedded second delimiter ⇒ `carrier-unreadable` and no verdict; an unknown field ⇒ `carrier-unreadable` and no verdict; missing `import SysproseVerification::*;` ⇒ `verification/keyword-names-nothing` (measured — so every shipped example carries the import or it documents a broken model). The census fields asserted on all three trio members, `domainSize: 0` asserted on a machine with no choice point and no trigger.

**Cost.** The largest behaviour-lane item: a carrier (a compatibility commitment), a splitting reader, the fair-SCC procedure, the fair-continuation precondition, the safety re-check and the census. Two codes: `verification/fairness-not-continuable` (info), `verification/safety-verdict-moved` (warning) — and the warning is raised from `patterns.ts`, not `explore.ts`, so it has no existing set to join: commit 18 adds `PATTERN_WARNING_CODES` in `patterns.ts` and feeds `VERIFICATION_WARNING_CODES` (`api/verification.ts`:1061) from both it and `BEHAVIOUR_WARNING_CODES`, or `diagnostic-codes.test.ts`:86-89's set equality goes red on landing.

### 3.6 — Stutter-simulation between an abstract machine and its refinement — **HELD**

**Release condition.** A census reports ≥1 pair of state machines related by `Refine` or `Subclassification` on a real model. Measured today: **0 across every `.sysml` file in the tree** (correction 10).

**What it would decide.** Does the detailed machine do only what the abstract one allows? Simulation, not bisimulation and not trace inclusion.

**Command.** `refine <file> [--element REF] --via specialize` — the **same** `--via` value 4b adds, dispatching on the metaclass of the two ends. One flag value, two features.

**No new vocabulary, and that is the finding.** Both carriers already exist and both were measured; what is missing is a **reader**, because `derivationsOf` iterates contracts. Ship the reader off `model.relationshipsTo`, exactly as `contracts.ts`:1074 does for requirements, and make the report **state which orientation was read**.

**Labelling is this tool's own choice and must say so.** The rule: the `state X` atoms whose names **both** machines declare. On the measured probe pair that is `{idle, active}` with `warmup` unlabelled — so **the stutter rule for an unlabelled intermediate state decides the verdict on the very example that motivates the feature**, and it is written down before the first fixture is recorded. The book's remark that would license comparing over common atomic propositions is `docs/05` §5 item 2, flagged unverified by its own author; it is **not cited**.

**MAY print.** `Detailed is simulated by Abstract — simulation relation of 7 pairs, over graphs of 3 and 2 configurations, both exhaustive; labels {idle, active}; \`warmup\` carries no common label and was read as a stutter step` · `no simulation relation exists over the common labels {idle, active}: the pair (Detailed::warmup, Abstract::idle) fails — \`warmup → active\` has no matching step` · **and four refusal sentences, one per clause of the gate, because a verdict refused on three of them had no sentence to print at all** — the draft's MAY-print list offered only the first, while §5(b) and commit 20 both said the refusal covers *"any of its four clauses"*:

- `inconclusive: \`Abstract\` was not walked to exhaustion` — clause (a), `!seenWhole`, on either machine, and the machine is named.
- `inconclusive: \`Detailed\` carries \`after(n)\` dwell transitions this walk takes without advancing a clock, so a step it matched may be one the interpreter never takes` + §2.4(c) — `failedClause === 'time'` on either machine.
- `inconclusive: \`Detailed\` names triggers this walk offers at every configuration, so a step it matched may be one the environment never supplies` + §2.4(a) — `failedClause === 'environment'` on either machine.
- `inconclusive: a transition of \`Detailed\` is guarded by an attribute with no declared value, so this walk never offered an edge the model states and the pair that would have broken the relation may be one it could not see` + the store sentence — `failedClause === 'store'` on either machine.

Each names **which** machine failed and **which** clause, exactly as §3.2a, §3.2b, §3.R, §3.5a and §3.8 do; one `Exactness` is computed per walk and the sentence is a `failedClause` switch over the pair.

**MUST NEVER.** **"equivalent"** — simulation is a pre-order. **"`Detailed` does not implement `Abstract`"** from a failed search — the method is sound but **incomplete** for trace inclusion, so the only honest sentence names the pair that broke it. **Any verdict when either walk fails `walkIsExact`, on any of its four clauses** (A12) — and the sentence printed is the clause-named one above, naming which of the two machines failed. An earlier revision spelled this MUST-NEVER as *"either walk fails the first three publishability conditions"*, which is strictly weaker than the register in the direction that matters: that conjunction is `seenWhole` and nothing else, and it contains **none** of the time, environment or guard clauses, so a probe pair that fails clause (b), (c) or (d) would have published a simulation verdict over a relation that is not either machine's — while §5(b) and commit 20 both already said *"any of its four clauses"*. **The numbered-condition vocabulary is retired for exactly this reason** (§2.3): a MUST-NEVER that names conditions by number is a MUST-NEVER that goes stale the moment the gate gains a clause, and this section was the last one still carrying it. Two sections stating different gates for one claim is this plan's own trap 62, committed in the last section still carrying the draft's conjunction. A verdict without saying **which reading of the child machine** was used: measured, the walk is containment-only (`regionTransitions` is `model.descendants`; `directStates` is `model.children`), so a specialized machine inherits **nothing** behavioural today, while `effectiveFeatures(Detailed)` returns 3 states (masked correctly) but **five** TransitionUsages, because name-masking is vacuous for anonymous features and every transition in this corpus is anonymous. A check using `effectiveFeatures` would compare a machine nobody wrote and that `reach` never walked.

**Exit-code contract — and this is the one held feature whose verdict has no bucket today.** `refine`. A refinement found ⇒ `refined` ⇒ **0**. `inconclusive: \`Abstract\` was not walked to exhaustion` ⇒ `inconclusive` ⇒ **2**, correctly. But *"no simulation relation exists over the common labels {idle, active}: the pair (Detailed::warmup, Abstract::idle) fails"* on the measured probe pair is a **DECIDED negative**, and `refinementExitCode` (`api/verification.ts`:2273-2288) has nowhere to put it: it buckets runs into refined/notRefined/vacuous/inconclusive and returns **2** when `refined + notRefined === 0` (:2280), while `REFINE_EXIT_CODES` (`sysprose-spec.ts`:154) publishes 2 as *"a model that states no decomposition or derivation chain the `--via` family can read, or ANY undecided group"*. So the run would print a decided answer under a sentence saying nothing was read and nothing was decided.

**The decision, and the alternative that was rejected.** This plan takes **option (a)**: the result counts as `inconclusive` for the arithmetic and `REFINE_EXIT_CODES`' **2** row gains one clause in commit 20 — *"…or a simulation search that decided no relation exists over the common labels, which names the pair that broke it and refutes no obligation"* — exactly the shape §2.1 writes for `cover`'s `not covered`, and `verification/no-simulation-relation` stays **info**. Option (b) — count it in `notRefined` for exit **1** — is rejected because it contradicts this section's own MUST-NEVER (*never say `Detailed` does not implement `Abstract` from a failed search*, the method being sound but incomplete for trace inclusion) and because `verification.ts`:2290-2298 documents 1 as *"one ERROR per decomposition that does not refine"* with REFINE_EXIT_CODES' 1 reading *"at least one obligation refuted, with a counterexample this tool re-read and confirmed"*. There is no obligation here and nothing was re-read. Taking (b) would require rewriting the MUST-NEVER and re-grading the code to error, and this plan does neither.

**Tests, if built.** The probe pair with the measured orientation and the **unlabelled-intermediate rule asserted byte-identically** — it decides the verdict on the very example that motivates the feature, so it is the fixture, not a footnote · the pair `(Detailed::warmup, Abstract::idle)` named in the failure sentence · exit **2** with the new REFINE_EXIT_CODES clause asserted in `cli-reference.test.ts`'s refine block, and `verification/no-simulation-relation` emitted at **info** · a verdict refused when **either** walk fails `walkIsExact` — asserted once per clause, with the latch machine as the child (environment), the trapguard machine as the child (store) and a factory-built dwell machine as the child (time) — A12: extra edges in the child make the search fail more easily, so this is an increasing claim on the child's side and a decreasing one on the parent's, and the gate is required on **both** rather than reasoned about per side · the verdict line naming **which reading of the child machine** was used (walked, not `effectiveFeatures`) · the words *"equivalent"* and *"does not implement"* absent from every string this feature can print · `refine --via refine` on a requirement chain **unchanged**, and no `obligationDigest` moved.

**Cost.** A genuinely new algorithm plus one new relationship reading — the highest-value item here that needs real machinery rather than a re-use, and the one with the clearest measured absence of a customer. New code: `verification/no-simulation-relation` (info) — info, so it joins neither `VERIFICATION_ERROR_CODES` nor `VERIFICATION_WARNING_CODES`, and `diagnostic-codes.test.ts`'s third assertion (*every remaining `verification/*` entry is info*) covers it with no constant to edit.

---

### 3.8 — Behavioural fault tree by failure-mode injection — **HELD**

**Release condition.** The behavioural-lane census (commit 21) reports a real model whose hazard is a **mode** rather than a broken contract — i.e. a machine carrying an `#exceptional` state that a contract-level fault tree cannot reach.

**What it would decide.** Which combinations of component failure modes drive this **machine** into the hazard state. A cut set is an `EF` query — candidate 1's engine — run once per fault configuration.

**Command.** `npm run sysprose -- fault-tree <file> --element <StateDefinition|StateUsage> [--max-order N]`. **`fault-tree` stops refusing a machine**, which is also what finally deletes the dead pointer rather than building a flag to satisfy it: commit 5 makes the message name a command that exists, commit 21 replaces the refusal with a measured "no subject here" answer, and this commit gives the input a lane.

**One new name, two reuses.** `metadata def <failureMode> FailureMode;` in the same package, applied as a **keyword on a Boolean attribute of the machine**:

```
#failureMode attribute navFail : Boolean = false;
```

A **tag**, not a body carrier, because the model can already declare the thing — the failure mode **is** the store Boolean the guards read, and a body carrier would invent a second name and then join the two by string. This is the specification's own tag case: *"a metadata definition with no nested features simply acts as a user-defined syntactic tag"*, and it says one thing by being present: **this Boolean is a fault variable the analysis may set, not a design parameter.** Measured: parses, stores `attrs.metadata = ["failureMode"]` beside `attrs.value = false`, round-trips idempotently, coexists with `#exceptional` and a `FaultHypothesis` carrier, and is inventoried by `contracts --keywords` as *sysprose vocabulary* once the package is imported. **Reuse `#exceptional`** for the top-level event and **`FaultHypothesis { maxOrder }`** for the order bound, read off the machine. So candidate 8 ships **one** new name, halving the compatibility commitment.

**Rejected:** `Fault` / `Failure` alone (collides with `FaultHypothesis` and with `#exceptional`'s outcome sense) · `FailureModeVariable` (jargon) · `@FailureMode { name = …; effect = …; }` (re-invents addressing for a declared attribute).

**Engine.** In-process TypeScript downstream of `cover`, over a **seeded store, not a new engine**: guards read the same store `seedStore` fills from the model's literal values. Measured, with `transition first nominal if navFail then hazard`, seeding false leaves `hazard` unreachable and 3 transitions dead; seeding true makes 3 of 3 states reachable.

**The enumeration discipline is `judgeTarget`'s, parameterised rather than transcribed** (`fault-tree.ts`:624-827): order-0 baseline first (an already-open hazard ⇒ `top-event-open`, the empty set is the cut set, no enumeration); then the order loop over lexicographic `combinations` (model order, for byte-identical JSON), **skipping supersets of found cut sets**; `undecidedSets` tracked separately so a cut set with an undecided proper subset publishes `minimal: false` and *"MINIMALITY NOT ESTABLISHED"*; `singlePointOfFailure` three-valued, **null** the moment one order-1 check is undecided; `maxOrder` clamped and reported back. Every group gains `lane: 'contract' | 'behavioural'` and every verdict line says which.

**MAY print.** `3 failure mode(s); minimal cut sets up to order 2: {navFail}, {radioFail, gpsFail}; 7 cover queries, all decided` · `no cut set up to order 2 — higher orders not explored` (**A13**) · `inconclusive: a bound stopped the walk under fault set {navFail} — no absence is claimed for it` · `inconclusive: this machine's edges include \`after(n)\` labels offered as named events, so a cut set found here may rest on a dwell the interpreter never grants — no cut set is published and no absence is claimed` (**`walkIsExact` fails on clause (b)**) + §2.4(c) · the same row for the other two clauses, with the injected machine's failed clause named — `…names triggers this walk offers at every configuration…` + §2.4(a), and `…is guarded by an attribute with no declared value, so an edge the model states was never offered and \`no cut set\` would be an absence over a graph this walk did not have…` + the store sentence, the last being the one that matters most here because **the injection substrate IS the store** · `top event already open with every failure mode cleared` · and, on **every** verdict: `cut sets are computed under a PERMANENCE assumption: each flag is set at the opening configuration and never cleared` · `no temporal ordering between failures is captured` · `the absence rows above are about the INJECTED machine; \`reach\`'s unreachable and dead rows on this file are about the nominal one`.

**Why the gate holds the whole report and not one sentence (A13's mixed polarity).** `no cut set` is monotone-**decreasing** in edges — extra edges make the hazard easier to reach, so they can only *add* cut sets — and would survive the two over-approximating mechanisms on its own. Every **published** cut set is the opposite: `{navFail}` is an existential reachability claim over the injected machine, and a hazard reachable only through a preempted timing edge, or only through a trigger the walk supplied, yields a cut set no run of the interpreter realises. **And the store cuts the other way and catches the half that would otherwise have been safe:** an edge the model states and the walk never offered makes the hazard *harder* to reach, so it manufactures `no cut set` — the decreasing half — directly, on a machine whose guards read exactly the store this feature injects into. So all four clauses hold the report, both halves together, and they are not split — for the same reason §3.2b does not split its arm: a reader cannot be asked to know which half of a report a qualification applies to.

**MUST NEVER.** `no cut set` with any order-≤N query undecided (A13). "No single point of failure" when any order-1 query was undecided — `singlePointOfFailure` stays `null`. Either of the two stated limits omitted. **A permanence claim it did not check:** guards read the same store `assign` effects write, so an effect can clear a `#failureMode` flag mid-run. The plan **refuses** such a model, naming the effect, rather than printing "permanence assumed and not checked" — the second is a claim nobody can act on. A behavioural cut set mixed into a contract-level group. An empty cut-set list over a machine the command did not walk — the refusal's own docstring: *"'0 cut sets' reads as 'no combination of failures breaks this', which about a behaviour this command never looked at is the loudest false statement it could make."*

**Exit-code contract.** `fault-tree`, and **this commit does not touch `FAULT_TREE_EXIT_CODES` at all** — the 2 row's state-machine clause was *replaced* in commit 21, in the commit that changed the behaviour it publishes (§2.1). A single point of failure ⇒ 1; undecided ⇒ 2; the no-`--free` and no-`--allow-inconclusive` rules apply to both lanes.

**Tests.** A three-mode machine with an `#exceptional` hazard ⇒ `{navFail}` and `{radioFail, gpsFail}`, exit 1, 7 queries. A redundant variant ⇒ order-2 only, exit 0. An `assign navFail = false` variant ⇒ **refused by name**. A `--max-configs 2` variant ⇒ inconclusive with the absence suppressed. **A timed variant** whose hazard is reachable only across a preempted dwell ⇒ **the clause-(b) row** — the one §3.8's MAY-print list spells *"`walkIsExact` fails on clause (b)"* — with **no cut set published and no `no cut set` printed either**, and `minimal cut sets up to order 2: {navFail}` named as the wrong answer. **That variant is built through the API and injected in-process, not added to the example this commit ships**: `accept after(n)` does not parse (correction 22), so a timed `.sysml` fixture would fail the corpus gate with parse errors and then assert a failed time clause over a walk that never ran. **A second timed variant carries its dwells as numeric `attrs.after` with no trigger** (§2.3's fourth reading) and asserts the same row, because that is the shape an alphabet-scoped clause (b) reads clean. Both limit sentences on every verdict. The two-world sentence wherever a `reach` row is printed beside it. Round-trip and import tests as above.

**Cost.** One new module (`injection.ts`), `judgeTarget` parameterised over a query function rather than transcribed, one `lane` field on every group, one example model carrying `#failureMode`, one code — `verification/failure-mode-cleared` (info), so it joins neither `VERIFICATION_ERROR_CODES` nor `VERIFICATION_WARNING_CODES` — and one prose figure in `CONFORMANCE.md`:29 (**6 shipped example models** → 7; `docs-counts`' `exampleCount` is derived and moves by itself, §4 commit 23). No exit-code contract moves here: commit 21 already replaced the `FAULT_TREE_EXIT_CODES` 2-row clause, and a test asserts the constant is byte-identical across commits 21 and 23. The compute is `cover` run once per fault configuration, bounded by `maxOrder`, and the report is the one place in this plan where the gate holds a whole report rather than a sentence.

**Census.** `fault-tree --json` records, per behavioural group: `{failureModeFlags, exceptionalStates, ordersExplored, queriesDecided, queriesUndecided, cutSetsByOrder, singlePointOfFailure, refusedByGate}` — the last carrying the injected walk's `failedClause`, or `null`. **The kill measurement is commit 21's census read a second time, after the vocabulary exists:** if `machinesWithFailureModeFlags` stays 0 on recorded runs, the `#failureMode` tag is a permanent compatibility commitment nobody applied and the lane retires with it. **The second, sharper measurement is `refusedByGate`, and §5(d) is its baseline:** the injection substrate *is* the store, so clause (d) is the clause most likely to fire here — on a model whose guards read any unbound attribute that is not one of the flags — and the only machine in the corpus that fires it today is the `guard-undetermined` fixture. If `queriesUndecided` is non-zero on most recorded runs, `singlePointOfFailure` is `null` on most of them and the report answers nothing an engineer can act on. **The measured baseline, from §5(d): of the eleven machines in the tree exactly one is refused by the gate (`GuardProbe::Ctrl`, clause (d)) and **none** carries a `#failureMode` flag or an `#exceptional` state**, so nothing in this repository exercises the second number and the fixtures are the whole of the evidence for it.

---

## 4. Commit plan

**Per-commit convention, unchanged from `docs/04` §4:** implement → adversarial review → fix → full gate.

```
npm run codes && npm run commands && npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test && npm run build
```
plus `npm run test:e2e` on any commit touching `src/ui/**`, `src/diagram/**` or `vite.config.ts` — **commits 12 and 24**. The draft's parenthetical said *"commit 12 only"*, and commit 24's own *Files:* list is `src/ui/panels/Properties.tsx` and `diagram/contracts-table.ts`: both paths exist, both are inside the rule's own scope, and taken literally the closing commit shipped two UI changes with the e2e suite never run — with the parenthetical as the reason an implementer would skip it. `npm run campaign` is inside `npm test`.

**Standing rules.**

- Anything §3 calls an in-process API is re-exported from `src/api/index.ts` **in the commit that adds it** — **and the same rule runs in the removal direction**, which is what the draft's version did not say and what commit 21 needed: a commit that **deletes** an exported symbol names every barrel that re-exports it, every test that imports it **and every test or document that asserts a sentence it composed**, in its own *Files:* list. `behaviourLaneRefusal` is the measured case (commit 21), and it has one site of each kind.
- Every commit adding a code runs `npm run codes` — the `**N catalogue codes**` figure is generated, so it can never be the thing that fails.
- **No absolute catalogue count appears anywhere in this plan.** Every guard line states a **delta** (`+1`, `+2`) and nothing else. The draft stated absolutes — commit 6's *"99 → 101"*, commit 8's *"101 → 102"* — which are only correct under one lane ordering: commit 8's own dependency line is 3, 4, so a build that runs lane C before lane B makes the real move `99 → 100`, and an implementer following the written number lands a `DIAGNOSTIC-CODES.md` regen that disagrees with `docs-counts.test.ts`. The re-order licence below and an absolute count cannot both be right.
- A new flag is **never** a new subcommand: `COMMANDS.length` stays 22 through the whole plan, and `RULES.length` stays 25.
- **A commit that deletes or replaces a printed sentence names every campaign case that asserts that sentence THROUGH THE CLI, not only the ones that import the function which composed it.** A symbol grep finds the second kind and misses the first; commit 21 is the measured case (`cli.sysprose.test.ts`'s `fault-tree refuses a state machine…` block asserts two substrings of a refusal it never imports), and a hand-pasted transcript in `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` is the same class of site with no test at all behind it. The check is a **string** grep for the sentence across `test/` and `docs/`, run in the commit that changes it.
- **Every commit that budgets time, or that adds a case to `test/campaign/cli.sysprose.test.ts`, assumes the restructured campaign file, which is what the tree already has** (§0.1, `189fc6d`). The gate is **548.74 s** measured at `dc6662c`, not the 790 s an earlier revision budgeted against, and that file is still the longest pole. **A new case picks its invoker, and the choice is part of the *Tests:* line**: `run(…)` in-process for anything asserting the TEXT a command prints, `spawnCli(…)` for anything only a process can be wrong about — exit status, `-` on stdin, an oversized payload, an unknown flag, an unreadable file, `--help` — **and for every solver-bearing subcommand**, because z3's WASM module carries process-global state and driving it many times in one worker aborts the module. The failure mode of choosing wrong in that last case is a **green** run, not a red one, which is why it is written down. A position in the file is anchored by `it(...)` title, never by line number.
- **A commit's *Files:* list never names a line.** It names a file, a symbol inside it, and — where a sentence moves — the sentence. §0.0 is the contract; §4 is where it is easiest to break.

**Lane A is the spine and is mandatory. Lane B is a SECOND spine, and the draft's "B…I are independent of one another" was wrong on its own dependency lines.** Measured against §4's own text: commit 9 (lane C) *Depends on:* 6, 8; commit 16 (lane G) *Depends on:* 3, 6; commit 19 (lane H) *Depends on:* 13; commit 23 (lane I) *Depends on:* 22, 6. Commit 6 is lane B and commit 13 is lane E. Drop lane B and commit 9's `recovery` arm has no `cover` dispatch to sit beside, commit 16 has no first `BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` edit to be *"the second pair of edits"* to, and commit 23's engine — *"`patterns.ts` (the `cover` query over a seeded store)"* — has no query function at all. So the rule is stated as it actually is:

> **A is the spine. B is a second spine: C, G and I depend on it. H depends on E. Given A and B, lanes C, D, E, F and G may be re-ordered or dropped whole; H and I may not be dropped independently of the lanes they name.**

Each lane's row says what it retires, and the two columns after it say what it needs and what needs it.

| Lane | Retires | Commits | Needs | Needed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A — spine | "an absence claim escapes its condition"; **"a shipped absence claim sits in no register at all"** (A0); "an increasing claim borrows a decreasing one's conditions"; "the relation the walk retained is not the machine's and nothing says so"; "a census reads `acyclic: true` off a walk that never happened, **or off a relation missing an edge the model states**"; "a new carrier breaks a user's file"; "a reserved sentence cannot be reserved"; "a message names a command that does not exist" | 0–5 | — | everything |
| B — guarantee class | "a correct design exits 1"; "`fail` is read as *every run*"; "exit 0 stops entailing what its own `--help` says" | 6–7 | A | C, G, I |
| C — recovery | "a trap is invented from a spanning tree"; "an escape is invented from a dwell, a trigger nobody sends, or an unset guard"; "an unrecoverable mode is never reported"; "an atom the engine cannot observe earns a refutation"; "a `node` atom is read as a `state` atom" | 8–9 | A, B | — |
| D — proof footprint | "evidence says *current* over a voided proof" | 10–11 | A | — |
| E — inheritance | "a verdict named after the child reports a subset of the child" | 12–13 | A | H |
| F — signature | "a check that fires on 100 % of ports ships as a finding" | 14–15 | A | — |
| G — liveness | "a fairness assumption silently moves a safety verdict"; "a fair pass discharges under a sentence about configurations" | 16–18 | A, B | — |
| H — simulation | "a feature ships for a modelling style nobody practises" | 19–20 | A, **E (commit 13)** — see the gate note below | — |
| I — behavioural FTA | "a second vocabulary is committed to before there is a subject for it" | 21–23 | A, B | — |
| — | closing | 24 | all | — |

**Lane H's gate is a conjunction, and the draft presented it as a single census that its own text predicts will not open.** §3.6's command is `refine <file> [--element REF] --via specialize` — *"the **same** `--via` value 4b adds. One flag value, two features."* That value is created only by commit 13, and commit 13 is **HELD**, gated on 12's census by Subclassification — while commit 12's own *Gate proves:* line says that census *"reads 0 on all six examples and every corpus file."* A gate that reads 0 means 13 is not built, means the `--via specialize` value does not exist, means commit 20 has no flag to dispatch on and commit 20's stated `REFINE_EXIT_CODES` guard move is unreachable. So:

> **Lane H opens only if BOTH: commit 12's census reads `contractsWithInheritedClauses ≥ 1` by Subclassification (so commit 13 ships and the `--via specialize` value exists), AND commit 19's machine-pair census reads ≥ 1 (so there is a pair to compare).** Measured today both read **0**, so lane H is held behind two gates and not one, and it is **not** an independently droppable lane. The alternative — give §3.6 its own `--via` value so it stops inheriting lane E's gate — is available and costs one more `VIA_VALUES` member plus a second family pin; it is **not** taken here, because two values for one dispatch would then have to explain why the tool has two names for "read a specialization", and because the honest reading of both zeros is that neither feature has a customer.

### Lane B — the guarantee class

**Commit 6 — `feat(behaviour): cover — an existential reachability property with a witness`** — §3.1
*Files:* CHANGE `patterns.ts` (`PatternClass` += `'guarantee'`; `PATTERNS` += `cover` **before `existence`**; `PropertyClaim` → six words; `monitorStep` += one `case`; the verdict tail ; **`PropertyVerdict` += `machineConfigs`, because `base` sets `configs: found.configs` unconditionally and no row may publish two unlabelled counts**; **the findings loop — a dedicated `not-covered` branch under `--cover-required` emitting at `severity: 'error'`, mirroring `--strict-vacuity`'s own branch, because the loop's own `severity:` expression derives it from the CLAIM and a `not-covered` row is not `fail`**; `counts`; `exitCode`; the §2.4(a) and §2.4(c) constants). CHANGE **`src/api/verification.ts` — add `verification/cover-required` to `VERIFICATION_ERROR_CODES`**, or `diagnostic-codes.test.ts`'s set equality goes red on landing. CHANGE `sysprose-spec.ts` (`PATTERN_NAMES`, `--cover-required`, **`BEHAVIOUR_EXIT_CODES` — the 0-row rewrite AND the 2-row clause, both quoted in §2.1**, the `--pattern` help row, **and `CommandSpec.question`, today *"Does this safety pattern hold on every reachable configuration?"*, which after this commit is false: it becomes *"Does this behaviour pattern hold on every reachable configuration, and can this design reach the situation I name?"***), `scripts/sysprose.ts` (`judgeBehaviour`, the summary line, **the four-words sentence at **), `src/text/langium/diagnostic-codes.ts` (+ `verification/not-covered` info, `verification/cover-required` error, **and an amendment to `verification/malformed-property`'s published `when`, which enumerates its causes exhaustively and spells the catalogue as six pattern names — *"a `pattern` outside the catalogue (`absence`, `universality`, `bounded-existence`, `precedence`, `existence`, `response`) …"*. The moment `cover` is in the catalogue that parenthesis is wrong, and a user who mistypes a field on a `cover` property reads in the generated `DIAGNOSTIC-CODES.md` that `cover` is a pattern outside the catalogue — plus the hint beside it telling them to write `pattern=absence`.
