## 5. Verification of the verifier — how each new claim is itself checked

Three mechanisms, and every new claim in §3 is covered by at least two — plus **(d)**, which is not a fourth mechanism but the measurement that says what the other three are worth on the models that actually exist.

**(a) The two registers are executable, and so is the polarity rule.** `ABSENCE_CLAIMS` is data, and `semantics.mc.publishable.test.ts` asserts by reflection that every row's producer is unreachable unless its condition holds. Adding a seventeenth absence claim without a condition is a red test — and the sixteenth, **A0**, is the one the fourth review found shipping ungated and the fifth found already closed: `verification/deadlock` is gated on the guards alone at `dc6662c` (`c929278`), so A0's value here is that a shipped absence claim is now **in the register** with its `producedBy` pointing at that gate, rather than that this plan repairs it. The headline for §5(a) is therefore *sixteen registered claims*, not *a sixteenth defect*. **And every row carries a polarity — one of four values, with a `polarityNote` — under the assertion that no `'increasing'` or `'mixed'` row reads `decreasingOk` unless its `alsoRequires` covers the mechanism its note names** — which is what makes §2.3's argument a check rather than a paragraph, and which is the mechanism that would have caught A6/A7 across the polarity flip. The two-valued version of that column was itself a defect one register down: six of the fifteen absence rows hold a value it could not express, and A0 could only have been made to type by stating the opposite of the paragraph that justifies it. This is hard constraint 1 made mechanical rather than reviewed. **After the third and fourth reviews the reflection test asserts four more things, and every one of them is there because a reviewer found the thing it checks:** that **`walkRequires`** takes one of exactly **four** values — `decreasingOk`, `walkIsExact`, `relationIsTheMachines`, or `null` for a claim that is not about a walk — so a per-mechanism conjunction cannot re-enter one row at a time, which is how the last two rounds of this defect arrived; that **`alsoRequires` names no member of the bound family on any row**, which is where a bound would otherwise creep back in as a conjunct; that **every `existential` witness row reads `relationIsTheMachines` and no bound**, which is what keeps §5(a) and commit 6's bound-frontier test from being mutually unsatisfiable; and that **every `maximality` witness row reads `walkIsExact`** — W3 and W4, the two rows whose claim is *this cycle has no exit* / *this run has no successor*, which a bound and an undecided guard both invent. **The draft asserted the third of those unscoped over all six witness rows while writing a bound condition into two of them**, so the register as tabled satisfied neither the test nor the rule it was written from — a defect one register down from the one it was written to prevent. Without the scoping the likely repair on landing is to weaken the reflection test, and then this mechanism covers nothing while still being counted. **`WITNESS_CLAIMS` is the second register and the second review's correction:** a refutation and a named avoiding cycle are *positive* claims over the retained relation, monotone-increasing in exactly the direction the time over-approximation falsifies, and the draft left all six of them — `covered`'s trace, a trap's `entry` path, `potential`'s cycle, 5a's `fail`, 5b's lasso, a published cut set — to prose, so §5(a)'s own sentence *"the polarity rule is machine-checked"* was false of every witness this plan prints. The reflection test runs over both tables, and the six witness rows are the reason §3.1's two `covered` rows now partition instead of leaving a hole an implementer resolves by taste.

**(b) Each claim has a fixture whose answer is known by construction**, and — for the three that can invent a finding — a **named negative test** that pins the wrong answer so the regression has a name in the suite:

| Claim | Positive fixture | The negative test that names the trap |
|---|---|---|
| the retained relation | 5 edges / 4 nodes on `FlightModes`, 4/3 on `vehicle`, 2/2 on `views-tour` | recording at the admission point yields 4 nodes / 3 edges — pinned as the wrong answer |
| `openFrontier` / `acyclic` | `--max-configs 2` on `uav-isr` ⇒ `openFrontier: true` | the unresolved-endpoint fixture: `configs 0, exhaustive false, boundHit 'none'` ⇒ `acyclic: null`. `acyclic: true` and `openFrontier: false` are pinned as the wrong answers, and `openFrontier === (boundHit !== 'none')` is named as the assertion that would pin the bug. **And the two-state trapguard file — exhaustive, `boundHit 'none'`, one dead transition — ⇒ `acyclic: null`, with `acyclic: true` pinned as the wrong answer**: the field reads `null` on `!walkIsExact` and not merely on `openFrontier`, or lane G's release gate can be flipped by a machine that is acyclic only because a guard was unbound |
| **the exactness gate's clause (b)** | the two-dwell fixture: `timedTransitions` and `timedLabels` both non-empty | **the numeric-`attrs.after` machine — three dwell transitions, `timedLabels` EMPTY, `bounds.alphabet` `[]` — ⇒ `walkIsExact: false`, `failedClause: 'time'`.** `walkIsExact: true` is pinned as the wrong answer, and with it `recoverable` at exit 0 and *"no component of this walk is inescapable beyond its endings"* on a machine whose `{failsafe, failsafeHold}` no run leaves |

The trapguard file cannot serve here: at `dc6662c` `c929278`'s walk-wise gate already empties it (measured `"deadlocks": []`, `verification/guard-undetermined` in its place), so asserting A0 on that file passes without the leaf-wise conjunct existing |

| the no-trap sentence | the mission DAG (1 ending exempted), the sink (1 deadlock exempted) | *"every reachable configuration lies in one component"* — false on the DAG's four trivial SCCs, and self-contradictory beside the sink's deadlock row |
| `not covered` | the sealed probe | at `--max-configs <depth−1>` it is `inconclusive`, not `not-covered` — **the bound is the witness depth, measured, not 1**; **the unset-guard fixture is `not covered` with the store named and exit 2 under `--cover-required`, never exit 1** |
| `covered` | the reachable probe, witness at depth *d*, asserted at `--max-configs d` | `--max-configs d−1` ⇒ `inconclusive` (measured: rc=2 at 1 **and 2** for a 3-step witness on `uav-isr`); `absence` on the same two machines still gives rc 1 / rc 0; **the two-dwell fixture prints the walk-admits wording and never a bare `covered`**; **the `abort`-then-dwell fixture (`timedTransitions` non-empty, witness crossed none of them) prints the BARE wording and never the walk-admits one — the hole the draft's two rows left, now W1** |

**(c) The sentence guards — and this claim is true only because commit 4 changes `negated()` AND scopes the forms, both of which were measured.** Commit 4 widens `claims.test.ts` to this plan's vocabulary before any of it can be printed, and commit 24 re-asserts every form against the shipped strings. Every new verdict string lives in `.ts`, which the guard already scans. **The guard as shipped cannot reserve a negative phrase at all** — `negated()` (:100-102) defuses any match whose span contains `no`/`not`/`never`, measured at 0 hits for `no cut set`, `not covered` and `no simulation relation` — which is why `negativeIsTheClaim` is in commit 4 and not in the closing pass. **And once that change is in place, the bare phrases the draft promised do not measure 0, which is the second correction:** executed over the 698-file scan set, `no cut set` reads **23**, `not covered` **3** and `recoverable` **2**, and nine of the 23 are in `src/semantics/fault-tree.ts` with two of those being the command's own honest bounded verdict. So commit 4 reserves the **sentence** rather than the phrase — the five scoped forms of §2.6's table, each measured at **0** and each asserted to fire on its planted sentence — and this section's opening count survives with its division stated plainly rather than implied: for the five new pieces of vocabulary, mechanism (c) covers the **prose sentence a reader would quote** (*"the machine is recoverable"*, *"this design has no cut set"*, *"the design is not covered"* all go red after commit 4), while the **verdict strings** — which carry no subject and are bounded by construction — are covered by (a) the register and (b) the byte-identical fixture. Two mechanisms each, and neither is the one that was quietly assumed.

**(d) The exactness survey — every state machine in the tree, RE-MEASURED at `dc6662c`, and the corpus has changed under it.** §2.3's collapse is only honest if somebody has counted what it refuses, and the honest answer moved between revisions: `c929278` and `a66ba1f` each landed a fixture model, and one of them is the first machine in this repository that carries a guard. Re-taken with `npm run sysprose -- reach <file>` over the complete population: `grep -rl "state def" --include=*.sysml` outside `node_modules` finds **10 files**. Three examples carry a machine (`uav-isr`, `vehicle`, `views-tour`); `contract-authoring-prompts`, `uav-isr-verification` and `uav-power-budget` report `"machines": []`. Under `test/fixtures/` there are now five: the two agent-authoring pairs (`L2-bare-transition-arrow`, `L3-unresolved-transition-end`) and **three new verification models** — `guard-undetermined.sysml`, `succession-mixed.sysml` and `succession-only.sysml`. That is **11 analysable machines**, plus `L2-bare-transition-arrow/input.sysml`, which does not parse (`ok:false`, `parse/bare-transition-arrow`, rc=1, no machine record), plus `succession-only.sysml`, which declares a `state def` owning only a succession and is therefore **not one of this tool's machines at all** — `stateMachinesIn` reads *directly owns a `TransitionUsage`* — so `reach` prints `0 state machine(s)` and makes no absence claim about it. That file is itself a fixture for the reading, not an omission.

Supporting figures, re-measured: 1 — 4/4 reachable, 5/5 fired, `deadlocks []`, rc 0 · 2 — 3/3, 4/4, `[]`, 0 · 3 — 2/2, 2/2, `[]`, 0 · 4 — 2/2, 1/1, `deadlocks ["on"]` 1 step, 0 · 5 — 2/2, 1/1, `deadlocks ["running"]` 1 step, 0 · 6 — 1/0 reachable, 0/0 fired, `suppressed` **true**, rc 0 · **7 — 1 of 2 reachable, `0 of 1 transition(s) fired, dead withheld`, `suppressed true`, one `verification/guard-undetermined`, rc 0** · 8 — 1 of 2 reachable, `0 of 1 fired, 1 dead`, and it raises `verification/unreachable-state` **and** `verification/dead-transition`, both correct · 9 — 2/2, 1/1, nothing absent · **10 — 3/3 reachable, `2 of 2 transition(s) fired` where one of the two is a `first active then done;` SUCCESSION, and one `verification/deadlock` at `done`, which is a genuine, fully-decided sink** · 11 — 2/2, 2/2, nothing absent, its succession closing the cycle. Machines 1 and 2 each emit one `verification/nondeterministic-choice`; machine 6 emits one `verification/behaviour-unsupported-construct`. Every invocation exits 0 except the unparseable L2 input (rc=1).

**How the gate columns were established, since the JSON does not state them directly — and what has changed since the last revision.** `machineAlphabet` puts `after(n)` spellings *into* the alphabet by design, so a dwell written as a trigger would be visible as an alphabet member; `bounds.alphabet` is `[]` on **all eleven** and `grep -c 'after('` is **0** in every source file. The other producer of a dwell — a numeric `attrs.after`, which contributes no label at all — cannot appear in a `.sysml` file by construction: there is no `after` token in the grammar and no `attrs.after` write anywhere in `map-to-model.ts` (correction 22). So **clause (b) remains satisfied vacuously everywhere, in both spellings, and remains unfalsifiable by the corpus.** `grep -c accept` is **0** everywhere and every transition is a completion transition (`event: ""` in the nondeterminism rows confirms it), so **clause (c) is satisfied everywhere and is also unfalsifiable.** **Clause (d) is the one that changed.** `guard-undetermined.sysml` carries three guarded machines — one undecidable, one decided false, one decided true — so the corpus now contains a real instance of the shape, a real control for over-firing in each direction, and a machine on which `walkIsExact` genuinely fails. The claim *"not one transition in this repository carries a guard"*, which earlier revisions of this plan leaned on in five places, is **false at `dc6662c`** and has been removed from all of them. **And the element-set half is exercised too**: `succession-mixed.sysml` walks a `first … then …` edge in the step relation and `succession-only.sysml` pins the *not a machine* reading beside it, so `census.unaccounted === 0` is a live assertion on real corpus files rather than a fixture-only one.

**This survey is not decoration and it is not a pass mark: it is the kill-measurement every feature that reads the gate is retired on, and each census row below cites it by name.** Said plainly, and not implied: **the corpus does not exercise clauses (b), (c) or (d) at all** — no `after(n)`, no `accept`, no guard in any `.sysml` file in the tree — so no shipped model can falsify them, and no run of the shipped examples is evidence that they behave correctly. What follows is what the survey says, and two of the three are uncomfortable.

1. **The gate is free on the shipped corpus, and that is still most of its weakness.** Nine of eleven machines are exact; the two that are not — the unresolved-endpoint fixture and `GuardProbe::Ctrl` — **already report `suppressed: true` today**, one on the unsupported construct and one on the guard clause `c929278` shipped. So `walkIsExact` suppresses nothing the shipped `suppressed` flag does not already suppress, and every increasing claim (A6, A7, A8, A10, A11, A12, A13, W1–W6) publishes on every machine in the tree. That is the same conclusion as before; what has changed is that the sample now contains a machine the gate refuses for a reason other than *nothing was walked*.
2. **The shipped conjunction is doing most of the work, and clause (d) is no longer fixture-only.** `const publishable` in `reachOne` is `walk.exhaustive && alphabetOffered && walk.unsupported.length === 0 && guardsDecided`; on this corpus `alphabetOffered` is vacuously true, and the shipped conjunction agrees with `seenWhole ∧ clause (d)` on all eleven machines. **The new content of `walkIsExact` is clauses (b) and (c), and those two remain unfalsifiable against the corpus** — 0 dwell transitions and 0 named triggers, everywhere, by construction for the first. Their `inconclusive` paths are exercised only by fixtures this plan invents: the latch machine (c), the preempted-dwell machine (b), and the **numeric-`attrs.after` machine** that clause (b) needed after its producer was corrected. Those three are **not optional colour; they are the only coverage those branches will ever get, and a commit that deletes one has deleted a clause** — §7 decision 14 says so to the reader rather than leaving it to be inferred. **Clause (d) is the exception and it is worth naming as a win:** `guard-undetermined.sysml` gives it a corpus witness plus two controls, so this plan's trapguard fixtures extend a real fixture family rather than inventing one. **The element-set half has corpus coverage too** — `succession-mixed.sysml` and `succession-only.sysml` — and it is the only part of exactness that does.
3. **The uncovered residue is real on two of the three flagship machines.** `nondeterministic = 1` on `FlightModes` (at `autonomous`) and on `VehicleStates` (at `idling`): the walk explores a declaration-order tie-break the simulator does not take (*"takes `autonomous -> manual` … and never `autonomous -> failsafe`"*). `walkIsExact` says nothing about it, which is why §2.4(d) prints. Separately, machines 4, 5 and **10** each have a **deadlock sink** (`on`, `running`, `SuccMix::Ctrl::Modes::done`, 1–2 steps) while all three shipped examples have `deadlocks: []` — so the finite-maximal-run class R3-12 found A11 failing to quantify over has **instances in the fixture corpus and none in the example corpus**. An `F p` fixture built from the shipped examples cannot exhibit that defect; commit 18's fourth trio member has to be built from the fixture shape, and §3.5b says so. **`SuccMix::Ctrl` is also the nearest thing the tree has to A0's missing fixture** — a genuine, fully-decided sink, carrying no guard — so the machine A0 needs is that shape with an undecidable guard added in a second region.

**And the census table — what each line owes its own retirement.** `mc-scope`'s standing rule, implemented rather than noted. Every row is a `--json` field inside an existing payload object, **and every row that reads the gate carries §5(d)'s survey as its measured baseline**, so the retirement question is asked against a number that already exists rather than against one the feature will supply later.

| Commit | Recorded, per run | The observation that retires the feature |
|---|---|---|
| 3 | `acyclic` (**three-valued, `null` on `!walkIsExact`**), `openFrontier`, `edges`, **`timedTransitions.size`**, `timedLabels.size`, **`alphabet.length`**, **`undeterminedGuards.length`** and the derived **`walkIsExact` / `failedClause`** per machine | `acyclic === true` stays 0 everywhere ⇒ 5a has no customer; stays true everywhere ⇒ 5b has none. **`walkIsExact` is the row the collapse adds, and §5(d) is its baseline: 5 of 6 machines exact, 0 of 6 refused, with clauses (b), (c) and (d) at 0, 0 and 0 across the whole tree.** Two readings, both actionable: if `failedClause` stays `null` on recorded runs, the gate costs nothing and its four fixtures are the only thing keeping it honest — which is a reason to protect them, not to drop the gate; if `failedClause` reads `'environment'` on most recorded machines, then lanes B, C and G answer only completion-driven designs and the population question is real. **`timedTransitions.size` is re-priced by correction 22 and no longer says what the draft said it says:** on any model a CLI command can load it is **0 by construction**, because `accept after(n)` is a parse error and `attrs.after` has no notation at all, so it cannot measure "how many machines in a user's corpus this plan can make an increasing-polarity claim about" and cannot retire lane C. What it measures is **SDK/API traffic** — machines built through `ModelFactory` or written with `model.create` — and the question it answers is *"does anything outside our own test suite build a timed machine?"*. If that stays 0 across recorded API runs, the time clause is a guard that costs nothing and protects a surface nobody uses; it is still not removable, because the SDK is supported and the hazard is real on it. **`timedLabels.size` is recorded beside it and retires nothing**: it exists to split the environment sentence from the time one, and the gap between the two numbers is itself the measurement — a machine where `timedTransitions` is non-empty and `timedLabels` is empty is the shape the draft's clause (b) could not see |
| 6 | `{atomKind, scope, coverUnreachedBehindUndefinedGuard}` per `cover` row | ≥80 % `state X × globally` ⇒ `reach` already answers them; retire in favour of a witness path in `reach`. **`coverUnreachedBehindUndefinedGuard` is correction 23's exposure as a number**: if most `not covered` answers turn out to be guards over attributes with no declared value, the honest fix is a `verification/unbound-guard-attribute` finding on the model rather than a cover verdict about the design — and post-`c929278` that finding half-exists as `verification/guard-undetermined`, so the census measures how much of the remaining exposure is `cover`-shaped. **Measured baseline from §5(d): 0 on the shipped examples**; `guard-undetermined.sysml` is the one corpus file that would make it non-zero, and it is a fixture rather than a design |
| 7 | `{guaranteed, potential, notDecided, refusedByGate}` | never separates anything ⇒ drop the annotation. **Measured baseline from §5(d): `refusedByGate` is `null` on every machine but `GuardProbe::Ctrl`, where it reads `'store'`**, and both modality values read the whole gate, so on this corpus the annotation costs nothing on any shipped example |
| 8 | `{sccs, bottomSccs, trapsAfterExemptions, exemptedAsEnding, exemptedAsDeadlock, exemptedAsCore, refusedByGate}` | three consecutive real models that are one component or a DAG into final states ⇒ drop it. **`refusedByGate` is the second, sharper measurement §5(d) makes necessary:** the intuitive trap — a mode you leave only by sending a trigger — is exactly the shape clause (c) refuses, and the draft's own second positive fixture (the latch machine) is now a refusal. If `refusedByGate` reads `'environment'` on most machines carrying a trap-shaped component, this feature answers only completion-driven designs and the honest question is whether that population exists. Measured baseline: **0 of 11** refused on `'environment'`, because no machine in the tree names a trigger at all |
| **9** | `{atomKind, targetConfigs, cannotReach, bottomSccOverlap, refusedByGate}` per `recovery` row | `cannotReach` is always 0 or the whole complement of the opening's component ⇒ `recovery` answers what commit 8's trap row already answers; retire the pattern and keep the structural report. Read together with row 8 at Checkpoint A. **`refusedByGate` replaces the draft's `timeSound`** and asks the wider question the collapse makes askable — which clause refuses, not just whether time does; measured baseline `null` on every machine but `GuardProbe::Ctrl`, so today the gate costs `recovery` nothing on the shipped examples and the corpus cannot say what it would cost anyone else |
| 10 | `{scriptAxioms, coreAxioms, footprintAxioms}` per obligation | core equals footprint everywhere ⇒ keep the display, never build commit 11 |
| 11 | `{wholeModelMoved, coreMoved, footprintMembershipMoved, axiomSetMoved, premisesMoved}` **per evidence record, per run** — *not* a stale rate over edits, which is longitudinal and cannot be a field in a payload that sees one model version (`sysml-check.ts`:141) | the five agree in >90 % of recorded runs ⇒ the extra digests earn nothing, and the one-directional schema commitment can be argued back on data instead of on memory. **`premisesMoved` is the third review's addition and it is measurable on the existing sweep before anything is built:** it is the only one of the five that reaches STEP 2, the check that separates `proved` from `vacuous`, and one measured edit (`>= 20.0 [V]` → `>= 200.0 [V]`) moves it while moving none of the other four |
| 12 | contracts inheriting clauses, **by edge family** | stays 0 ⇒ commit 13 never runs |
| 14 | parts declaring boundary ports **and** owning internal parts wired to them | stays 0 or degenerate ⇒ conditions 1 and 2 have no subject |
| **18** | `{strength, fairStates, fairTriggers, domainSize, continuable, safetyVerdictsMoved}` per run | `domainSize` stays 0, or `safetyVerdictsMoved` stays 0 while no liveness verdict is decided ⇒ **the declared assumption carries no information**, which is the second half of `docs/05` candidate 5's kill measurement and the number Checkpoint B is read against. Without this row commit 18 ships a permanent compatibility commitment with nothing to retire it on |
| 19 | state-machine `Refine` / `:>` pairs | stays 0 ⇒ commit 20 waits |
| 21 | `{machinesWithExceptionalStates, machinesWithFailureModeFlags, hazardsUnreachableFromTheContractLane}` — hazards that are a mode rather than a broken contract | stays 0 ⇒ the second vocabulary is not worth the commitment. **Measured baseline: 0 across the corpus** — `grep -rl "exceptional" --include=*.sysml` returns nothing — which is why commit 21's own verdict sentence carries the measured `0` and the `1` spelling lives on a factory fixture |
| **23** | `{failureModeFlags, exceptionalStates, ordersExplored, queriesDecided, queriesUndecided, cutSetsByOrder, singlePointOfFailure, refusedByGate}` per behavioural group | `machinesWithFailureModeFlags` stays 0 on recorded runs ⇒ the `#failureMode` tag is a permanent commitment nobody applied, and the lane retires with it. `queriesUndecided` non-zero on most runs ⇒ `singlePointOfFailure` is `null` on most of them and the report answers nothing an engineer can act on. **`refusedByGate` is the sharper one and §5(d) is its baseline: the injection substrate IS the store, so clause (d) is the clause most likely to fire here — and the corpus now has exactly one machine that fires it, `GuardProbe::Ctrl`, which is a fixture and not a design.** The draft's census table had no row for this commit and §3.8 had no Census subsection, so the last feature in the plan was the only one with nothing to retire it on |

Commits 6, 8 and 9 are the three that close a defect an engineer can hit today. **Everything from commit 13 onward is specified in full and gated on a number the tool now records**, which is the difference between a plan and a wish list.

---

## 6. Traps and non-goals

### 6.1 Non-goals, and the trigger for each

| Not built | Trigger that would change it |
|---|---|
| BMC / k-induction | commit 3's census stops reading `boundHit: 'none'` on a model that matters. Note the completeness threshold that would upgrade "no violation within k" to `proved` is as hard as the model-checking problem, which is why `certificate-import`'s rule — a bounded run never imports as proved — is right and stays |
| `guaranteed` on a triggered machine | commit 17's carrier, and only with the assumption printed on the same line |
| **any increasing claim on a triggered machine** — the gate's clause (c) | a carrier for *what the environment can supply*, which this engine does not have. `FairnessAssumption` is not it: fairness constrains which infinite runs count, not which triggers arrive. Until then the honest alphabet to be exact over is the empty one, and §5(d) records that every machine in this tree has one. **The trigger, when it comes, releases clause (c) for A6, A7, A8, A10, A11, A12, A13 and 5b's `fairTriggers` in one edit inside `Exactness`** — which is the property the collapse was built for |
| **any increasing claim over an unbound model parameter** — the gate's clause (d) | a way to say *this attribute is a free parameter and here is its domain*, so the walk can offer the guarded edge under an assumption instead of reading `undefined` as `false`. That is a modelling-language question, not an engine one, and it is where the `verification/unbound-guard-attribute` finding §5's commit-6 row contemplates would go |
| a `StateMap` carrier for candidate 6 | never — both carriers already exist and were measured |
| `verification/inherited-clause-not-read` | never — it fires on 0 of every model in the tree while costing a codes bump |
| an author-written dependency annotation for candidate 7 | never — it is the side of the axis `docs/05` rules out; the feature waits for models with internal structure behind boundary ports |
| `check-behaviour --from-keywords` | never — commit 21 gives the input a lane instead of building a flag to satisfy a message |
| BDDs and symbolic representation | rejected on **measured scale** — *an inference, not a book claim* (`docs/05`:295, and §5 item 3 is a page its author did not open). Revisit when commit 3's census stops reading `boundHit: 'none'` on a model that matters — the same observation the BMC row above names |
| partial-order reduction, CEGAR, nested DFS, symmetry reduction, timed / probabilistic / μ-calculus | `docs/05` §3 and §4, unchanged. (Nested DFS specifically: `docs/05` §3's reason holds — it is the on-the-fly answer to a product you cannot store, and this walk stores four configurations) |
| a per-index atom valuation in `ExploreResult` | it is what would let `recovery` and the modality accept `trigger`, `fires` and `expression` atoms (§3.2b). Revisit when commit 9's census shows users writing those kinds and being refused — the refusal is recorded, so the demand is a number |
| a grammar for `accept after(n)` | not in this plan, and not a hidden prerequisite either. `AcceptClause` takes a `QualifiedName` (`sysml.langium`:214-215) and the spelling is a parse error (correction 22), so a timed transition is API-only. Writing the grammar is a change to the notation with its own round-trip, serializer and conformance cost, and this plan does not spend it. **The time clause ships anyway** — the SDK builds these machines today, in both spellings — and it is exactly the apparatus a future grammar commit would otherwise have to add after the fact, on a corpus that had already been walked without it |
| reactive realizability, and the word | `docs/04` §6 non-goal 5, unchanged |

### 6.2 The traps, every one measured

1. **Recording the retained edge one line late** yields the BFS spanning tree — acyclic on every machine in the repository — and Tarjan over it names `{failsafe}` a bottom component on the flagship example. The two pruned edges on `FlightModes` are precisely the cycle-closing ones.
2. **Recording an edge whose target a bound dropped** creates a successor-free node, which Tarjan reads as a trap.
3. **Configuration hashes carry fresh UUIDs** — 114 characters on `FlightModes`. Intern to integers; never let one reach a report, a digest or a JSON payload.
4. **A bottom component containing the opening configuration is the whole reachable graph.** Without exemption 3, the trap row fires on all three shipped machines and calls the flagship's normal operating region inescapable.
5. **Endings are not traps**, and **a trivial bottom component with no self-loop is already `verification/deadlock`**. Both exemptions reuse `explore.ts`:639-640.
6. **The recovery target is a STATE; the nodes are CONFIGURATIONS.** `configStates[i].includes(p)`, never `leafOf() === p`, and never `walk.reachable`.
7. **`AG EF p` with `p` unreachable is refuted, not vacuous.**
8. **`search` returns at the first violation**, so `found.configs` was never a total and no absence claim may quote it.
9. **Exit 1 is one careless line away:** `absence` produces `fail` + `verification/refuted` + error, and `cover` reuses the identical monitor breach.
10. **A claim word in `PropertyClaim` and not in `counts`** leaves every bucket at zero with rows present, which falls through to exit **0**.
11. **A flag may not re-grade a code** — one severity per catalogue entry, asserted; `--cover-required` emits a second code.
12. **`guaranteed` on a triggered machine** calls `absence(locked)` guaranteed on the `latch` machine, because the walk's environment always offers `latch`.
13. **A modality read off the declaration-order tie-break** prints a claim about the simulator as a claim about the model.
14. **A refutation of `F p` from a truncated walk** is an absence claim wearing a witness's clothes: a bound-dropped configuration is indistinguishable from a terminal one.
15. **A universal liveness pass on a triggered machine** is a claim about an environment; the refutation direction is sound across environments and the pass direction is not.
16. **STEP 0 is global.** A per-proof staleness scope cannot see it, and `docs/05`'s MUST-NEVER list does not name it.
17. **z3's core names an arbitrary sufficient reason** — measured, the weakest of three, with the model's own value absent.
18. **Premises drop out of the core while remaining load-bearing** for the check that separates `proved` from `vacuous`.
19. **A `--why` display filtered to the `axiom:` prefix** drops the `side:` divisor condition, which shares its qualified name with an axiom.
20. **`effectiveFeatures` masks by bare `declaredName`, and the shipped clause idiom is anonymous** — so a specialize check can only ever pass on the syntax every model uses.
21. **`generalizationsOf` follows FeatureTyping too**, so an unqualified reading double-files every applied `requirement r : Def;`.
22. **`obligationDigest` collides on an inherited clause**, and fixing it moves every digest in the tree.
23. **`connectivity`'s port inventory counts constraint parameters as ports** (4 of 6 on `views-tour`), and `unconnectedPortUsages` reports 3 false danglers on `vehicle.sysml` through a definition-vs-instance enumeration defect.
24. **Direction must be read through `portFacets`**, never `attrs.direction` — net conjugation flips it, silently.
25. **`inout` satisfies both signature conditions through itself** unless self-dependency is excluded.
26. **There is no finite-run semantics in the engine.** 5a introduces one; that is a semantic commitment, not an algorithm.
27. **A fairness assumption can move a safety verdict**, and the check that says so is the same SCC pass.
28. **`propertiesOf`'s cell reader is last-wins**; a set-valued carrier copying it drops every constraint but the last.
29. **A fairness set naming transitions is unusable on the flagship model** — both transitions in its nondeterminism row are anonymous and render as the same qualified name.
30. **The two readings of a specialized machine differ** — 3 walked transitions vs 5 from `effectiveFeatures` — because masking is vacuous for anonymous features.
31. **Permanence is not self-enforcing:** guards read the store `assign` effects write.
32. **`reach`'s nominal absence rows contradict the injected walk**; every behavioural cut-set report says which world each claim belongs to.
33. **A keyword resolves only when its package is imported.** Without `import SysproseVerification::*;`, both `#failureMode` and the shipped `#exceptional` report `verification/keyword-names-nothing`.
34. **The guide's hand-pasted `reach` transcript is not machine-checked** — no `docs-counts` claim pins a `reach` number — so it rots silently; commit 8 adds it to the claims list. Find it by grepping `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` for `configuration(s) explored`.
35. **The campaign's exact `reach` diagnostics array is commit 2's trip-wire**, and `semantics.mc.reach.test.ts`'s `it('names the seven codes this engine can raise')` — SEVEN at `dc6662c`, and its title carries the number — is commit 8's, going to eight. Moving either is a deliberate re-record, never a fixup; and the code-set guard is found by its `it(...)` title, not by a line, because an earlier revision pointed one line block too high and would have had an implementer edit the profile-provenance loop instead.
36. **The claims guard is narrower than it looks** (§2.6): today it would not catch *"the machine is safe to enter failsafe"* or `guaranteed`. Commit 4 widens it before anything can print them.
37. **The claims guard cannot reserve a NEGATIVE phrase at all.** `negated()` defuses any match whose span contains `no`/`not`/`never`; executed, `no cut set`, `not covered` and `no simulation relation` each score **0 hits**. Commit 4 changes `negated()` itself, and measures the tree-wide hit count in the same commit.
38. **The walk's edge relation over-approximates time, and no bound sees it.** `machineAlphabet` includes `after(n)` labels, the walk offers them as named events and `matches` tests the dwell only on the `timeout` branch. Sound for A1/A2, which are monotone-decreasing; **unsound for every increasing claim and for every positive witness** — that is the polarity rule of §2.3 and clause (b) of the gate.
39. **The polarity of an absence claim is not a property of the lane, it is a property of the claim.** Reusing A1/A2's condition set for A6/A7 across the flip is the single defect that turns this lane into a liar, and no diagnostic fires when it does — not even `verification/nondeterministic-choice`, because two dwell transitions match different trigger strings.
40. **`openFrontier` is `!exhaustive`, not `boundHit !== 'none'`.** The unsupported-construct early return sets `exhaustive: false` with `boundHit: 'none'`, reachable from ordinary `.sysml` through an unresolved transition endpoint, and the naive spelling publishes `acyclic: true` over an empty relation.
41. **`base` sets `configs: found.configs` for every verdict branch** (`patterns.ts`:1189), so a row whose prose quotes the configuration-graph count publishes two unlabelled numbers unless the sentence names a field of its own.
42. **The severity of an emitted behaviour diagnostic comes from the CLAIM, not the code** (`patterns.ts`:1401), and the guard that is supposed to stop the drift compares two constants to the catalogue and never looks at an emitted diagnostic.
43. **`verification/vacuous` and `verification/vacuous-property` never coexist** — the loop substitutes or omits. Copying the "precedent" literally drops `verification/not-covered` under `--cover-required`.
44. **A `verification/*` code catalogued `error` or `warning` and not added to `VERIFICATION_ERROR_CODES` / `VERIFICATION_WARNING_CODES` turns `diagnostic-codes.test.ts` red on landing** — and a warning raised from `patterns.ts` has no set to join until one is exported.
45. **`SYSPROSE_VERIFICATION_LIBRARY` has THREE sites, not two:** the export, `semantics.keywords.test.ts`'s round-trip, and the verbatim paste in `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` that `user-guide.test.ts` pins with the message *"paste the export, do not retype it"* — grep the guide for `SYSPROSE_VERIFICATION_LIBRARY` to find it.
46. **A repeated same-named cell inside a metadata body is a `validation/duplicate-name` ERROR** (`rules.ts`:181), so a set-valued carrier spelled that way cannot appear in any model that passes `npm run check`. Measured on the draft's `FairnessAssumption`: two errors, rc=1.
47. **A witness at depth *d* needs `--max-configs ≥ d`:** the violation check runs on a successor that is only enqueued past the bound at `patterns.ts`:969, and `seen` starts holding the root. A `--max-configs 1` test written for a 2-step witness fails on landing.
48. **`relevantAxioms` is typed over unexported SMT-encoded types**, so "export it" is not a one-line change — and making it callable from a `ValidationRule` pulls the SMT encoder into the browser bundle through `src/ui/store.ts`.
49. **`check-behaviour` writes no evidence record**, so no flag it takes can be recorded in one without a new record kind through an `additionalProperties: false` schema.
50. **`CommandSpec.question` is rendered twice into the byte-pinned reference and hand-copied into README.md**:137, so a command whose question changes moves four places, not one.
51. **A decided negative with no bucket in its exit-code contract is published under a sentence that means something else** — `refinementExitCode` returns 2 for `refined + notRefined === 0`, and a decided "no simulation relation" lands there.
52. **A semantic profile that admits one run class and a verdict that prints another contradict each other in one output.** 5a's finite maximal run and 5b's lasso are two classes; the profile names both or the 1 row's *"a run this semantics admits"* is false of the lasso.
53. **`accept after(n)` DOES NOT PARSE** (correction 22). `AcceptClause` is `'accept' payload=QualifiedName`; measured, a file carrying it fails with `no-viable-alt` at the `(` and 8 errors, rc=1, and the escaped `accept 'after(5)'` is a *different trigger string* that `afterDuration` does not match. Every dwell fixture is built through the API; a `.sysml` one fails the corpus gate on landing and then asserts a failed time clause over a walk that never ran.
54. **A single publishability boolean cannot express a per-claim `walkRequires`.** One `ok` conjoining the increasing side's conditions turns A1 and A2 — monotone-decreasing, shipped, and sound under the over-approximation — into suppressions on every timed machine, empties a true `verification/unreachable-state` finding, and prints *"a trigger the machine names was never offered"* when every trigger was offered. Two conjunctions, selected by the register's polarity column.
55. **A refutation is a positive claim over the retained relation.** The existence of a `p`-free finite maximal run (5a's `fail`) and of a fair lasso (5b's) are both monotone-**increasing**, and both spend exit 1. The transfer argument that licenses ignoring the environment — any prefix of a `p`-free run is `p`-free — is about a **withheld trigger**; under a preempted dwell the interpreter does not stall, it takes a different edge, and that edge may satisfy the property.
56. **`bounds.alphabet` contains every `after(n)` label by design**, so `alphabet.length > 0` is not *"this machine names triggers"*. A machine whose only labelled transitions are dwells would be told it names triggers and sent to `FairnessAssumption` for a hazard that is not a fairness question — and, in 5b, a dwell counted as a `fairTrigger` would inflate the `domainSize` the carrier is retired on. Split by `alphabet ∖ timedLabels`.
57. **`SEMANTIC_PROFILE` is ONE constant rendered into TWO reports** (`patterns.ts`:1416 and `explore.ts`:916). A field added for liveness changes `reach`'s text and JSON and breaks three exact six-element arrays plus the guide's *"six things"* prose.
58. **Moving a refusal from before the walk to after it flips an already-passing test on an ACYCLIC TRIGGERED machine** — `buildChain()`, `semantics.mc.patterns.test.ts`:410-431. That is the one shape a promise of *"byte-identical on every cyclic machine in the corpus"* does not cover, and `toContain('fairness')` is case-sensitive against a sentence that carries `FairnessAssumption`.
59. **`seedStore` fills the store from declared LITERAL values only**, so a guard over an attribute with no value evaluates `undefined` and its edge is never offered. Until `c929278` that happened on a walk where every publishability condition held; the fifth conjunct now withholds the decreasing lists and raises `verification/guard-undetermined`, and the increasing side is clause (d) of the gate. **Two things follow that are easy to get wrong.** The shipped predicate is *the guard was consulted and decided nothing*, which is WIDER than *no declared value* — `not mode` over a fully valued `mode` is undetermined — so a clause written to the narrow reading goes green on a missing edge. And the parameter that decided the answer is still the one the bounds line does not name; the store clause this plan appends to `boundsSentence` is a **disclosure** of the common case, not a restatement of the predicate.
60. **A diagnostic's published `when` enumerates its causes exhaustively.** `verification/malformed-property`:830 spells six pattern names and closes the list; adding `cover` and `recovery` to the catalogue, or a new refusal cause, publishes a false explanation to the very user who just hit the code — plus a hint telling them to rewrite it as a pattern that is no longer the alternative.
61. **One edit assigned to two commits, where one of them is HELD behind the other's census, ships a false `--help` indefinitely.** And a *removal* where a *replacement* is owed leaves the most common cause of an exit unpublished.
62. **Two sections stating opposite gates for one restriction** — §3.R accepting `expression` while §3.2b, §6.1 and decision 12 refuse it — is not a documentation defect. Whichever is implemented, the other section's verdict table is a lie about the shipped tool.
63. **Two verdict rows whose conditions do not partition** leave the implementer to pick the sentence by taste, with no test that can go red either way. §3.1's two `covered` rows and §3.R's `potential`-vs-`alphabet` overlap were both this shape; the fix is a disjunction in one case and a stated dispatch order in the other.
64. **The walk's edge relation over-approximates the ENVIRONMENT, and no bound and no time condition sees it.** Every trigger in the alphabet is offered at every configuration. Measured on a clean-loading `.sysml` file — `transition locked accept unlatch then nominal;` — the walk is exhaustive with an empty `timedLabels`, so every condition the first two reviews produced holds, and the tool publishes *"no component of this walk is inescapable"* and `recoverable` at exit 0 about a mode nothing leaves unless an operator acts.
65. **The walk's edge relation UNDER-approximates through the store**, and that direction manufactures a refutation rather than a pass: on the trapguard file the tool prints a `trap` row and `not recoverable … verification/refuted` at **exit 1** on a design whose model states the escape. A gate written only against over-approximation misses it by construction.
66. **Enumerating one publishability condition per mechanism is the defect, not the fix — and the fifth mechanism arrived on schedule to prove it.** Five mechanisms in five review rounds, each invisible to the condition set built for the last one, and nothing bounds the number. The fifth was an edge METACLASS missing from the step relation, and it falsified both polarities at once. The answer is not a fifth clause: `edgeCensus` counts the element set **without asking the walk** and refuses the machine on anything it cannot account for, so a sixth mechanism of that kind costs nothing at all, and a sixth of the *offering* kind costs one clause inside `Exactness` and no register row, no verdict table and no commit.
67. **A bound condition on a WITNESS row is unsatisfiable against the plan's own tests.** `covered` stands on a partial walk by design (§2.1, §3.1) and measured `--max-configs 3` on a 3-step witness prints `partial under {…}` and still publishes; an **existential** witness row that reads `seenWhole` or a product-search condition makes the reflection test and the bound-frontier test mutually exclusive, and the cheap repair hollows out the reflection test. The converse trap is trap 79: a **maximality** witness row that does *not* read the bound.
68. **One atom kind read through another's observation function flips a verdict and an exit code.** `atomHolds` reads `state` as `stack.includes` and `node` as `leafOf ===`, and `nodesOf` admits composites; measured, `absence p=node degraded` PASSes and `absence p=state degraded` FAILs on one walk. A hand-written predicate for both makes `recovery` and `absence` disagree about one atom inside one command.
69. **`F p` under fairness is refuted by TWO run classes, and a cyclic graph still admits the finite one.** `idle ⇄ busy`, `busy → done`, `idle → parked` with `parked` a dead end: every fair lasso reaches `done`, so *"no fair cycle avoids `state done`"* is true and prints at exit 0, while `idle → parked` reaches it on no continuation. The second class is one reachability query over the same relation.
70. **A digest set that closes STEP 0 and leaves STEP 2 open publishes a green absence over a vacuous proof.** Premises are in no core, in no `relevantAxioms` result and in no axiom-set digest; measured, `>= 20.0 [V]` → `>= 200.0 [V]` turns `proved` into `vacuous` while the three-part staleness scope still reads `current within this proof's scope`.
71. **A lane declared independent whose commits depend on another lane is a re-order licence that breaks the build.** Measured on §4's own dependency lines: commits 9, 16 and 23 depend on lane B and commit 19 on lane E. The same licence makes every absolute catalogue count wrong under some legal ordering, which is why every guard line states a delta.
72. **A held lane whose gate is another held lane's census is dead behind a gate the plan predicts will not open** — and if only its own census is written down, it reads as independently droppable while its guard moves are unreachable. Lane H is the measured case: `--via specialize` exists only if commit 13 ships, and commit 13 is gated on a census commit 12 expects to read 0.
73. **A deleted export takes barrels and campaign cases with it.** `behaviourLaneRefusal` is re-exported from two barrels and imported by three files including a campaign test; deleting it while they still name it fails `npm run typecheck` before `npm test` runs. A standing rule written only for the addition direction does not catch it.
74. **A verdict string quoted with a number no model in the tree produces lands a red test on the input the commit names.** `grep -rl "exceptional" --include=*.sysml` returns nothing, so `1 \`#exceptional\` state` is unwritable from the corpus; the number belongs on a factory fixture and the corpus sentence carries the measured `0`.
75. **A new discharge shape under an unedited exit-code row is the same laundering the plan opened with.** 5b's fair pass matches none of the 0 row's three shapes after commit 16 — it is run-wise, it is not a cover, and it runs only on cyclic graphs — and a byte-identity assertion between neighbouring commits converts the omission into a pinned test.
76. **A gate clause scoped to the producer that happened to be measured is a clause with a hole the shape of the other producer.** `afterDuration` has two — a numeric `attrs.after` and an `after(n)` trigger string — and only the second reaches the alphabet, because a transition carrying only the number is a **completion** transition to `isCompletion` and `triggerLabelOf` is `''`. Measured: three such transitions give `alphabet []`, `timedLabels {}`, `walkIsExact true` on a machine every edge of which is a dwell the walk erased. Clause (b) is over the **transitions**; the `timedLabels ⊆ alphabet` containment is deleted, and the per-step disjunction tests `afterDuration(step.transition)`, never the recorded label, which for such a step is the empty string.
77. **A shipped absence claim that nobody registered stays ungated until somebody notices — and the fifth review's finding was that somebody had.** `verification/deadlock` was emitted with no guard while the neighbouring lists were gated; it is the one claim an undecided guard *inverts* rather than shrinks, because a missing edge invents it; and §3.2a's exemption 1 defers a trivial bottom SCC to it as *"the same defect"*. `c929278` closed it — `deadlocks: guardsDecided ? walk.deadlocks : []`, gated on the guards alone and not on `publishable`, with the reason in a comment above the line — so what register row A0 buys now is (i) the claim being **in** the register at all, with `producedBy` pointing at that gate, and (ii) one strengthening: leaf-wise as well as walk-wise, so an undecided guard in one region does not silence a decided sink in another. **The trap survives in a sharper form:** a measurement taken before a fix and quoted after it sends an implementer to write a fixture that cannot fail, which is what an earlier revision of A0 did.
78. **A `requires` column asked to hold both a walk predicate and every unrelated conjunct cannot be asserted to hold one of two values, and the test written for it is red against the plan's own data.** Nine of fifteen absence rows and six of six witness rows carried extra conjuncts, and `relationIsTheMachines` — what every existential witness reads — was not an admissible value at all, so no witness row could ever have been legal. Split into `walkRequires` (four values, asserted) and `alsoRequires` (free-form, asserted to name no bound).
79. **A maximality claim wearing a witness's clothes needs the bound the existential witnesses must not have.** *"This cycle has no exit"* and *"this run has no successor"* are falsified by a **missing** edge, which a bound and an undecided guard both produce, while *"this run exists"* is falsified only by an invented one. One rule for all six witness rows is wrong in one direction or the other; the `kind` column is what makes it two rules and one test.
80. **A census field computed from the retained relation is a fact about the WALK, not about the machine, and publishing it as the second is how a release gate gets flipped by an artefact.** `acyclic` reads `true` on a two-state machine whose stated cycle is closed by an edge behind an unbound guard. Gate it on `walkIsExact`, not on `openFrontier`.
81. **A deleted sentence is asserted in two kinds of place and a symbol grep finds only one.** `behaviourLaneRefusal` is imported by one campaign file and its *printed string* is asserted by another (`cli.sysprose.test.ts`'s `fault-tree refuses a state machine…` block, two `toContain`s on the exact input the commit names) and pasted verbatim into `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`. `npm run typecheck` passes first, so the barrel tripwire does not fire; only a grep for the **sentence** finds all three.
82. **Deleting a test block for the assertion that no longer applies deletes the assertions beside it that now matter more.** The refusal block's last two lines test `isBehaviouralElement`, which this plan does not delete and which becomes the predicate routing a machine into the new answer; dropping them leaves that routing untested, with `noUnusedLocals: false` and an unbounded lint letting the orphaned import through as well.
83. **A count stated in prose in the conformance record is protected by nothing.** `CONFORMANCE.md` says *"six-field semantic profile"* twice; the three exact arrays and the guide prose that a seventh field breaks are all guarded, and those two sentences are not — so the one document readers treat as measured is the one place the arity goes silently false.
84. **A census that reads all-clear on the shipped corpus is not evidence that a gate is right; it is evidence the corpus cannot falsify it.** Measured (§5(d)) at `dc6662c`: 0 dwell transitions and 0 named triggers across all eleven machines, so clauses (b) and (c) are vacuously satisfied everywhere and their refusal branches are exercised only by fixtures this plan invents — those fixtures are the coverage, and deleting one deletes the clause. **Clause (d) is the counter-example and the lesson runs both ways:** it was fixture-only two commits ago and is not any more, because somebody landed a fixture model with the fix. A corpus that cannot falsify a clause today can falsify it tomorrow, and the census is what notices.
85. **A plan pinned line-by-line to a tree under active change cannot converge, and four revisions proved it.** Roughly sixty percent of the fifth review's findings were line numbers stale against commits that landed while the plan was being written — five of them in one day. Two of the stale citations landed inside a **different construct** rather than merely nearby, which is the difference between a hint that misses and an instruction that is wrong: one pointed at a field declaration where a conjunction used to be, the other at the body of a different function. §0.0 is the rule this trap produced: instructions name a symbol and an anchor, line numbers are dated hints, and a re-record is found by the sentence it moves.
86. **A plan that proposes to BUILD what the tree already has is worse than one that is merely out of date.** At `dc6662c`, `ExploreResult.undeterminedGuards`, the fifth publishability conjunct, the gated `deadlocks` array, the normalised port inventory with `unreconciledPorts`, `Succession` in the step relation and `edgeCensus` all **exist**. A commit list that adds them again lands a duplicate field under a second name (the dangerous case: the re-derived one was *narrower* than the shipped one), a fixture that cannot fail, and a dependency on unassigned work. The rule §0.1 states: where this plan describes a mechanism, it describes it because a claim reasons about it — only a *Files:* line says what is built, and the implementer verifies each one against the tree before writing it.
87. **A measurement quoted from before a fix sends an implementer to write a test that cannot fail.** The trapguard family's `1 dead` / `exhaustive` transcripts, the ungated `deadlocks ['P::Modes::degraded']`, and `connectivity`'s three false danglers were all re-executed at `dc6662c` and none of them reproduces. Each was load-bearing in a *Tests:* line. The discipline is to **date every transcript to the commit it was taken on**, and to re-take it in the commit that asserts it.
88. **A closed enumeration narrower than its own data is a red test written against the plan.** It happened twice: the `requires` column asked to hold one of two values while nine of fifteen absence rows and six of six witness rows carried more, and then the `polarity` column beside it asked for one of two while six rows were `mixed` or not a walk at all. The second was the same defect one column over, introduced in the paragraph that repaired the first. A column is typed from its data, and a rule that cannot be satisfied is repaired by weakening the rule — which is how a mechanism §5 counts becomes a mechanism that covers nothing.

---

## 7. Open decisions, with a recommendation for each

**Decision 15 — `walkRequires` holds a disjunction on four of the six witness rows, and the column is specified as holding one of four literal values.** W1, W2, W5 and W6 read *"`relationIsTheMachines`, or the per-step disjunction in its place"*. That is a real alternative and not sloppy prose — a witness may stand either on the whole relation being the machine's, or on every step of the witness itself being one the machine has — but it cannot be a single literal in a column the reflection test reads. Three shapes are available: widen the union with a fifth member; split the column into `walkRequires` plus a `perStepAlternative: boolean`; or drop the alternative and make every witness read the whole-relation gate, which is sound but refuses witnesses the tool could honestly publish. **Recommendation: the second.** It keeps the reflection test's four literals, states the alternative where a reader sees it, and the per-step check is a different computation rather than a weaker version of the same one. Settle it in commit 1, where a type checker is holding the question.



**1. Does `--cover-required` belong at all, or should a cover over an `#exceptional` state be required by default?**
*Recommendation: ship the flag, default off, and revisit at Checkpoint A with the census.* A cover over a hazard state is the one shape where "the design cannot reach it" is the *desired* answer, so a default-on requirement would invert the intent for exactly the population most likely to write one. The census records the atom shape of every cover; if the field shows covers are overwhelmingly written over non-hazard states, default-on becomes arguable.

**2. Should `recovery` spend the 1, given that `cover` does not?**
*Recommendation: yes, as specified.* The distinction is who made the claim. `cover` asks whether the design *admits* a behaviour and its negative answer is a missing behaviour; `recovery` is a universal assertion the engineer wrote, naming the state, and its exhaustive refutation is a violated requirement with a witness. If the user prefers one rule for both, the honest version is to move `recovery` to `not-covered`/exit 2 as well — **not** to move `cover` to exit 1.

**3. Does the `--why` core belong in the evidence record before commit 11 is gated in?**
*Recommendation: no, and this plan is written that way.* Displaying it costs nothing and is reversible; recording it is a one-directional schema commitment compiled by ajv in two places, and it buys nothing until staleness reads it. If the user wants the record populated early anyway — for external tooling — that is a deliberate choice to spend the commitment ahead of its justification, and it should be taken explicitly rather than as a side effect of commit 10.

**4. Is the two-tier staleness verdict acceptable, or must part (iii) always be enforced?**
*Recommendation: enforce all three, and fall back to the two-tier sentence only if the per-model measurement shows (iii) firing on essentially every edit.* On `uav-isr` all 12 axioms are feature-value bindings, so (iii) will fire on any numeric edit and the survival rate collapses toward today's. The two-tier sentence is strictly weaker than `current` and exactly what the evidence supports; a boolean that says `current` when STEP 0 has moved is not.

**5. Should 5a ship a `pass` on a triggered acyclic machine, with the environment sentence beside it, instead of refusing?**
*Recommendation: refuse, as specified — and after the third review this is no longer a per-lane decision at all, which is the point.* The rider already refused `guaranteed` on a triggered machine for the identical reason, and the draft then made the opposite call in two other lanes without noticing: §3.2a published *"no component of this walk is inescapable"* and §3.2b published `recoverable` at exit 0 on the measured latch machine, with a disclosure sentence beside them. Making the claim in one lane while refusing it in another is the plan contradicting itself, and the draft did it four ways. The collapse removes the possibility: clause (c) is **one** clause read by **every** increasing claim, so the trade can only be taken globally. **If the user would rather have the verdicts with a caveat, the change is now a single edit** — drop clause (c) from `relationIsTheMachines` — and it lands in every lane at once with one sentence, which is exactly the property §7 decision 5 was asking for and could not previously enforce. The cost of taking it is stated plainly: `recoverable`, `no trap`, `guaranteed` and `holds on every maximal run` would then all be claims about a maximally cooperative environment, on machines where the interesting failure is precisely that the environment stops cooperating.

**6. Does the `FairnessAssumption` carrier survive Checkpoint B?**
*Recommendation: build the carrier only after 5a has a real acyclic customer, and be prepared to retire it.* Measured, `alphabet` is `[]` on all three shipped machines, so the only writable assumption is over choice points — which is the case `docs/05` itself flags as possibly carrying no information. A carrier is a permanent compatibility commitment; this is the one place in the plan where the honest answer may be to spend nothing.

**7. Which reading of a specialized machine does candidate 6 compare — the walked one or `effectiveFeatures`?**
*Recommendation: the walked one, and print which.* `reach` walks containment only, so the walked machine is the one every other command in the tool reports on. `effectiveFeatures` returns five transitions where the author wrote three, because masking is vacuous for anonymous features, and comparing a machine nobody wrote would make the verdict unreproducible from any other command's output.

**8. Do lanes F and H get built at all, given their censuses read zero?**
*Recommendation: ship commits 14 and 19 (the censuses and the reader), and hold 15 and 20 indefinitely.* Both censuses are cheap, both surface real defects in shipped inventories (commit 14 alone surfaces three), and both make the build decision a number rather than an argument. Neither gated commit should be written on speculation.

**9. Should `docs/05` §5's unverified items be re-derived before candidate 6 is built, or is declaring the rule as this tool's own enough?**
*Recommendation: declaring it is enough, and re-deriving is not.* The labelling rule decides the verdict on the very example that motivates the feature, so it must be written down and owned regardless of what the book says. Citing a source for it would add a warrant this plan cannot check; declaring it as this tool's choice is both honest and sufficient.

**10. Does the widened claims guard belong in commit 4, ahead of the features, or in the closing pass?**
*Recommendation: commit 4, as specified.* A guard added after the sentences it governs has to be reconciled against strings already shipped, and the measurement that each new form has zero pre-existing hits is only clean before the features land. The closing pass re-asserts; it does not introduce. **And commit 4 now carries a change to the guard's own machinery** (`negated()`, §2.6), which is a second reason it cannot slide: a guard-machinery change measured against a tree that already contains this plan's sentences cannot distinguish a pre-existing hit from one this plan introduced.

**11. Should `check-behaviour` gain an evidence record, so that a flag-raised exit code is durable?**
*Recommendation: no, and §3.1 is written that way.* The draft promised the `--cover-required` flag would be "recorded in the evidence record's `flags`"; measured, this command writes no record and has no `--record` flag, and adding one is a new record kind through `evidence-record.schema.json`'s `additionalProperties: false` — the one-directional commitment §3.3a spends only in commit 11, priced there and nowhere else. The flag is on the summary line and in the `--json` payload, which is where every other behaviour-lane provenance lives. If a durable record is genuinely wanted — for CI archaeology, say — it is a deliberate schema spend with commit 11's cost paragraph as the template, taken explicitly.

**12. Should `ExploreResult` retain a per-index atom valuation, so `recovery` and the modality accept all five atom kinds?**
*Recommendation: no, not in this plan.* `configStates` holds state ids, which is what `state` and `node` atoms need; `trigger` and `fires` hold on a **step** and `expression` needs the store, so all three would require retaining a valuation per configuration — materially more than §3.P's twelve lines and its zero-new-compute claim. The three kinds are **refused with a reason** rather than silently mishandled, **in BOTH sections and in the same wording** (§3.2b's arm and §3.R's row 4): the draft had §3.R accepting `expression` and §3.2b refusing it, two opposite gates for one restriction, and §3.R is the one that moved — because this decision and §6.1 both already said the store is what `expression` needs, so §3.R was the outlier. The refusal is recorded in commit 9's census, and §6.1 carries the revisit trigger: build it when the census shows users writing those kinds and being turned away. Two alternatives must never be taken: the one the draft left open in §3.2b — letting them through to an empty target set and a `verification/refuted` at exit 1 — and its mirror in §3.R, where an `expression` atom marks **no** configuration violating, makes *G* the whole graph, and prints `potential — some run avoids it: the cycle …` on every cyclic machine whatever the model says.

**And the third review found the same class of defect inside the two kinds this plan DOES accept.** `state` and `node` are not one predicate: `atomHolds` reads the first as `stack.includes` and the second as `leafOf ===`, and `nodesOf` admits composites, so measured on one walk `absence p=node degraded` **passes** and `absence p=state degraded` **fails**. The draft mandated `configStates[i].includes(p)` for both, which flips `recovery p = node degraded` from this section's second `fail` (exit 1) to `recoverable` (exit 0) and makes `recovery` and `absence` disagree about one atom inside one command. *Recommendation: retain `configLeaves` — one integer array beside `configStates` — and read `p` through `atomHolds`'s own selection.* It is a strictly smaller change than the per-index valuation this decision rejects, it costs no new compute, and it is the only option that keeps the two commands agreeing. The fallback, if even that array is judged not worth it, is to **refuse `node`** with the reason its own kind earns, in both sections and in the identical wording — what is not available is keeping `node` and reading it as `state`.

**13. Commit 4 scopes the new reserved forms instead of reserving the bare phrases. Is the narrower guard enough?**
*Recommendation: yes, and the measurement is why — but it is a judgement worth overruling explicitly if you disagree.* The draft promised each new form at "0 pre-existing hits"; measured with `negativeIsTheClaim` in place, the bare phrases read `no cut set` **23**, `not covered` **3**, `recoverable` **2**, `no simulation relation` **0**, and two of the 23 are `fault-tree.ts`:859/:861 — the command's own honest bounded verdict. Reserving the bare phrase lands 28 red on arrival; the only allowance that clears those two defuses exactly the sentence the form exists to reserve. So commit 4 reserves the **sentence** — five subject- or shape-scoped forms, each measured at 0 and each asserted to fire on the planted sentence §2.6 quotes. The cost is stated in §5(c): the guard covers the prose a reader would quote and not the verdict strings, which carry no subject and are pinned byte-identically by the fixtures instead. The alternative — land the bare forms with a 28-line allowance list — is available and is *not* recommended: every allowance is a hole nobody re-measures, and the largest of them would have to be `no cut set (was found|up to order)`.

**14. The increasing side is now one gate, `walkIsExact`, whose clause (c) requires an EMPTY ALPHABET. Is that narrowing acceptable, given that it is unfalsifiable on today's corpus?**
*Recommendation: yes, and the two halves of the reason should be weighed separately, because only the first is measured.* The **first half is measured and comfortable**: §5(d) surveys every state machine in the tree and finds 9 of 11 exact, the two exceptions already suppressed today, and 0 dwell transitions / 0 named triggers across the whole tree — so the gate costs this repository nothing and every increasing claim publishes on every model that exists here. The **second half is not measured and should not be pretended otherwise**: that same census means **no model can falsify clause (b) or clause (c)**, and the only evidence those two behave correctly is **three** fixtures this plan invents — the latch machine, the trapguard machine, the preempted-dwell machine, and the numeric-`attrs.after` machine that the fourth review's reading of clause (b) made necessary. Two of the four are ordinary `.sysml` files, which is the best that can be done; the other two cannot be, because `accept after(n)` does not parse and `attrs.after` has no notation at all. **And the fourth is the sharpest evidence in this section for why the gate is a predicate and not a list:** it is not a new mechanism, it is the *first* mechanism reached through the producer nobody instrumented, and a clause scoped to the wrong producer read clean on a machine every edge of which was a dwell. **What follows for the reader:** the fixtures are load-bearing infrastructure, not colour, and a future commit that deletes one has deleted a clause; and the honest summary of what lanes B, C and G answer today is *completion-driven machines*, with the trigger-driven case — measured, the intuitive one for a trap — refused rather than qualified. If the user judges the disclosure better than the refusal, decision 5 is where that trade is taken, and it is now a one-line change rather than four.
